1: # EdgeHost
2: 
3: Run iCUE HTML widgets on a CORSAIR XENEON EDGE connected to a Mac.
4: 
5: Corsair's iCUE only drives the XENEON EDGE's widget dashboard on Windows; on macOS the Edge is a plain
6: 2560×720 monitor. EdgeHost hosts widgets written against the public
7: [iCUE Widget API](https://docs.elgato.com/icue/widgets/) (1.9.0) on that display, and adds a
8: sandboxed add-in system (Rhai scripts) for data providers.
9: 
10: **Status:** milestone M1. One widget runs in a borderless window on the Edge, or in a scaled stand-in
11: window when no Edge is attached. See [`docs/SPEC.md`](docs/SPEC.md) for the plan and milestones.
12: 
13: ## What it is not
14: 
15: - Not affiliated with or endorsed by CORSAIR or Elgato. "iCUE" and "XENEON" are their trademarks, used
16:   here only to describe compatibility.
17: - Not a replacement for iCUE's device features: no RGB, fan, pump or firmware control.
18: - No Marketplace or account integration. EdgeHost ships no Corsair code, widgets or assets.
19: 
20: ## Requirements
21: 
22: - macOS 14 Sonoma or later (per-widget WebKit data stores need it)
23: - Rust (edition 2024) and Node.js (for the shim tests)
24: - Optional: `brew install lld`. `.cargo/config.toml` links with `ld64.lld`; if linking fails after an
25:   Xcode update, run `brew upgrade lld`, or build with `RUSTFLAGS=""` to use Apple's linker.
26: 
27: macOS permissions are requested only when a feature needs them. Input Monitoring (Edge touch, M4) and
28: Automation (media fallback, v1.5) are expected.
29: 
30: ## Usage
31: 
32: ```bash
33: # Inspect a widget directory: manifest, controls, plugins, warnings
34: cargo run -p edgectl -- validate examples/widgets/hello-edge
35: 
36: # Run it at Large size; uses the Edge if attached, otherwise a half-size stand-in window
37: cargo run -p edgectl -- run-one examples/widgets/hello-edge --slot L
38: 
39: # Force the stand-in window, pick a slot and grid cell, open Safari Web Inspector
40: cargo run -p edgectl -- run-one path/to/widget --windowed --slot S --col 2 --row 1 --devtools
41: ```
42: 
43: Widgets that load iCUE's shared `../common/...` folder need it beside the widget directory, or passed
44: with `--common-dir`. Press Esc to close the borderless Edge window.
45: 
46: ## Development
47: 
48: ```bash
49: cargo check --all-targets
50: cargo test --workspace
51: cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
52: node --test crates/widget-runtime/shim/
53: ```
54: 
55: | Path | Contents |
56: |---|---|
57: | `crates/icuew-manifest` | Parse `manifest.json`, `<head>` controls and groups, `translation.json` |
58: | `crates/edge-layout` | Edge grid, S/M/L/XL slots, display selection |
59: | `crates/widget-runtime` | Injection shim, `icuew://` asset server, Edge surface and web views |
60: | `apps/edgectl` | Developer CLI |
61: | `examples/widgets/hello-edge` | Test widget that displays the injected globals |
62: | `docs/` | Spec, review, and research notes (reverse-engineering findings, API reference) |

1: //! XENEON EDGE panel geometry.
2: //!
3: //! The grid is derived from Corsair's published widget slot sizes (see `docs/SPEC.md` §5.3):
4: //!
5: //! | Orientation | Panel    | Cells | Cell    | Gutter | Margin |
6: //! |-------------|----------|-------|---------|--------|--------|
7: //! | Landscape   | 2560×720 | 3×2   | 840×344 | 8      | 12     |
8: //! | Portrait    | 720×2560 | 1×6   | 696×416 | 8      | 12     |
9: //!
10: //! Every published slot size (S/M/L/XL in both orientations) is reproduced exactly by
11: //! [`slot_rect`]. [`select_display`] chooses the display the panel is drawn on.
12: 
13: mod display;
14: 
15: use std::fmt;
16: use std::str::FromStr;
17: 
18: use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
19: 
20: pub use display::{
21:     DisplayChoice, DisplayInfo, EDGE_EDID_MODEL, EDGE_EDID_VENDOR, Fit, Selection, fit_panel,
22:     select_display,
23: };
24: 
25: /// Space between adjacent cells, in panel pixels.
26: pub const GUTTER: u32 = 8;
27: /// Space between the outermost cells and the panel edge, in panel pixels.
28: pub const MARGIN: u32 = 12;
29: 
30: /// Panel orientation.
31: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
32: #[serde(rename_all = "lowercase")]
33: pub enum Orientation {
34:     /// 2560×720, three columns by two rows.
35:     #[default]
36:     Landscape,
37:     /// 720×2560, one column by six rows.
38:     Portrait,
39: }
40: 
41: impl Orientation {
42:     /// Native panel size `(width, height)` in pixels.
43:     #[must_use]
44:     pub const fn panel_size(self) -> (u32, u32) {
45:         match self {
46:             Self::Landscape => (2560, 720),
47:             Self::Portrait => (720, 2560),
48:         }
49:     }
50: 
51:     /// Grid dimensions `(columns, rows)`.
52:     #[must_use]
53:     pub const fn grid(self) -> (u32, u32) {
54:         match self {
55:             Self::Landscape => (3, 2),
56:             Self::Portrait => (1, 6),
57:         }
58:     }
59: 
60:     /// Size of one grid cell `(width, height)` in pixels.
61:     #[must_use]
62:     pub const fn cell_size(self) -> (u32, u32) {
63:         match self {
64:             Self::Landscape => (840, 344),
65:             Self::Portrait => (696, 416),
66:         }
67:     }
68: }
69: 
70: impl FromStr for Orientation {
71:     type Err = LayoutError;
72: 
73:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
74:         match s.to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
75:             "landscape" | "horizontal" => Ok(Self::Landscape),
76:             "portrait" | "vertical" => Ok(Self::Portrait),
77:             _ => Err(LayoutError::UnknownOrientation(s.to_owned())),
78:         }
79:     }
80: }
81: 
82: impl fmt::Display for Orientation {
83:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
84:         f.write_str(match self {
85:             Self::Landscape => "landscape",
86:             Self::Portrait => "portrait",
87:         })
88:     }
89: }
90: 
91: /// iCUE widget slot size.
92: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
93: pub enum Slot {
94:     /// Small: one cell.
95:     S,
96:     /// Medium: two cells.
97:     M,
98:     /// Large: four cells.
99:     L,
100:     /// Extra large: the whole panel.
101:     XL,
102: }
103: 
104: impl Slot {
105:     /// Cells spanned `(columns, rows)` in the given orientation.
106:     #[must_use]
107:     pub const fn span(self, orientation: Orientation) -> (u32, u32) {
108:         match (orientation, self) {
109:             (Orientation::Landscape | Orientation::Portrait, Self::S) => (1, 1),
110:             (Orientation::Landscape | Orientation::Portrait, Self::M) => (1, 2),
111:             (Orientation::Landscape, Self::L) => (2, 2),
112:             (Orientation::Landscape, Self::XL) => (3, 2),
113:             (Orientation::Portrait, Self::L) => (1, 4),
114:             (Orientation::Portrait, Self::XL) => (1, 6),
115:         }
116:     }
117: }
118: 
119: impl FromStr for Slot {
120:     type Err = LayoutError;
121: 
122:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
123:         match s.to_ascii_uppercase().as_str() {
124:             "S" | "SMALL" => Ok(Self::S),
125:             "M" | "MEDIUM" => Ok(Self::M),
126:             "L" | "LARGE" => Ok(Self::L),
127:             "XL" | "EXTRA-LARGE" | "EXTRALARGE" => Ok(Self::XL),
128:             _ => Err(LayoutError::UnknownSlot(s.to_owned())),
129:         }
130:     }
131: }
132: 
133: impl fmt::Display for Slot {
134:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
135:         f.write_str(match self {
136:             Self::S => "S",
137:             Self::M => "M",
138:             Self::L => "L",
139:             Self::XL => "XL",
140:         })
141:     }
142: }
143: 
144: /// A grid cell coordinate, zero-based from the top-left.
145: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Default, Serialize, Deserialize)]
146: pub struct Cell {
147:     /// Column index.
148:     pub col: u32,
149:     /// Row index.
150:     pub row: u32,
151: }
152: 
153: /// A rectangle in panel pixels.
154: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
155: pub struct PxRect {
156:     /// Left edge.
157:     pub x: u32,
158:     /// Top edge.
159:     pub y: u32,
160:     /// Width.
161:     pub width: u32,
162:     /// Height.
163:     pub height: u32,
164: }
165: 
166: /// Errors from layout calculations and parsing.
167: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
168: pub enum LayoutError {
169:     /// The slot placed at `origin` would extend past the grid.
170:     #[error("slot {slot} at column {col}, row {row} does not fit the {orientation} grid", col = origin.col, row = origin.row)]
171:     OutOfBounds {
172:         /// Slot being placed.
173:         slot: Slot,
174:         /// Requested top-left cell.
175:         origin: Cell,
176:         /// Grid orientation.
177:         orientation: Orientation,
178:     },
179:     /// Unrecognised slot name.
180:     #[error("unknown slot size {0:?} (expected S, M, L or XL)")]
181:     UnknownSlot(String),
182:     /// Unrecognised orientation name.
183:     #[error("unknown orientation {0:?} (expected landscape or portrait)")]
184:     UnknownOrientation(String),
185:     /// A display pick matched no connected display.
186:     #[error("no display matches {query:?}; connected: {}", available.join(", "))]
187:     NoSuchDisplay {
188:         /// The index or name that was requested.
189:         query: String,
190:         /// Names of the connected displays.
191:         available: Vec<String>,
192:     },
193: }
194: 
195: impl Slot {
196:     /// Every slot, smallest first.
197:     pub const ALL: [Self; 4] = [Self::S, Self::M, Self::L, Self::XL];
198: 
199:     /// The cells this slot covers when placed at `origin`, or `None` if it does not fit.
200:     #[must_use]
201:     pub fn cells(self, orientation: Orientation, origin: Cell) -> Option<Vec<Cell>> {
202:         slot_rect(orientation, self, origin).ok()?;
203:         let (span_c, span_r) = self.span(orientation);
204:         Some(
205:             (origin.row..origin.row + span_r)
206:                 .flat_map(|row| (origin.col..origin.col + span_c).map(move |col| Cell { col, row }))
207:                 .collect(),
208:         )
209:     }
210: }
211: 
212: impl Orientation {
213:     /// Every cell of the grid in reading order.
214:     pub fn all_cells(self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Cell> {
215:         let (cols, rows) = self.grid();
216:         (0..rows).flat_map(move |row| (0..cols).map(move |col| Cell { col, row }))
217:     }
218: }
219: 
220: /// Pixel rectangle of `slot` placed with its top-left corner at `origin`.
221: ///
222: /// ```
223: /// use edge_layout::{slot_rect, Cell, Orientation, PxRect, Slot};
224: /// let r = slot_rect(Orientation::Landscape, Slot::L, Cell::default()).unwrap();
225: /// assert_eq!(r, PxRect { x: 12, y: 12, width: 1688, height: 696 });
226: /// ```
227: ///
228: /// # Errors
229: ///
230: /// [`LayoutError::OutOfBounds`] if the slot does not fit inside the grid at `origin`.
231: pub fn slot_rect(
232:     orientation: Orientation,
233:     slot: Slot,
234:     origin: Cell,
235: ) -> Result<PxRect, LayoutError> {
236:     let (cols, rows) = orientation.grid();
237:     let (span_c, span_r) = slot.span(orientation);
238:     let fits = origin
239:         .col
240:         .checked_add(span_c)
241:         .is_some_and(|end| end <= cols)
242:         && origin
243:             .row
244:             .checked_add(span_r)
245:             .is_some_and(|end| end <= rows);
246:     if !fits {
247:         return Err(LayoutError::OutOfBounds {
248:             slot,
249:             origin,
250:             orientation,
251:         });
252:     }
253:     // All operands are bounded by the grid constants above, so this arithmetic cannot overflow.
254:     let (cell_w, cell_h) = orientation.cell_size();
255:     Ok(PxRect {
256:         x: MARGIN + origin.col * (cell_w + GUTTER),
257:         y: MARGIN + origin.row * (cell_h + GUTTER),
258:         width: span_c * cell_w + (span_c - 1) * GUTTER,
259:         height: span_r * cell_h + (span_r - 1) * GUTTER,
260:     })
261: }
262: 
263: #[cfg(test)]
264: mod tests {
265:     use super::*;
266: 
267:     /// Published slot sizes (Corsair Labs icue-widget-builder, CORSAIR blog).
268:     const PUBLISHED: [(Orientation, Slot, u32, u32); 8] = [
269:         (Orientation::Landscape, Slot::S, 840, 344),
270:         (Orientation::Landscape, Slot::M, 840, 696),
271:         (Orientation::Landscape, Slot::L, 1688, 696),
272:         (Orientation::Landscape, Slot::XL, 2536, 696),
273:         (Orientation::Portrait, Slot::S, 696, 416),
274:         (Orientation::Portrait, Slot::M, 696, 840),
275:         (Orientation::Portrait, Slot::L, 696, 1688),
276:         (Orientation::Portrait, Slot::XL, 696, 2536),
277:     ];
278: 
279:     #[test]
280:     fn slot_sizes_match_published_values() {
281:         for (o, s, w, h) in PUBLISHED {
282:             let r = slot_rect(o, s, Cell::default()).unwrap();
283:             assert_eq!((r.width, r.height), (w, h), "{o} {s}");
284:         }
285:     }
286: 
287:     #[test]
288:     fn grid_fills_panel_exactly() {
289:         for o in [Orientation::Landscape, Orientation::Portrait] {
290:             let (cols, rows) = o.grid();
291:             let (cw, ch) = o.cell_size();
292:             let (pw, ph) = o.panel_size();
293:             assert_eq!(
294:                 2 * MARGIN + cols * cw + (cols - 1) * GUTTER,
295:                 pw,
296:                 "{o} width"
297:             );
298:             assert_eq!(
299:                 2 * MARGIN + rows * ch + (rows - 1) * GUTTER,
300:                 ph,
301:                 "{o} height"
302:             );
303:         }
304:     }
305: 
306:     #[test]
307:     fn offsets_and_bounds() {
308:         let r = slot_rect(Orientation::Landscape, Slot::S, Cell { col: 2, row: 1 }).unwrap();
309:         assert_eq!(
310:             r,
311:             PxRect {
312:                 x: 12 + 2 * 848,
313:                 y: 12 + 352,
314:                 width: 840,
315:                 height: 344
316:             }
317:         );
318:         let err = slot_rect(Orientation::Landscape, Slot::L, Cell { col: 2, row: 0 }).unwrap_err();
319:         assert!(matches!(err, LayoutError::OutOfBounds { .. }));
320:         assert!(slot_rect(Orientation::Portrait, Slot::XL, Cell { col: 0, row: 1 }).is_err());
321:         assert!(
322:             slot_rect(
323:                 Orientation::Landscape,
324:                 Slot::S,
325:                 Cell {
326:                     col: u32::MAX,
327:                     row: 0
328:                 }
329:             )
330:             .is_err()
331:         );
332:     }
333: 
334:     #[test]
335:     fn parse_slot_and_orientation() {
336:         assert_eq!("xl".parse::<Slot>().unwrap(), Slot::XL);
337:         assert_eq!("Large".parse::<Slot>().unwrap(), Slot::L);
338:         assert!("XXL".parse::<Slot>().is_err());
339:         assert_eq!(
340:             "vertical".parse::<Orientation>().unwrap(),
341:             Orientation::Portrait
342:         );
343:     }

3: //! Selection is pure: the caller describes the connected displays as [`DisplayInfo`] (from tao and
4: //! CoreGraphics) and gets back a [`Selection`].

7: use std::str::FromStr;

9: use crate::{LayoutError, Orientation};

11: /// EDID manufacturer id of the XENEON EDGE as reported by `CGDisplayVendorNumber` ("CRX").
12: pub const EDGE_EDID_VENDOR: u32 = 0x0e58;
13: /// EDID product id of the XENEON EDGE as reported by `CGDisplayModelNumber`.
14: pub const EDGE_EDID_MODEL: u32 = 0xed00;
15: 
16: /// A connected display.
17: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
18: pub struct DisplayInfo {
19:     /// Name reported by the windowing system (tao calls the Edge `Monitor #60672`).
20:     pub name: String,
21:     /// EDID vendor number, when known.
22:     pub vendor: Option<u32>,
23:     /// EDID model number, when known.
24:     pub model: Option<u32>,
25:     /// Current mode in device pixels `(width, height)`.
26:     pub pixel_size: (u32, u32),
27:     /// Device pixels per logical point.
28:     pub scale_factor: f64,
29:     /// Top-left in global logical coordinates.
30:     pub logical_origin: (f64, f64),
31:     /// Size in logical points.
32:     pub logical_size: (f64, f64),
33: }
34: 
35: impl DisplayInfo {
36:     /// Whether the EDID identifies this display as a XENEON EDGE.
37:     #[must_use]
38:     pub fn is_edge_hardware(&self) -> bool {
39:         self.vendor == Some(EDGE_EDID_VENDOR) && self.model == Some(EDGE_EDID_MODEL)
40:     }
41: 
42:     /// The orientation whose native panel size equals the current pixel size, if any.
43:     #[must_use]
44:     pub fn native_orientation(&self) -> Option<Orientation> {
45:         [Orientation::Landscape, Orientation::Portrait]
46:             .into_iter()
47:             .find(|o| o.panel_size() == self.pixel_size)
48:     }
49: 
50:     /// Orientation to lay the panel out in: native if the mode matches, otherwise by aspect.
51:     #[must_use]
52:     pub fn orientation(&self) -> Orientation {
53:         self.native_orientation()
54:             .unwrap_or(if self.pixel_size.0 >= self.pixel_size.1 {
55:                 Orientation::Landscape
56:             } else {
57:                 Orientation::Portrait
58:             })
59:     }
60: }

62: /// Which display to use, as given by the user (`--display`, or `display` in `layout.json`).
63: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
64: pub enum DisplayChoice {
65:     /// The XENEON EDGE by EDID, else a display in its native size, else a stand-in window.
66:     #[default]
67:     Auto,
68:     /// Always a stand-in window on the current display.
69:     Windowed,
70:     /// A display by index (as listed by `edgectl displays`) or case-insensitive name substring.
71:     Pick(String),
72: }
73: 
74: impl FromStr for DisplayChoice {
75:     type Err = std::convert::Infallible;
76: 
77:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
78:         Ok(match s.trim().to_ascii_lowercase().as_str() {
79:             "" | "auto" => Self::Auto,
80:             "windowed" | "window" => Self::Windowed,
81:             _ => Self::Pick(s.trim().to_owned()),
82:         })
83:     }
84: }
85: 
86: impl fmt::Display for DisplayChoice {
87:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
88:         match self {
89:             Self::Auto => f.write_str("auto"),
90:             Self::Windowed => f.write_str("windowed"),
91:             Self::Pick(s) => f.write_str(s),
92:         }
93:     }
94: }

96: /// Result of [`select_display`].
97: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
98: pub enum Selection {
99:     /// Cover display `index` with the surface.
100:     Display {
101:         /// Index into the slice given to [`select_display`].
102:         index: usize,
103:         /// Orientation to lay out in.
104:         orientation: Orientation,
105:         /// The EDID says this is a XENEON EDGE.
106:         edge_hardware: bool,
107:         /// The current mode is the panel's native 2560×720 / 720×2560.
108:         native: bool,
109:     },
110:     /// No suitable display: use a stand-in window.
111:     Windowed,
112: }
113: 
114: /// Choose the display for the surface.
115: ///
116: /// ```
117: /// use edge_layout::{select_display, DisplayChoice, DisplayInfo, Orientation, Selection};
118: /// let edge = DisplayInfo {
119: ///     name: "Monitor #60672".into(), vendor: Some(0x0e58), model: Some(0xed00),
120: ///     pixel_size: (2560, 720), scale_factor: 1.0,
121: ///     logical_origin: (1496.0, 0.0), logical_size: (2560.0, 720.0),
122: /// };
123: /// let sel = select_display(&[edge], &DisplayChoice::Auto).unwrap();
124: /// assert_eq!(sel, Selection::Display { index: 0, orientation: Orientation::Landscape,
125: ///                                      edge_hardware: true, native: true });
126: /// ```
127: ///
128: /// # Errors
129: ///
130: /// [`LayoutError::NoSuchDisplay`] if a [`DisplayChoice::Pick`] matches nothing.
131: pub fn select_display(
132:     displays: &[DisplayInfo],
133:     choice: &DisplayChoice,
134: ) -> Result<Selection, LayoutError> {
135:     let chosen = |index: usize, d: &DisplayInfo| Selection::Display {
136:         index,
137:         orientation: d.orientation(),
138:         edge_hardware: d.is_edge_hardware(),
139:         native: d.native_orientation().is_some(),
140:     };
141:     match choice {
142:         DisplayChoice::Windowed => Ok(Selection::Windowed),
143:         DisplayChoice::Auto => Ok(displays
144:             .iter()
145:             .position(DisplayInfo::is_edge_hardware)
146:             .or_else(|| {
147:                 displays
148:                     .iter()
149:                     .position(|d| d.native_orientation().is_some())
150:             })
151:             .and_then(|i| displays.get(i).map(|d| chosen(i, d)))
152:             .unwrap_or(Selection::Windowed)),
153:         DisplayChoice::Pick(query) => {
154:             let by_index = query
155:                 .parse::<usize>()
156:                 .ok()
157:                 .and_then(|i| displays.get(i).map(|d| (i, d)));
158:             let needle = query.to_lowercase();
159:             by_index
160:                 .or_else(|| {
161:                     displays
162:                         .iter()
163:                         .enumerate()
164:                         .find(|(_, d)| d.name.to_lowercase().contains(&needle))
165:                 })
166:                 .map(|(i, d)| chosen(i, d))
167:                 .ok_or_else(|| LayoutError::NoSuchDisplay {
168:                     query: query.clone(),
169:                     available: displays.iter().map(|d| d.name.clone()).collect(),
170:                 })
171:         }
172:     }
173: }
174: 
175: /// How the panel is drawn inside an area of logical points.
176: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
177: pub struct Fit {
178:     /// Logical points per panel pixel.
179:     pub scale: f64,
180:     /// Offset of the panel's top-left inside the area, in logical points (letterboxing).
181:     pub offset: (f64, f64),
182: }
183: 
184: /// Fit the panel for `orientation` into `area` (logical points), preserving aspect ratio.
185: ///
186: /// ```
187: /// use edge_layout::{fit_panel, Orientation};
188: /// let f = fit_panel(Orientation::Landscape, (2560.0, 720.0));
189: /// assert!((f.scale - 1.0).abs() < 1e-9 && f.offset == (0.0, 0.0));
190: /// ```
191: #[must_use]
192: pub fn fit_panel(orientation: Orientation, area: (f64, f64)) -> Fit {
193:     let (pw, ph) = orientation.panel_size();
194:     let (pw, ph) = (f64::from(pw), f64::from(ph));
195:     let scale = (area.0 / pw).min(area.1 / ph);
196:     Fit {
197:         scale,
198:         offset: ((area.0 - pw * scale) / 2.0, (area.1 - ph * scale) / 2.0),
199:     }
200: }

6: //! ├── common/                  optional shared folder served at /common/ (iCUE's widgets/common)
7: //! ├── layout.json              pages, widget instances and their property values
8: //! └── tmp/                     staging for installs (same volume, so installs are a rename)

24: /// Errors reading or writing the store.
25: #[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
26: pub enum StoreError {
27:     /// A file or directory could not be read or written.
28:     #[error("{action} {path}")]
29:     Io {
30:         /// What was being done ("reading", "writing", …).
31:         action: &'static str,
32:         /// The path involved.
33:         path: PathBuf,
34:         /// Underlying error.
35:         #[source]
36:         source: io::Error,
37:     },
38:     /// `layout.json` is not valid.
39:     #[error("parsing {path}")]
40:     Layout {
41:         /// The layout file.
42:         path: PathBuf,
43:         /// Underlying error.
44:         #[source]
45:         source: serde_json::Error,
46:     },
47:     /// Neither `$EDGEHOST_HOME` nor `$HOME` is set.
48:     #[error("cannot locate the data folder: neither EDGEHOST_HOME nor HOME is set")]
49:     NoHome,
50:     /// The requested widget is not installed.
51:     #[error("widget {0} is not installed")]
52:     NotInstalled(String),
53: }

57: pub struct Installed {

74: impl Store {
75:     /// `$EDGEHOST_HOME`, else `~/Library/Application Support/EdgeHost`.
76:     ///
77:     /// # Errors
78:     ///
79:     /// [`StoreError::NoHome`] if neither variable is set.
80:     pub fn default_root() -> Result<PathBuf, StoreError> {
81:         if let Some(home) = std::env::var_os("EDGEHOST_HOME") {
82:             return Ok(PathBuf::from(home));
83:         }
84:         let home = std::env::var_os("HOME").ok_or(StoreError::NoHome)?;
85:         Ok(PathBuf::from(home).join("Library/Application Support/EdgeHost"))
86:     }
87: 
88:     /// Open (creating if needed) the store at `root`.
89:     ///
90:     /// # Errors
91:     ///
92:     /// [`StoreError::Io`] if the directories cannot be created.
93:     pub fn open(root: impl Into<PathBuf>) -> Result<Self, StoreError> {
94:         let store = Self { root: root.into() };
95:         for dir in [store.widgets_dir(), store.tmp_dir()] {
96:             fs::create_dir_all(&dir).map_err(|source| StoreError::Io {
97:                 action: "creating",
98:                 path: dir,
99:                 source,
100:             })?;
101:         }
102:         Ok(store)
103:     }
104:

106:     #[must_use]

108:         &self.root
109:     }
110:

113:     pub fn widgets_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {
114:         self.root.join("widgets")
115:     }
116:

119:     pub fn tmp_dir(&self) -> PathBuf {

121:     }
122:

124:     #[must_use]
125:     pub fn common_dir(&self) -> Option<PathBuf> {
126:         Some(self.root.join("common")).filter(|d| d.is_dir())
127:     }
128: 
129:     /// The layout file.
130:     #[must_use]
131:     pub fn layout_path(&self) -> PathBuf {
132:         self.root.join("layout.json")
133:     }
134: 
135:     /// Directory of one installed widget version.
136:     #[must_use]
137:     pub fn widget_dir(&self, id: &WidgetId, version: &str) -> PathBuf {
138:         self.widgets_dir().join(id.as_str()).join(version)
139:     }
140: 
141:     /// All installed widget versions, sorted by id then version (oldest first).
142:     ///
143:     /// Directories that do not hold a loadable widget are skipped.
144:     ///
145:     /// # Errors
146:     ///
147:     /// [`StoreError::Io`] if the widgets folder cannot be listed.
148:     pub fn installed(&self) -> Result<Vec<Installed>, StoreError> {
149:         let mut out = Vec::new();
150:         for id_dir in read_dirs(&self.widgets_dir())? {
151:             for version_dir in read_dirs(&id_dir)? {
152:                 if let Ok(def) = WidgetDef::load(&version_dir) {
153:                     let version = file_name(&version_dir);
154:                     out.push(Installed {
155:                         id: def.manifest.id,
156:                         version,
157:                         name: def.manifest.name,
158:                         path: version_dir,
159:                     });
160:                 }
161:             }
162:         }
163:         out.sort_by(|a, b| {
164:             a.id.as_str()
165:                 .cmp(b.id.as_str())
166:                 .then_with(|| compare_versions(&a.version, &b.version))
167:         });
168:         Ok(out)
169:     }
170: 
171:     /// The newest installed version of `id`.
172:     ///
173:     /// # Errors
174:     ///
175:     /// [`StoreError::NotInstalled`] if no version is installed, or a listing error.
176:     pub fn latest(&self, id: &WidgetId) -> Result<Installed, StoreError> {
177:         self.installed()?
178:             .into_iter()
179:             .filter(|w| &w.id == id)
180:             .max_by(|a, b| compare_versions(&a.version, &b.version))
181:             .ok_or_else(|| StoreError::NotInstalled(id.to_string()))
182:     }
183: 
184:     /// Delete one version, or every version when `version` is `None`.
185:     ///
186:     /// # Errors
187:     ///
188:     /// [`StoreError::NotInstalled`] if nothing matched, or [`StoreError::Io`].
189:     pub fn uninstall(
190:         &self,
191:         id: &WidgetId,
192:         version: Option<&str>,
193:     ) -> Result<Vec<String>, StoreError> {
194:         let targets: Vec<Installed> = self
195:             .installed()?
196:             .into_iter()
197:             .filter(|w| &w.id == id && version.is_none_or(|v| v == w.version))
198:             .collect();
199:         if targets.is_empty() {
200:             return Err(StoreError::NotInstalled(
201:                 version.map_or_else(|| id.to_string(), |v| format!("{id} {v}")),
202:             ));
203:         }
204:         for w in &targets {
205:             fs::remove_dir_all(&w.path).map_err(|source| StoreError::Io {
206:                 action: "removing",
207:                 path: w.path.clone(),
208:                 source,
209:             })?;
210:         }
211:         let id_dir = self.widgets_dir().join(id.as_str());
212:         if read_dirs(&id_dir).is_ok_and(|v| v.is_empty()) {
213:             // Best effort: an empty id folder is harmless if it cannot be removed.
214:             let _ = fs::remove_dir(&id_dir);
215:         }
216:         Ok(targets.into_iter().map(|w| w.version).collect())
217:     }
218: 
219:     /// Load `layout.json`, or an empty layout if it does not exist yet.
220:     ///
221:     /// # Errors
222:     ///
223:     /// [`StoreError::Io`] or [`StoreError::Layout`].
224:     pub fn load_layout(&self) -> Result<Layout, StoreError> {
225:         let path = self.layout_path();
226:         match fs::read_to_string(&path) {
227:             Ok(text) => {
228:                 serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|source| StoreError::Layout { path, source })
229:             }
230:             Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Ok(Layout::default()),
231:             Err(source) => Err(StoreError::Io {
232:                 action: "reading",
233:                 path,
234:                 source,
235:             }),
236:         }
237:     }
238: 
239:     /// Write `layout.json` atomically (temporary file, then rename).
240:     ///
241:     /// # Errors
242:     ///
243:     /// [`StoreError::Io`].
244:     pub fn save_layout(&self, layout: &Layout) -> Result<(), StoreError> {
245:         let path = self.layout_path();
246:         let io_err = |source| StoreError::Io {
247:             action: "writing",
248:             path: path.clone(),
249:             source,
250:         };
251:         let mut tmp = tempfile::NamedTempFile::new_in(&self.root).map_err(io_err)?;
252:         let json = serde_json::to_string_pretty(layout).map_err(|e| io_err(io::Error::other(e)))?;
253:         tmp.write_all(json.as_bytes()).map_err(io_err)?;
254:         tmp.write_all(b"\n").map_err(io_err)?;
255:         tmp.persist(&path).map_err(|e| io_err(e.error))?;
256:         Ok(())
257:     }
258: }
259: 
260: /// Compare dotted version strings numerically where possible (`1.10.0` > `1.9.2`).
261: #[must_use]
262: pub fn compare_versions(a: &str, b: &str) -> Ordering {
263:     let parts = |v: &str| {
264:         v.split('.')
265:             .map(|p| p.parse::<u64>().ok())
266:             .collect::<Vec<_>>()
267:     };
268:     let (pa, pb) = (parts(a), parts(b));
269:     if pa.iter().chain(&pb).all(Option::is_some) {
270:         pa.cmp(&pb)
271:     } else {
272:         a.cmp(b)
273:     }
274: }
275: 
276: fn read_dirs(dir: &Path) -> Result<Vec<PathBuf>, StoreError> {
277:     let entries = match fs::read_dir(dir) {
278:         Ok(e) => e,
279:         Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => return Ok(Vec::new()),
280:         Err(source) => {
281:             return Err(StoreError::Io {
282:                 action: "listing",
283:                 path: dir.to_path_buf(),
284:                 source,
285:             });
286:         }
287:     };
288:     Ok(entries
289:         .filter_map(Result::ok)
290:         .filter(|e| e.file_type().is_ok_and(|t| t.is_dir()))
291:         .map(|e| e.path())
292:         .collect())
293: }
294: 
295: fn file_name(path: &Path) -> String {
296:     path.file_name()
297:         .map(|n| n.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
298:         .unwrap_or_default()
299: }

308:         assert_eq!(compare_versions("1.0.0", "1.0.0"), Ordering::Equal);

1: //! Installing widgets from `.icuewidget` / `.zip` packages or folders.
2: //!
3: //! Untrusted input: entries that escape the destination, symlinks, and oversized or overlong
4: //! archives are rejected before anything reaches the widgets folder. A package is staged in the
5: //! store's `tmp/`, validated, and only then renamed into `widgets/<id>/<version>/`.

15: /// Maximum total uncompressed size of a package.
16: pub const MAX_TOTAL_BYTES: u64 = 64 * 1024 * 1024;
17: /// Maximum number of entries in a package.
18: pub const MAX_ENTRIES: usize = 10_000;
19: 
20: /// Errors installing a widget.
21: #[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
22: pub enum InstallError {
23:     /// A file could not be read or written.
24:     #[error("{action} {path}")]
25:     Io {
26:         /// What was being done.
27:         action: &'static str,
28:         /// The path involved.
29:         path: PathBuf,
30:         /// Underlying error.
31:         #[source]
32:         source: io::Error,
33:     },
34:     /// The archive is not a readable zip file.
35:     #[error("reading archive {path}")]
36:     Zip {
37:         /// The archive.
38:         path: PathBuf,
39:         /// Underlying error.
40:         #[source]
41:         source: zip::result::ZipError,
42:     },
43:     /// The package contains something unsafe (path escape, symlink).
44:     #[error("refusing unsafe package entry {0:?}")]
45:     Unsafe(String),
46:     /// The package exceeds [`MAX_TOTAL_BYTES`] or [`MAX_ENTRIES`].
47:     #[error("package is too large (limits: {MAX_ENTRIES} entries, {} MiB)", MAX_TOTAL_BYTES / 1024 / 1024)]
48:     TooLarge,
49:     /// No `manifest.json` at the top level or in a single top-level folder.
50:     #[error("no manifest.json found in the package")]
51:     NoManifest,
52:     /// Validation found errors.
53:     #[error("the widget failed validation")]
54:     Invalid(Box<Report>),
55: }
56: 
57: /// A successful install.
58: #[derive(Debug)]
59: pub struct InstallOutcome {
60:     /// Widget id.
61:     pub id: WidgetId,
62:     /// Installed version.
63:     pub version: String,
64:     /// Where it was installed.
65:     pub path: PathBuf,
66:     /// Whether an existing copy of this version was replaced.
67:     pub replaced: bool,
68:     /// Validation report (warnings only).
69:     pub report: Report,
70: }
71: 
72: /// Install a package file or widget folder into `store`.
73: ///
74: /// # Errors
75: ///
76: /// See [`InstallError`]; validation errors come back as [`InstallError::Invalid`] with the report.
77: pub fn install(store: &Store, source: &Path) -> Result<InstallOutcome, InstallError> {
78:     let staging = tempfile::Builder::new()
79:         .prefix("install-")
80:         .tempdir_in(store.tmp_dir())
81:         .map_err(|e| io_err("creating staging folder in", &store.tmp_dir(), e))?;
82:     let root = unpack(source, staging.path())?;
83:     let mut report = validate_dir(&root);
84:     if report.has_errors() {
85:         return Err(InstallError::Invalid(Box::new(report)));
86:     }
87:     let Some(def) = report.widget.take() else {
88:         return Err(InstallError::Invalid(Box::new(report)));
89:     };
90:     let id = def.manifest.id;
91:     let version = def.manifest.version;
92:     let dest = store.widget_dir(&id, &version);
93:     let replaced = dest.exists();
94:     if replaced {
95:         fs::remove_dir_all(&dest).map_err(|e| io_err("removing old copy", &dest, e))?;
96:     }
97:     if let Some(parent) = dest.parent() {
98:         fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| io_err("creating", parent, e))?;
99:     }
100:     fs::rename(&root, &dest).map_err(|e| io_err("moving widget into", &dest, e))?;
101:     Ok(InstallOutcome {
102:         id,
103:         version,
104:         path: dest,
105:         replaced,
106:         report,
107:     })
108: }
109: 
110: /// Unpack `source` (archive or folder) into `dest` and return the widget's root inside it: `dest`
111: /// itself, or the single top-level folder that holds `manifest.json`.
112: ///
113: /// # Errors
114: ///
115: /// I/O, zip, safety and size errors, and [`InstallError::NoManifest`].
116: pub fn unpack(source: &Path, dest: &Path) -> Result<PathBuf, InstallError> {
117:     let meta = fs::metadata(source).map_err(|e| io_err("reading", source, e))?;
118:     let mut budget = Budget::default();
119:     if meta.is_dir() {
120:         copy_tree(source, dest, &mut budget)?;
121:     } else {
122:         extract_zip(source, dest, &mut budget)?;
123:     }
124:     if dest.join("manifest.json").is_file() {
125:         return Ok(dest.to_path_buf());
126:     }
127:     let mut dirs = fs::read_dir(dest)
128:         .map_err(|e| io_err("listing", dest, e))?
129:         .filter_map(Result::ok)
130:         .filter(|e| e.file_type().is_ok_and(|t| t.is_dir()))
131:         .map(|e| e.path());
132:     match (dirs.next(), dirs.next()) {
133:         (Some(only), None) if only.join("manifest.json").is_file() => Ok(only),
134:         _ => Err(InstallError::NoManifest),
135:     }
136: }
137: 
138: #[derive(Default)]
139: struct Budget {
140:     entries: usize,
141:     bytes: u64,
142: }
143: 
144: impl Budget {
145:     fn entry(&mut self) -> Result<(), InstallError> {
146:         self.entries += 1;
147:         if self.entries > MAX_ENTRIES {
148:             Err(InstallError::TooLarge)
149:         } else {
150:             Ok(())
151:         }
152:     }
153: 
154:     fn remaining(&self) -> u64 {
155:         MAX_TOTAL_BYTES.saturating_sub(self.bytes)
156:     }
157: 
158:     fn spend(&mut self, n: u64) -> Result<(), InstallError> {
159:         self.bytes = self.bytes.saturating_add(n);
160:         if self.bytes > MAX_TOTAL_BYTES {
161:             Err(InstallError::TooLarge)
162:         } else {
163:             Ok(())
164:         }
165:     }
166: }
167: 
168: /// Skip hidden files and macOS archive metadata, as `icuewidget package` does.
169: fn is_ignored(path: &Path) -> bool {
170:     path.components().any(|c| match c {
171:         Component::Normal(name) => {
172:             let name = name.to_string_lossy();
173:             name.starts_with('.') || name == "__MACOSX" || name == "__pycache__"
174:         }
175:         _ => false,
176:     })
177: }
178: 
179: fn extract_zip(archive_path: &Path, dest: &Path, budget: &mut Budget) -> Result<(), InstallError> {
180:     let zip_err = |source| InstallError::Zip {
181:         path: archive_path.to_path_buf(),
182:         source,
183:     };
184:     let file = File::open(archive_path).map_err(|e| io_err("opening", archive_path, e))?;
185:     let mut archive = zip::ZipArchive::new(file).map_err(zip_err)?;
186:     if archive.len() > MAX_ENTRIES {
187:         return Err(InstallError::TooLarge);
188:     }
189:     for i in 0..archive.len() {
190:         let mut entry = archive.by_index(i).map_err(zip_err)?;
191:         let raw_name = entry
192:             .name()
193:             .map(std::borrow::Cow::into_owned)
194:             .unwrap_or_default();
195:         let Some(rel) = entry.enclosed_name() else {
196:             return Err(InstallError::Unsafe(raw_name));
197:         };
198:         if is_ignored(&rel) {
199:             continue;
200:         }
201:         if entry.is_symlink() {
202:             return Err(InstallError::Unsafe(raw_name));
203:         }
204:         budget.entry()?;
205:         let out = dest.join(&rel);
206:         if entry.is_dir() {
207:             fs::create_dir_all(&out).map_err(|e| io_err("creating", &out, e))?;
208:             continue;
209:         }
210:         if let Some(parent) = out.parent() {
211:             fs::create_dir_all(parent).map_err(|e| io_err("creating", parent, e))?;
212:         }
213:         let mut file = File::create(&out).map_err(|e| io_err("writing", &out, e))?;
214:         // Read one byte past the budget so an oversized entry is detected without trusting headers.
215:         let limit = budget.remaining().saturating_add(1);
216:         let written = io::copy(&mut (&mut entry).take(limit), &mut file)
217:             .map_err(|e| io_err("extracting", &out, e))?;
218:         budget.spend(written)?;
219:     }
220:     Ok(())
221: }
222: 
223: fn copy_tree(from: &Path, to: &Path, budget: &mut Budget) -> Result<(), InstallError> {
224:     fs::create_dir_all(to).map_err(|e| io_err("creating", to, e))?;
225:     for entry in fs::read_dir(from).map_err(|e| io_err("listing", from, e))? {
226:         let entry = entry.map_err(|e| io_err("listing", from, e))?;
227:         let path = entry.path();
228:         if is_ignored(Path::new(&entry.file_name())) {
229:             continue;
230:         }
231:         let kind = entry
232:             .file_type()
233:             .map_err(|e| io_err("inspecting", &path, e))?;
234:         if kind.is_symlink() {
235:             return Err(InstallError::Unsafe(path.display().to_string()));
236:         }
237:         budget.entry()?;
238:         let target = to.join(entry.file_name());
239:         if kind.is_dir() {
240:             copy_tree(&path, &target, budget)?;
241:         } else {
242:             let n = fs::copy(&path, &target).map_err(|e| io_err("copying", &path, e))?;
243:             budget.spend(n)?;
244:         }
245:     }
246:     Ok(())
247: }
248: 
249: fn io_err(action: &'static str, path: &Path, source: io::Error) -> InstallError {
250:     InstallError::Io {
251:         action,
252:         path: path.to_path_buf(),
253:         source,
254:     }
255: }

228:                             orientation,
229:                         })?;
230:                 if let Some(other) = cells.iter().find_map(|c| occupied.get(c)) {
231:                     return Err(LayoutError::Overlap {
232:                         slot,
233:                         origin: cell,
234:                         other: other.short(),
235:                     });
236:                 }
237:                 cell
238:             }
239:             None => orientation
240:                 .all_cells()
241:                 .find(|&c| {
242:                     slot.cells(orientation, c)
243:                         .is_some_and(|cells| cells.iter().all(|x| !occupied.contains_key(x)))
244:                 })
245:                 .ok_or(LayoutError::NoRoom { slot, page })?,
246:         };
247:         if page == self.pages.len() {
248:             self.pages.push(Page::default());
249:         }
250:         let id = InstanceId::new();
251:         let instance = Instance {
252:             id,
253:             widget,
254:             version,
255:             slot,
256:             origin,
257:             values: BTreeMap::new(),
258:             user_set: BTreeSet::new(),
259:             evaluated_version: None,
260:         };
261:         if let Some(p) = self.pages.get_mut(page) {
262:             p.instances.push(instance);
263:         }
264:         Ok(id)
265:     }
266: 
267:     /// Remove an instance; empty trailing pages are dropped.
268:     ///
269:     /// # Errors
270:     ///
271:     /// [`LayoutError::UnknownInstance`].
272:     pub fn remove(&mut self, id: InstanceId) -> Result<Instance, LayoutError> {
273:         for page in &mut self.pages {
274:             if let Some(pos) = page.instances.iter().position(|i| i.id == id) {
275:                 let removed = page.instances.remove(pos);
276:                 while self.pages.last().is_some_and(|p| p.instances.is_empty()) {
277:                     self.pages.pop();
278:                 }
279:                 return Ok(removed);
280:             }
281:         }
282:         Err(LayoutError::UnknownInstance(id.to_string()))
283:     }
284: 
285:     /// Set a property value chosen by the user.
286:     ///
287:     /// # Errors
288:     ///
289:     /// [`LayoutError::UnknownInstance`].
290:     pub fn set_value(
291:         &mut self,
292:         id: InstanceId,
293:         key: &str,
294:         value: Value,
295:     ) -> Result<(), LayoutError> {
296:         let inst = self.get_mut(id)?;
297:         inst.values.insert(key.to_owned(), value);
298:         inst.user_set.insert(key.to_owned());
299:         Ok(())
300:     }
301: 
302:     /// Forget a user-set value; the default is restored on the next evaluation.
303:     ///
304:     /// # Errors
305:     ///
306:     /// [`LayoutError::UnknownInstance`].
307:     pub fn unset_value(&mut self, id: InstanceId, key: &str) -> Result<(), LayoutError> {
308:         let inst = self.get_mut(id)?;
309:         inst.values.remove(key);
310:         inst.user_set.remove(key);
311:         inst.evaluated_version = None;
312:         Ok(())
313:     }
314: 
315:     /// Merge evaluated defaults, keeping user-set values, and mark the version as evaluated.
316:     ///
317:     /// # Errors
318:     ///
319:     /// [`LayoutError::UnknownInstance`].
320:     pub fn apply_evaluated(
321:         &mut self,
322:         id: InstanceId,
323:         values: BTreeMap<String, Value>,
324:     ) -> Result<(), LayoutError> {
325:         let inst = self.get_mut(id)?;
326:         for (k, v) in values {
327:             if !inst.user_set.contains(&k) {
328:                 inst.values.insert(k, v);
329:             }
330:         }
331:         inst.evaluated_version = Some(inst.version.clone());
332:         Ok(())
333:     }
334: }
335: 
336: fn occupied_cells(page: &Page, orientation: Orientation) -> HashMap<Cell, InstanceId> {
337:     page.instances
338:         .iter()
339:         .flat_map(|i| {
340:             i.slot
341:                 .cells(orientation, i.origin)
342:                 .unwrap_or_default()
343:                 .into_iter()
344:                 .map(move |c| (c, i.id))
345:         })
346:         .collect()
347: }
348: 
349: impl FromStr for InstanceId {
350:     type Err = uuid::Error;
351: 
352:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
353:         Uuid::parse_str(s).map(Self)
354:     }
355: }

367:         let mut l = Layout::default();

369:         let b = l.add(0, id(), "1.0.0".into(), Slot::S, None).unwrap();
370:         let c = l.add(0, id(), "1.0.0".into(), Slot::S, None).unwrap();
371:         let origin = |x| {

373:                 .find(|(_, i)| i.id == x)
374:                 .map(|(_, i)| i.origin)

377:         assert_eq!(origin(a), Cell { col: 0, row: 0 });

379:         assert_eq!(origin(c), Cell { col: 2, row: 1 });
380:         assert_eq!(

1: //! Parsing for iCUE HTML widgets (Widget API 1.9.0, see `docs/research/icue-widget-api-reference.md`).
2: //!
3: //! A widget directory holds `manifest.json`, `index.html` and optionally `translation.json`.
4: //! [`WidgetDef::load`] reads all three; the pure parsers ([`Manifest::from_json`],
5: //! [`HeadInfo::parse`], [`Translations::from_json`]) are usable on in-memory text.
6: //!
7: //! Attribute values such as `data-default` are JavaScript expressions. They are kept as raw
8: //! text ([`PropertyDef::attrs`]) and evaluated inside the widget's web view, never in Rust.

26: /// Errors produced while loading or parsing a widget.
27: #[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
28: pub enum WidgetError {
29:     /// A required file could not be read.
30:     #[error("reading {path}")]
31:     Io {
32:         /// File that failed.
33:         path: PathBuf,
34:         /// Underlying error.
35:         #[source]
36:         source: io::Error,
37:     },
38:     /// `manifest.json` or `translation.json` is not valid for its schema.
39:     #[error("parsing {file}")]
40:     Json {
41:         /// Which file (`manifest.json` / `translation.json`).
42:         file: &'static str,
43:         /// Underlying error.
44:         #[source]
45:         source: serde_json::Error,
46:     },
47:     /// The widget id is not reverse-DNS characters only.
48:     #[error("invalid widget id {0:?}: only letters, digits, '.' and '-' are allowed")]
49:     InvalidWidgetId(String),
50:     /// A `required_plugins` entry is not `namespace:Name:version`.
51:     #[error("invalid plugin reference {0:?}: expected namespace:Name:major.minor")]
52:     InvalidPluginRef(String),
53:     /// An `x-icue-property` has an unknown `data-type` (iCUE: "Invalid meta parameter data type").
54:     #[error("invalid meta parameter data type {data_type:?} on property {property:?}")]
55:     InvalidDataType {
56:         /// Property `content` name.
57:         property: String,
58:         /// The offending `data-type` value.
59:         data_type: String,
60:     },
61:     /// An `x-icue-property` is missing a required attribute.
62:     #[error("x-icue-property {property:?} is missing {attribute}")]
63:     MissingAttribute {
64:         /// Property `content` name (empty if `content` itself is missing).
65:         property: String,
66:         /// Missing attribute name.
67:         attribute: &'static str,
68:     },
69:     /// A property name is not usable as a JavaScript global.
70:     #[error(
71:         "invalid property name {0:?}: use letters, digits and '_' and do not start with a digit"
72:     )]
73:     InvalidPropertyName(String),
74:     /// The same property name is declared twice.
75:     #[error("duplicate x-icue-property {0:?}")]
76:     DuplicateProperty(String),
77:     /// The `x-icue-groups` script is not a valid JSON array of groups.
78:     #[error("parsing x-icue-groups")]
79:     Groups(#[source] serde_json::Error),
80: }

82: /// A fully parsed widget directory.
83: #[derive(Debug)]
84: pub struct WidgetDef {
85:     root: PathBuf,
86:     /// Parsed `manifest.json`.
87:     pub manifest: Manifest,
88:     /// Parsed `<head>` of `index.html`.
89:     pub head: HeadInfo,
90:     /// Parsed `translation.json` (empty if the file is absent).
91:     pub translations: Translations,
92: }
93: 
94: impl WidgetDef {
95:     /// Load and parse a widget directory.
96:     ///
97:     /// # Errors
98:     ///
99:     /// Returns [`WidgetError::Io`] if `manifest.json` or `index.html` cannot be read, and the
100:     /// parse errors of [`Manifest::from_json`], [`HeadInfo::parse`] and
101:     /// [`Translations::from_json`].
102:     pub fn load(dir: impl AsRef<Path>) -> Result<Self, WidgetError> {
103:         let root = dir.as_ref().to_path_buf();
104:         let manifest = Manifest::from_json(&read(&root.join("manifest.json"))?)?;
105:         let head = HeadInfo::parse(&read(&root.join("index.html"))?)?;
106:         let tr_path = root.join("translation.json");
107:         let translations = match fs::read_to_string(&tr_path) {
108:             Ok(text) => Translations::from_json(&text)?,
109:             Err(e) if e.kind() == io::ErrorKind::NotFound => Translations::default(),
110:             Err(source) => {
111:                 return Err(WidgetError::Io {
112:                     path: tr_path,
113:                     source,
114:                 });
115:             }
116:         };
117:         Ok(Self {
118:             root,
119:             manifest,
120:             head,
121:             translations,
122:         })
123:     }
124: 
125:     /// Directory the widget was loaded from.
126:     #[must_use]
127:     pub fn root(&self) -> &Path {
128:         &self.root
129:     }
130: }
131: 
132: fn read(path: &Path) -> Result<String, WidgetError> {
133:     fs::read_to_string(path).map_err(|source| WidgetError::Io {
134:         path: path.to_path_buf(),
135:         source,
136:     })
137: }

29:             && s.chars()
30:                 .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '.' || c == '-');
31:         if valid {

33:         } else {

59: /// A `required_plugins` entry: `namespace:Name:major.minor`.
60: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
61: pub struct PluginRef {
62:     /// Module name, e.g. `widgetbuilder.sensorsdataprovider`.
63:     pub module: String,
64:     /// Plugin name, e.g. `Sensors`.
65:     pub name: String,

71: 
72: impl PluginRef {
73:     /// Name of the `window.plugins` facade: the module name without its namespace, first letter
74:     /// upper-cased and the rest lower-cased (`widgetbuilder.sensorsdataprovider` → `Sensorsdataprovider`).
75:     #[must_use]
76:     pub fn facade_name(&self) -> String {
77:         let last = self.module.rsplit('.').next().unwrap_or(&self.module);
78:         let mut chars = last.chars();
79:         chars.next().map_or_else(String::new, |first| {
80:             first
81:                 .to_uppercase()
82:                 .chain(chars.flat_map(char::to_lowercase))
83:                 .collect()
84:         })
85:     }
86: }
87: 
88: impl FromStr for PluginRef {
89:     type Err = WidgetError;
90: 
91:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
92:         let bad = || WidgetError::InvalidPluginRef(s.to_owned());
93:         let mut parts = s.split(':');
94:         let (Some(module), Some(name), Some(version), None) =
95:             (parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next(), parts.next())
96:         else {
97:             return Err(bad());
98:         };
99:         let (major, minor) = version.split_once('.').ok_or_else(bad)?;
100:         if module.is_empty() || name.is_empty() {
101:             return Err(bad());
102:         }
103:         Ok(Self {
104:             module: module.to_owned(),
105:             name: name.to_owned(),
106:             major: major.parse().map_err(|_| bad())?,
107:             minor: minor.parse().map_err(|_| bad())?,
108:         })
109:     }
110: }

184: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Deserialize)]
185: pub struct Manifest {

188:     /// Reverse-DNS id.
189:     pub id: WidgetId,
190:     /// Display name.
191:     pub name: String,
192:     /// Description.
193:     pub description: String,
194:     /// Semver widget version.
195:     pub version: String,
196:     /// Icon path relative to the manifest.
197:     pub preview_icon: String,
198:     /// Minimum Widget API version.
199:     pub min_framework_version: String,
200:     /// Minimum iCUE version. Required by the spec but omitted by some official examples.
201:     #[serde(default)]
202:     pub min_app_version: Option<String>,
203:     /// Declared platforms (advisory on macOS).
204:     pub os: Vec<OsEntry>,
205:     /// Device classes the widget targets.
206:     pub supported_devices: Vec<SupportedDevice>,
207:     /// Whether the widget handles touch input.
208:     #[serde(default)]
209:     pub interactive: bool,
210:     /// Plugins the widget needs.
211:     #[serde(default)]
212:     pub required_plugins: Vec<PluginRef>,
213:     /// ES module paths whose exports become globals named after the file stem.
214:     #[serde(default)]
215:     pub modules: Vec<String>,
216:     /// Permission declarations, enforced from milestone M3.
217:     #[serde(default)]
218:     pub permissions: Vec<serde_json::Value>,
219: }
220: 
221: impl Manifest {
222:     /// Parse `manifest.json` text.
223:     ///
224:     /// # Errors
225:     ///
226:     /// [`WidgetError::Json`] for missing required keys, wrong types, an invalid `id`, unknown
227:     /// device types or malformed `required_plugins` entries.
228:     pub fn from_json(text: &str) -> Result<Self, WidgetError> {
229:         serde_json::from_str(text).map_err(|source| WidgetError::Json {
230:             file: "manifest.json",
231:             source,
232:         })
233:     }
234: 
235:     /// The `{"type": "url", "domain": …, "port"?: …}` permissions, normalised (lower-case domain).
236:     /// Malformed entries are skipped.
237:     #[must_use]
238:     pub fn url_permissions(&self) -> Vec<UrlPermission> {
239:         self.permissions
240:             .iter()
241:             .filter(|p| p.get("type").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_str) == Some("url"))
242:             .filter_map(|p| {
243:                 let domain = p.get("domain")?.as_str()?.trim().trim_end_matches('.').to_ascii_lowercase();
244:                 let port = match p.get("port") {
245:                     None | Some(serde_json::Value::Null) => None,
246:                     Some(v) => Some(u16::try_from(v.as_u64()?).ok()?),
247:                 };
248:                 (!domain.is_empty()).then_some(UrlPermission { domain, port })
249:             })
250:             .collect()
251:     }

253:     /// Whether the widget declares support for the XENEON EDGE.
254:     #[must_use]
255:     pub fn supports_edge(&self) -> bool {
256:         self.supported_devices
257:             .iter()
258:             .any(|d| d.kind == DeviceType::DashboardLcd)
259:     }
260: }

74: impl FromStr for PropertyName {

77:     fn from_str(s: &str) -> Result<Self, Self::Err> {
78:         let mut chars = s.chars();

80:             .next()
81:             .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_alphabetic() || c == '_')
82:             && chars.all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_');
83:         if valid {
84:             Ok(Self(s.to_owned()))
85:         } else {

87:         }
88:     }

131: /// Widget metadata from the `<head>` of `index.html`.
132: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
133: pub struct HeadInfo {
134:     /// `<title>` text: a literal or an expression such as `tr('Name')`.
135:     pub title: Option<String>,
136:     /// `<link rel="icon">` href.
137:     pub icon: Option<String>,
138:     /// `x-icue-widget-group` content.
139:     pub group: Option<String>,
140:     /// `x-icue-widget-preview` content.
141:     pub preview: Option<String>,
142:     /// Controls, in document order.
143:     pub properties: Vec<PropertyDef>,
144:     /// Property groups.
145:     pub groups: Vec<PropertyGroup>,
146: }
147: 
148: impl HeadInfo {
149:     /// Parse the widget's `index.html`.
150:     ///
151:     /// Parsing is lenient HTML5 (iCUE's stricter XML parsing of `<head>` is a validator concern).
152:     ///
153:     /// # Errors
154:     ///
155:     /// Invalid or duplicate property names, unknown `data-type` values, missing `content` or
156:     /// `data-type` attributes, and malformed `x-icue-groups` JSON.
157:     pub fn parse(html: &str) -> Result<Self, WidgetError> {
158:         let doc = Html::parse_document(html);
159:         let mut info = Self {
160:             title: select_first(&doc, "head > title")
161:                 .map(|e| e.text().collect::<String>().trim().to_owned())
162:                 .filter(|t| !t.is_empty()),
163:             icon: select_first(&doc, r#"link[rel~="icon"]"#)
164:                 .and_then(|e| e.attr("href"))
165:                 .map(str::to_owned),
166:             group: meta_content(&doc, "x-icue-widget-group"),
167:             preview: meta_content(&doc, "x-icue-widget-preview"),
168:             ..Self::default()
169:         };
170: 
171:         let mut seen = HashSet::new();
172:         for el in doc.select(&selector(r#"meta[name="x-icue-property"]"#)) {
173:             let e = el.value();
174:             let content = e.attr("content").ok_or(WidgetError::MissingAttribute {
175:                 property: String::new(),
176:                 attribute: "content",
177:             })?;
178:             let name: PropertyName = content.parse()?;
179:             let data_type = e
180:                 .attr("data-type")
181:                 .ok_or_else(|| WidgetError::MissingAttribute {
182:                     property: content.to_owned(),
183:                     attribute: "data-type",
184:                 })?;
185:             let kind = data_type
186:                 .parse()
187:                 .map_err(|()| WidgetError::InvalidDataType {
188:                     property: content.to_owned(),
189:                     data_type: data_type.to_owned(),
190:                 })?;
191:             if !seen.insert(name.clone()) {
192:                 return Err(WidgetError::DuplicateProperty(content.to_owned()));
193:             }
194:             let attrs = e
195:                 .attrs()
196:                 .filter_map(|(k, v)| k.strip_prefix("data-").map(|k| (k, v)))
197:                 .filter(|(k, _)| *k != "type" && *k != "label")
198:                 .map(|(k, v)| (k.to_owned(), v.to_owned()))
199:                 .collect();
200:             info.properties.push(PropertyDef {
201:                 name,
202:                 kind,
203:                 label: e.attr("data-label").map(str::to_owned),
204:                 attrs,
205:             });
206:         }
207: 
208:         if let Some(script) = select_first(&doc, "script#x-icue-groups") {
209:             let text: String = script.text().collect();
210:             info.groups = serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(WidgetError::Groups)?;
211:         }
212:         Ok(info)
213:     }
214: }
215: 
216: fn selector(css: &str) -> Selector {
217:     // Only called with the literal selectors in this module, which are valid CSS.
218:     Selector::parse(css).unwrap_or_else(|e| unreachable!("invalid built-in selector {css:?}: {e}"))
219: }
220: 
221: fn select_first<'a>(doc: &'a Html, css: &str) -> Option<scraper::ElementRef<'a>> {
222:     doc.select(&selector(css)).next()
223: }
224: 
225: fn meta_content(doc: &Html, name: &str) -> Option<String> {
226:     doc.select(&selector("meta[name]"))
227:         .find(|e| e.value().attr("name") == Some(name))
228:         .and_then(|e| e.value().attr("content"))
229:         .map(str::to_owned)
230: }

1: //! `translation.json`.
2: //!
3: //! The documented shape is `{ "<lang>": { "translation": { "Key": "Value" } } }`. Some shipped
4: //! widgets (e.g. Corsair's Doodle Pad sample) use a flat `{ "Key": "Value" }` table instead; that
5: //! is read as the `en` table.
6: 
7: use std::collections::BTreeMap;
8: 
9: use serde::Deserialize;
10: 
11: use crate::WidgetError;
12: 
13: #[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
14: struct LangEntry {
15:     #[serde(default)]
16:     translation: BTreeMap<String, String>,
17: }
18: 
19: #[derive(Debug, Deserialize)]
20: #[serde(untagged)]
21: enum RawTranslations {
22:     Flat(BTreeMap<String, String>),
23:     PerLanguage(BTreeMap<String, LangEntry>),
24: }
25: 
26: /// Translation tables by language code (`en`, `de`, `zh_CN`, …).
27: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
28: pub struct Translations(BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, String>>);
29: 
30: impl Translations {
31:     /// Parse `translation.json` text.
32:     ///
33:     /// # Errors
34:     ///
35:     /// [`WidgetError::Json`] if the text is neither the documented shape nor a flat table.
36:     pub fn from_json(text: &str) -> Result<Self, WidgetError> {
37:         let raw: RawTranslations =
38:             serde_json::from_str(text).map_err(|source| WidgetError::Json {
39:                 file: "translation.json",
40:                 source,
41:             })?;
42:         Ok(Self(match raw {
43:             RawTranslations::Flat(table) if table.is_empty() => BTreeMap::new(),
44:             RawTranslations::Flat(table) => BTreeMap::from([("en".to_owned(), table)]),
45:             RawTranslations::PerLanguage(langs) => langs
46:                 .into_iter()
47:                 .map(|(lang, e)| (lang, e.translation))
48:                 .collect(),
49:         }))
50:     }
51: 
52:     /// The table for `lang`, with `en` entries filling any gaps (iCUE's documented fallback).
53:     ///
54:     /// ```
55:     /// use icuew_manifest::Translations;
56:     /// let t = Translations::from_json(r#"{"en":{"translation":{"Hi":"Hi","Bye":"Bye"}},
57:     ///                                     "de":{"translation":{"Hi":"Hallo"}}}"#).unwrap();
58:     /// let de = t.table("de");
59:     /// assert_eq!(de["Hi"], "Hallo");
60:     /// assert_eq!(de["Bye"], "Bye");
61:     /// ```
62:     #[must_use]
63:     pub fn table(&self, lang: &str) -> BTreeMap<String, String> {
64:         let mut out = self.0.get("en").cloned().unwrap_or_default();
65:         if lang != "en" {
66:             if let Some(t) = self.0.get(lang) {
67:                 out.extend(t.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), v.clone())));
68:             }
69:         }
70:         out
71:     }
72: 
73:     /// Language codes present.
74:     pub fn languages(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &str> {
75:         self.0.keys().map(String::as_str)
76:     }
77: }
78: 
79: #[cfg(test)]
80: mod tests {
81:     use super::*;
82: 
83:     #[test]
84:     fn flat_table_is_english() {
85:         let t = Translations::from_json(r#"{"Doodle pad": "Doodle pad", "Color 1": "Color 1"}"#)
86:             .unwrap();
87:         assert_eq!(t.languages().collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["en"]);
88:         assert_eq!(t.table("de")["Color 1"], "Color 1");
89:     }
90: 
91:     #[test]
92:     fn rejects_other_shapes() {
93:         assert!(Translations::from_json(r#"{"en": 5}"#).is_err());
94:         assert!(Translations::from_json("[]").is_err());
95:         assert_eq!(
96:             Translations::from_json("{}").unwrap(),
97:             Translations::default()
98:         );
99:     }
100: }

1: //! Package validation: the rules of `icuewidget-cli` 0.4.48 (`validate`), plus EdgeHost checks.
2: //!
3: //! Errors block installation; warnings are shown and the widget is installed anyway. Where
4: //! EdgeHost is more lenient than iCUE (for example a non-self-closing `<meta>` in `<head>`, which
5: //! iCUE's XML import rejects), the finding is a warning that says so.
6: 
7: use std::fmt;
8: use std::fs;
9: use std::path::{Component, Path};
10: 
11: use scraper::{Html, Selector};
12: use serde_json::Value;
13: 
14: use crate::{PluginRef, WidgetDef, WidgetError};
15: 
16: /// How serious a finding is.
17: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
18: pub enum Severity {
19:     /// Shown, does not block installation.
20:     Warning,
21:     /// Blocks installation.
22:     Error,
23: }
24: 
25: /// One validation finding.
26: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
27: pub struct Diagnostic {
28:     /// Severity.
29:     pub severity: Severity,
30:     /// Human-readable message.
31:     pub message: String,
32: }
33: 
34: impl fmt::Display for Diagnostic {
35:     fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
36:         let label = match self.severity {
37:             Severity::Warning => "warning",
38:             Severity::Error => "error",
39:         };
40:         write!(f, "{label}: {}", self.message)
41:     }
42: }
43: 
44: /// Result of [`validate_dir`].
45: #[derive(Debug, Default)]
46: pub struct Report {
47:     /// All findings, in discovery order.
48:     pub diagnostics: Vec<Diagnostic>,
49:     /// The parsed widget, if parsing succeeded.
50:     pub widget: Option<WidgetDef>,
51: }
52: 
53: impl Report {
54:     /// Whether any finding blocks installation.
55:     #[must_use]
56:     pub fn has_errors(&self) -> bool {
57:         self.diagnostics
58:             .iter()
59:             .any(|d| d.severity == Severity::Error)
60:     }
61: 
62:     fn push(&mut self, severity: Severity, message: impl Into<String>) {
63:         self.diagnostics.push(Diagnostic {
64:             severity,
65:             message: message.into(),
66:         });
67:     }
68: 
69:     fn error(&mut self, message: impl Into<String>) {
70:         self.push(Severity::Error, message);
71:     }
72: 
73:     fn warn(&mut self, message: impl Into<String>) {
74:         self.push(Severity::Warning, message);
75:     }
76: }
77: 
78: /// Validate an unpacked widget directory.
79: ///
80: /// Never fails: problems, including unreadable files, are reported as diagnostics.
81: #[must_use]
82: pub fn validate_dir(dir: &Path) -> Report {
83:     let mut report = Report::default();
84:     let manifest_text = fs::read_to_string(dir.join("manifest.json"));
85:     let index_text = fs::read_to_string(dir.join("index.html"));
86:     if manifest_text.is_err() {
87:         report.error("missing required file manifest.json");
88:     }
89:     if index_text.is_err() {
90:         report.error("missing required file index.html");
91:     }
92:     if !dir.join("translation.json").is_file() {
93:         report.warn("translation.json is missing; tr() will return its keys");
94:     }
95:     if let Ok(text) = &manifest_text {
96:         check_manifest(dir, text, &mut report);
97:     }
98:     if let Ok(html) = &index_text {
99:         check_index(dir, html, &mut report);
100:     }
101:     if !report.has_errors() {
102:         match WidgetDef::load(dir) {
103:             Ok(def) => {
104:                 if !def.manifest.supports_edge() {
105:                     report.warn("supported_devices does not include dashboard_lcd (XENEON EDGE)");
106:                 }
107:                 report.widget = Some(def);
108:             }
109:             Err(e) => report.error(error_chain(&e)),
110:         }
111:     }
112:     report
113: }
114: 
115: fn check_manifest(dir: &Path, text: &str, report: &mut Report) {
116:     let manifest: Value = match serde_json::from_str(text) {
117:         Ok(v @ Value::Object(_)) => v,
118:         Ok(_) => return report.error("manifest.json must be a JSON object"),
119:         Err(e) => return report.error(format!("manifest.json is not valid JSON: {e}")),
120:     };
121:     let string = |key: &str| manifest.get(key).and_then(Value::as_str).map(str::trim);
122:     for key in [
123:         "author",
124:         "id",
125:         "name",
126:         "description",
127:         "version",
128:         "preview_icon",
129:         "min_framework_version",
130:     ] {
131:         if string(key).is_none_or(str::is_empty) {
132:             report.error(format!(
133:                 "manifest.json: \"{key}\" must be a non-empty string"
134:             ));
135:         }
136:     }
137:     match string("min_app_version") {
138:         None | Some("") => {
139:             report.warn("manifest.json: \"min_app_version\" is missing (required by iCUE)");
140:         }
141:         Some(v) if !is_version(v, 2..=3) => {
142:             report.error(format!(
143:                 "manifest.json: \"min_app_version\" {v:?} must look like 5.47 or 5.47.1"
144:             ));
145:         }
146:         Some(_) => {}
147:     }
148:     if let Some(id) = string("id").filter(|id| !id.is_empty()) {
149:         if !id
150:             .chars()
151:             .all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '.' || c == '-')
152:         {
153:             report.error(format!(
154:                 "manifest.json: \"id\" {id:?} may only contain letters, digits, '.' and '-'"
155:             ));
156:         }
157:     }
158:     for key in ["version", "min_framework_version"] {
159:         if let Some(v) = string(key).filter(|v| !v.is_empty() && !is_version(v, 3..=3)) {
160:             report.error(format!(
161:                 "manifest.json: \"{key}\" {v:?} must be MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH"
162:             ));
163:         }
164:     }
165:     if let Some(icon) = string("preview_icon").filter(|p| !p.is_empty()) {
166:         check_local_file(dir, icon, "preview_icon", report);
167:     }
168:     check_array(&manifest, "os", report);
169:     check_array(&manifest, "supported_devices", report);
170:     if let Some(devices) = manifest.get("supported_devices").and_then(Value::as_array) {
171:         for d in devices {
172:             let kind = d.get("type").and_then(Value::as_str).unwrap_or_default();
173:             if !matches!(kind, "dashboard_lcd" | "pump_lcd" | "keyboard_lcd") {
174:                 report.error(format!(
175:                     "manifest.json: unknown supported_devices type {kind:?}"
176:                 ));
177:             }
178:         }
179:     }
180:     match manifest.get("interactive") {
181:         None | Some(Value::Bool(false)) => {}
182:         Some(Value::Bool(true)) => {
183:             let others = manifest
184:                 .get("supported_devices")
185:                 .and_then(Value::as_array)
186:                 .is_some_and(|ds| {
187:                     ds.iter()
188:                         .any(|d| d.get("type").and_then(Value::as_str) != Some("dashboard_lcd"))
189:                 });
190:             if others {
191:                 report.warn("\"interactive\": true only applies to dashboard_lcd; iCUE ignores it for pump and keyboard LCDs");
192:             }
193:         }
194:         Some(_) => report.error("manifest.json: \"interactive\" must be true or false"),
195:     }
196:     for plugin in strings(&manifest, "required_plugins", report) {
197:         if let Err(e) = plugin.parse::<PluginRef>() {
198:             report.error(format!("manifest.json: {e}"));
199:         }
200:     }
201:     for module in strings(&manifest, "modules", report) {
202:         check_local_file(dir, &module, "module", report);
203:     }
204: }
205: 
206: fn check_index(dir: &Path, html: &str, report: &mut Report) {
207:     let doc = Html::parse_document(html);
208:     if doc.select(&selector("head > title")).next().is_none() {
209:         report.warn("index.html has no <title>");
210:     }
211:     if doc
212:         .select(&selector(r#"link[rel~="icon"]"#))
213:         .next()
214:         .is_none()
215:     {
216:         report.warn("index.html has no <link rel=\"icon\">");
217:     }
218:     for tag in unclosed_void_elements_in_head(html) {
219:         report.warn(format!(
220:             "<{tag}> in <head> is not self-closed (\"/>\"); iCUE's importer rejects this, EdgeHost accepts it"
221:         ));
222:     }
223: 
224:     let mut mentions_icue_events = html.contains("icueEvents");
225:     for el in doc.select(&selector("[src], link[href]")) {
226:         let attr = if el.value().name() == "link" {
227:             "href"
228:         } else {
229:             "src"
230:         };
231:         let Some(reference) = el.value().attr(attr).and_then(local_reference) else {
232:             continue;
233:         };
234:         if let Some(common) = reference.strip_prefix("../common/") {
235:             report.warn(format!("{reference} needs iCUE's shared common/ folder (common/{common}); install it in EdgeHost's common folder"));
236:             continue;
237:         }
238:         if check_local_file(dir, reference, attr, report) && el.value().name() == "script" {
239:             mentions_icue_events |=
240:                 fs::read_to_string(dir.join(reference)).is_ok_and(|js| js.contains("icueEvents"));
241:         }
242:     }
243:     if !mentions_icue_events {
244:         report.warn(
245:             "index.html and its scripts never reference icueEvents; iCUE rejects such widgets",
246:         );
247:     }
248: }
249: 
250: /// Warn about `../` and absolute references and report missing files. Returns whether the file exists.
251: fn check_local_file(dir: &Path, reference: &str, what: &str, report: &mut Report) -> bool {
252:     let rel = Path::new(reference.trim_start_matches("./"));
253:     if rel.components().any(|c| {
254:         matches!(
255:             c,
256:             Component::ParentDir | Component::RootDir | Component::Prefix(_)
257:         )
258:     }) {
259:         report.error(format!(
260:             "{what} {reference:?} points outside the widget directory"
261:         ));
262:         return false;
263:     }
264:     if dir.join(rel).is_file() {
265:         true
266:     } else {
267:         report.error(format!("{what} {reference:?} does not exist"));
268:         false
269:     }
270: }
271: 
272: fn check_array(manifest: &Value, key: &str, report: &mut Report) {
273:     if manifest
274:         .get(key)
275:         .and_then(Value::as_array)
276:         .is_none_or(Vec::is_empty)
277:     {
278:         report.error(format!(
279:             "manifest.json: \"{key}\" must be a non-empty array"
280:         ));
281:     }
282: }
283: 
284: /// String items of an optional array key; reports non-string items.
285: fn strings(manifest: &Value, key: &str, report: &mut Report) -> Vec<String> {
286:     let Some(value) = manifest.get(key) else {
287:         return Vec::new();
288:     };
289:     let Some(items) = value.as_array() else {
290:         report.error(format!(
291:             "manifest.json: \"{key}\" must be an array of strings"
292:         ));
293:         return Vec::new();
294:     };
295:     items
296:         .iter()
297:         .filter_map(|v| match v.as_str() {
298:             Some(s) if !s.trim().is_empty() => Some(s.to_owned()),
299:             _ => {
300:                 report.error(format!(
301:                     "manifest.json: \"{key}\" entries must be non-empty strings"
302:                 ));
303:                 None
304:             }
305:         })
306:         .collect()
307: }
308: 
309: /// A reference into the widget's own files, without query or fragment; `None` for remote URLs.
310: fn local_reference(raw: &str) -> Option<&str> {
311:     let r = raw.trim();
312:     let is_remote = r.is_empty()
313:         || r.starts_with('#')
314:         || r.starts_with("//")
315:         || r.contains("://")
316:         || ["data:", "blob:", "mailto:", "javascript:"]
317:             .iter()
318:             .any(|p| r.starts_with(p));
319:     if is_remote {
320:         return None;
321:     }
322:     let end = r.find(['?', '#']).unwrap_or(r.len());
323:     r.get(..end).filter(|s| !s.is_empty())
324: }
325: 
326: fn is_version(v: &str, parts: std::ops::RangeInclusive<usize>) -> bool {
327:     let pieces: Vec<&str> = v.split('.').collect();
328:     parts.contains(&pieces.len())
329:         && pieces
330:             .iter()
331:             .all(|p| !p.is_empty() && p.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_digit()))
332: }
333: 
334: /// Void elements inside `<head>` written without a closing `/>`, by tag name.
335: fn unclosed_void_elements_in_head(html: &str) -> Vec<String> {
336:     let lower = html.to_ascii_lowercase();
337:     let (Some(start), Some(end)) = (lower.find("<head"), lower.find("</head>")) else {
338:         return Vec::new();
339:     };
340:     let Some(head) = html.get(start..end) else {
341:         return Vec::new();
342:     };
343:     head.split('<')
344:         .skip(1)
345:         .filter_map(|chunk| {
346:             let tag_end = chunk.find('>')?;
347:             let tag = chunk.get(..tag_end)?;
348:             let name: String = tag
349:                 .chars()
350:                 .take_while(char::is_ascii_alphanumeric)
351:                 .collect::<String>()
352:                 .to_ascii_lowercase();
353:             let void = matches!(
354:                 name.as_str(),
355:                 "meta" | "link" | "base" | "br" | "hr" | "img" | "input"
356:             );
357:             (void && !tag.trim_end().ends_with('/')).then_some(name)
358:         })
359:         .collect()
360: }
361: 
362: fn selector(css: &str) -> Selector {
363:     // Only called with the literal selectors in this module, which are valid CSS.
364:     Selector::parse(css).unwrap_or_else(|e| unreachable!("invalid built-in selector {css:?}: {e}"))
365: }
366: 
367: fn error_chain(e: &WidgetError) -> String {
368:     let mut out = e.to_string();
369:     let mut source = std::error::Error::source(e);
370:     while let Some(s) = source {
371:         out.push_str(": ");
372:         out.push_str(&s.to_string());
373:         source = s.source();
374:     }
375:     out
376: }
