- `start`: installs menus, reloads. OK.
- `reload`: loads layout, opens surface, shows page. OK.
- But: after `self.shown.clear(); self.surface = None;`, it creates new surface. The old surface is dropped. WebViews are dropped. Good.
- `show_page`: unmounts all, clears shown, loads each instance. Good.
- But note: if `surface.orientation() != self.layout.orientation`, it warns. The surface will use its own orientation for slot_rect. So instances laid out for a different orientation might fail to fit. The code just skips them with error. OK.
- `on_runtime`: handles Evaluated. Persists defaults, saves layout, then mounts. Good.
- But: when `on_runtime` receives an Evaluated event, it calls `surface.end_evaluation(instance)` only if surface exists. If surface was None (shouldn't happen in normal flow), it still persists to layout. OK.
- `poll_layout`: checks mtime, reloads if changed. OK.
- But there's a subtle bug: when `apply_evaluated` saves layout, `layout_mtime` is updated in `on_runtime`. But if the user edits layout.json externally at the exact same time, there might be a race. Not a real bug.

## apps/edgehost/src/menus.rs
- `icon`: creates 18x18 icon. OK.
- `Menus::install`: creates app menu and tray. OK.

## General observations

Now let me think about potential bugs that might not be caught by tests:

1. **In `surface.rs` `serve` function**: When serving from the common dir fallback, the MIME type is computed from the resolved path under the widget root, not the common dir. As I analyzed, this only affects extension-based MIME, which is the same. Not a bug.

2. **In `surface.rs` `serve` function**: When `read_asset(root, url_path)` returns 404, it tries common dir. But what if `read_asset(root, url_path)` returns `Escapes` or `BadEncoding`? The `match` only catches `Err(e) if e.status() == 404`. For other errors, it falls through to the response building and returns the error status. So a `/../x` request would get 403, not try common. That's correct.

3. **In `surface.rs` `serve` function**: For `/common/foo`, it strips prefix and tries common. But what if `read_asset(root, "/common/foo")` succeeds? Then it serves from root and doesn't try common. That's correct - widget's own files take precedence.

4. **In `proxy.rs` `is_allowed`**: The `host.ends_with(&format!(".{}", p.domain))` check. If `p.domain = "com"` and `host = "evil.com"`, `host.ends_with(".com")` is true. So declaring `domain: "com"` would allow any .com site. Is that a bug? It matches the documented behavior: "A permission matches its domain and any subdomain". `com` is technically a domain. So allowing all `.com` is expected if someone declares it. Not a bug.

5. **In `proxy.rs` `is_allowed`**: What about `p.domain = "example.com"` and `host = "example.com"`? Exact match. Good. What about `host = "sub.example.com"`? `ends_with(".example.com")` true. Good.

6. **In `proxy.rs` `target_url`**: It finds first `url=` prefix. But what if query has `url=...` inside another param value? e.g., `other=url=https://x`. The `strip_prefix("url=")` would match the `other=` value if it starts with `url=`. But `split('&')` splits on `&`, so `other=url=https...` wouldn't have a `url=` prefix at start of the segment. Actually it would: the segment is `other=url=https%3A%2F%2Fx`. `strip_prefix("url=")` returns None because it starts with `other=`. Good.

7. **In `install.rs` `extract_zip`**: The `entry.enclosed_name()` returns safe path within archive. But `zip::read::ZipFile::enclosed_name()` returns None for paths with `..`. Good.

8. **In `install.rs` `extract_zip`**: It uses `entry.name()` to get raw_name for error messages. Good.

9. **In `edge-store/src/install.rs` copy_tree**: There's a bug! Let me re-read:
```rust
fn copy_tree(from: &Path, to: &Path, budget: &mut Budget) -> Result<(), InstallError> {
    fs::create_dir_all(to).map_err(|e| io_err("creating", to, e))?;
    for entry in fs::read_dir(from).map_err(|e| io_err("listing", from, e))? {
        let entry = entry.map_err(|e| io_err("listing", from, e))?;
        let path = entry.path();
        if is_ignored(Path::new(&entry.file_name())) {
            continue;
        }
        ...
    }
```
This is fine.

10. **In `edge-store/src/install.rs` `copy_tree`**: For symlinks, it returns `InstallError::Unsafe(path.display().to_string())`. Good. But the budget entry is counted only after the symlink check. So symlinks don't count toward MAX_ENTRIES. Is that a bug? Maybe minor. A zip bomb could use many symlinks. But symlinks are rejected immediately, so not a real issue.

11. **In `edge-layout/src/display.rs` `DisplayChoice::from_str`**: It accepts empty string as Auto. The `Display` Display impl for Pick returns the raw string. For "auto" or "windowed" it returns those. The test checks round trip. Good.

12. **In `icuew-manifest/src/manifest.rs` `UrlPermission` serialization**: Only Serialize, not Deserialize. But that's fine, it's derived from `permissions` Vec<Value>.

13. **In `icuew-manifest/src/manifest.rs` `Manifest`**: Some fields are required (author, id, etc.). Good.

14. **In `icuew-manifest/src/head.rs` `HeadInfo::parse`**: The `title` selector is `head > title`. If title is not a direct child (e.g., inside another element in head), it won't find it. But spec says title is direct. OK.

15. **In `widget-runtime/src/shim.rs` `render`**: The `values` in Runtime mode is `Option<&'a BTreeMap<String, Value>>`. It's serialized as `values` in payload. If empty, it serializes as `{}`. OK.

16. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `build_view`**: The `with_url` is called with `icuew://<id><path>`. But the custom protocol is registered for `SCHEME` ("icuew") and is asynchronous. The URL is loaded after the webview is created. Good.

17. **In `apps/edgehost/src/main.rs` event loop**: Uses `ControlFlow::WaitUntil(next_poll)`. But it checks `if Instant::now() >= next_poll` at start of every event. If events arrive frequently, it might starve polling? Actually `WaitUntil` means the loop will wake up at `next_poll` even if no events. So polling is guaranteed. Good.

Wait, the comment says "A fixed deadline: re-arming 'now + POLL' on every event would starve the poll while events keep arriving". So using a fixed deadline is correct. But actually, if events keep arriving, `WaitUntil` still wakes up at the deadline. Good.

18. **In `apps/edgehost/src/app.rs` `poll_layout`**: It checks mtime != layout_mtime. If layout is saved internally (e.g., after evaluation), layout_mtime is updated. So internal saves don't trigger reload. Good. But if external edit happens between internal save and next poll, mtime might be equal? No, because the internal save set layout_mtime to the file's mtime at that time. If external edit happens later, mtime changes. Good.

19. **In `apps/edgehost/src/app.rs` `reload`**: It clears `surface = None` before creating new one. If `Surface::open` fails, the old surface is gone and the app has no surface. But reload is only called from start/reload menu/poll. If it fails, the app logs error and continues without surface. That's a degradation but not a crash. Could be considered a bug: old surface should be kept until new one succeeds. Let me check:

```rust
fn reload(&mut self, target: &EventLoopWindowTarget<AppEvent>) -> Result<()> {
    self.layout_mtime = self.layout_file_mtime();
    self.layout = self.store.load_layout().context("loading layout.json")?;
    ...
    self.shown.clear();
    self.surface = None;  // Old surface dropped here
    ...
    let surface = Surface::open(...)?;  // If this fails, no surface
    ...
    self.surface = Some(surface);
    ...
}
```

This is a real issue! If `Surface::open` fails (e.g., display disconnected), the app loses its current surface. But maybe acceptable for a dashboard app. Not necessarily a bug, but a robustness issue.

20. **In `widget-runtime/src/host.rs` `run_one`**: If `surface.evaluate` fails, the function returns early. The event loop isn't run. That's fine.

21. **In `widget-runtime/src/host.rs` `run_one`**: The `instance.id` is deterministic based on widget id and slot. If you run the same widget twice, you get the same instance id. This means WebKit data stores could collide. But for `run-one` development, maybe that's OK. Actually, the comment says "run one widget". Re-running same widget would use same data store. Is this a bug? Possibly if user expects isolated runs. But deterministic ID means persistence across runs, which might be intended.

22. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `Surface::open`**: `device_id` is computed as UUID v5 from a key. For windowed, key is "windowed". For display, key includes vendor/model/name. This means same display always gets same device_id. That's fine.

23. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `Surface::open`**: When edge_hardware and not native, it prints a warning. Good.

24. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `mount`**: It sets `view.zoom(self.fit.scale)`. But `fit.scale` is the same for all views. This means each widget is zoomed by the same factor. That's correct because they're all on the same surface.

25. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `slot_bounds`**: Uses `fit.scale` and `fit.offset` to convert panel pixels to logical points. Good.

26. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `build_view`**: The background view is created in `Surface::open`. It's a child of the window. But widget views are also children. WebViews are ordered by creation. Background first, then widgets on top. Good.

27. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `serve`**: The `EVAL_PATH` is `/__edgehost/eval.html`. But in `build_view`, the URL is `format!("{SCHEME}://{}{path}", id.simple())` where path is `EVAL_PATH`. So the request URI path will be `/__edgehost/eval.html`. The check `if uri.path() == proxy::ROUTE` is done first in the protocol handler. `proxy::ROUTE` is `/__edgehost/fetch`. So eval path doesn't hit proxy. Good.

28. **In `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `serve`**: There's a subtle issue. `serve` is called from within the async protocol closure. It takes `root: &Path` and `common: Option<&Path>`. The closure owns `serve_root: PathBuf` and `serve_common: Option<PathBuf>`. It passes references. Good.

29. **In `widget-runtime/src/proxy.rs` `handle`**: Spawns thread. The `responder` is Send. Good.

30. **In `widget-runtime/src/proxy.rs` `fetch_permitted`**: Uses `agent().get(&url).call()`. `ureq` 3.x API. Good.

31. **In `edge-store/src/install.rs` `unpack` for folder**: It doesn't check for symlinks inside subdirectories when copying? Wait, `copy_tree` recursively checks each entry for symlink and rejects. Good.

32. **In `edge-store/src/install.rs` `install`**: It uses `fs::rename(&root, &dest)`. If root and dest are on different filesystems, rename fails. But root is in `store.tmp_dir()` and dest is in `store.widgets_dir()`, both under `store.root`. Same filesystem. Good.

33. **In `edge-store/src/install.rs` `install`**: The `tmp` dir is created by `tempfile::Builder::new().prefix("install-").tempdir_in(store.tmp_dir())`. Good.

34. **In `edge-store/src/lib.rs` `Store::open`**: Creates widgets_dir and tmp_dir but not common_dir. That's intentional since common is optional.

35. **In `edge-store/src/layout.rs` `InstanceId`**: `FromStr` uses `Uuid::parse_str`. OK.

36. **In `edge-store/src/layout.rs` `Layout::resolve`**: Filters by simple UUID string prefix. But `needle` has dashes removed. What if query is "123e4567-89b" (with dashes)? It removes dashes, becomes "123e456789b", and matches simple UUID starting with that. Good. What if query is just "123"? Matches. Good.

37. **In `apps/edgectl/src/main.rs` `displays`**: It creates an EventLoop just to list displays. Fine.

38. **In `apps/edgectl/src/main.rs` `RunOne` command**: `--windowed` flag overrides `--display`. OK.

39. **In `apps/edgehost/src/main.rs`**: MenuEvent handler uses `event.id.0`. OK.

The closure passed to `with_asynchronous_custom_protocol` is `move`. Actually `with_asynchronous_custom_protocol` takes a closure. The closure is called on each request. It must be `'static`. All captured values are owned or `Arc`/`Rc`. Good.

The `Rc<dyn Fn(RuntimeEvent)>` is `'static` if the trait object itself is `'static`. The type alias `EventSink = Rc<dyn Fn(RuntimeEvent)>`. Is this implicitly `'static`? Yes, trait objects without lifetime are `'static`. Good.

Actually, there's another subtle issue: `resolve` might fail with BadEncoding if the URL has bad percent encoding. `read_asset` would have already failed with the same error. But in the success branch, `resolve(root, url_path)` is called again. If it fails, it falls back to "application/octet-stream". But `read_asset` succeeded, so `resolve` should succeed too. OK.

## Potential Bug: `widget-runtime/src/shim.rs` `Payload` serializes `fps_limit` as `fpsLimit` due to rename_all = "camelCase". Good.

```rust
instance_id: cfg.instance_id.hyphenated().to_string(),
```
Both are the same! iCUE's `widgetId` is usually the widget's reverse-DNS id, not the instance id. The shim might use `widget_id` for something. Let me check the shim JS to see what it expects.

1: // EdgeHost iCUE compatibility shim (milestone M1).
2: //
3: // Injected at document start, main frame only, before any widget script runs. It recreates the
4: // globals iCUE documents for HTML widgets (Widget API 1.9.0):
5: //   iCUE, device, uniqueId, one global per x-icue-property, tr(), window.plugins,
6: //   iCUE_initialized, plugin<Module>_initialized, and the icueEvents lifecycle calls.
7: // Plugins are not implemented yet (M3): their *_initialized flags stay false so widgets that
8: // guard on them degrade instead of throwing ReferenceError.
9: //
10: // The payload placeholder on the last line is replaced by Rust (widget_runtime::shim::render)
11: // with script-safe JSON; it must appear exactly once in this file.

28: 			return JSON.stringify(value);

38: 			post({ k: "console", level, msg: args.map(fmt).join(" ") });
39: 		};

42: 		post({ k: "console", level: "error", msg: `${e.message} (${e.filename}:${e.lineno}:${e.colno})` }),

160: 			}
161: 		}
162: 	};

## Assistant

_Thinking:_

Now I see the shim expects `P.widgetId` to be the widget id. But in `shim.rs` `render`, we set:
```rust
widget_id: cfg.instance_id.hyphenated().to_string(),
```
This is a bug! `widget_id` should be the widget's reverse-DNS id (from `def.manifest.id`), not the instance id. In the shim test, `widgetId: "w-1"` is the widget id. But in the Rust code, it's set to `cfg.instance_id`.

Let me verify. The `WidgetDef` has `manifest.id`. The `ShimConfig` doesn't have widget_id, only instance_id. So `render` should use `def.manifest.id.as_str()` for `widget_id`.

This is a real bug. Widgets that check `iCUE.widgetId` will get the wrong value (the instance UUID instead of their reverse-DNS id).

Let me also check `device_id`. `device_id` in ShimConfig is set to `self.device_id` in `surface.rs` `shim_config`. That's a UUID generated from display/windowed key. That's fine. The shim test has `deviceId: "d-1"`. OK.

So bug #1: `widget_id` in shim payload is set to instance_id instead of widget id.

Let me look for other bugs.

## Potential bug: `surface.rs` `serve` common fallback path

Actually I want to double-check the common fallback. In `serve`:
```rust
let result = match read_asset(root, url_path) {
    Err(e) if e.status() == 404 => match (common, url_path.strip_prefix("/common/")) {
        (Some(dir), Some(rest)) => read_asset(dir, rest).map_err(|_| e),
        _ => Err(e),
    },
    other => other,
};
```

If `read_asset(root, "/common/foo.js")` returns 404, it tries `read_asset(common_dir, "foo.js")`. But what if the widget has a `common` directory with its own file? `read_asset(root, "/common/foo.js")` would succeed and serve from widget, not fallback. Good.

But wait: `url_path.strip_prefix("/common/")` strips one leading slash? `strip_prefix` for `&str` takes a string prefix. So `/common/foo.js`.strip_prefix("/common/") = Some("foo.js"). Good.

## Potential bug: `install.rs` `unpack` directory source

When source is a directory, `copy_tree(source, dest, budget)` copies the contents of source into dest. Then it checks if `dest.join("manifest.json").is_file()`. If yes, returns dest. If not, looks for single top-level folder. But when copying a directory, the contents are copied directly into dest. So if source is `hello-edge/`, dest will have `manifest.json` at top level. Good.

## Potential bug: `install.rs` `copy_tree` and `is_ignored`

When `is_ignored(Path::new(&entry.file_name()))` is called, `entry.file_name()` is e.g. `.DS_Store`. Path::new(".DS_Store").components() gives Normal(".DS_Store"). So ignored. Good.

## Potential bug: `edge-store` `latest` uses `max_by`

`max_by` returns the maximum according to comparator. compare_versions returns Ordering. Good.

## Potential bug: `edge-layout` `DisplayChoice::from_str` and Display impl

For `Pick`, `Display` returns the original string. For "auto" or "windowed" it returns those. But what if user parses "AUTO" (uppercase)? It converts to lowercase and matches "auto", so `from_str` returns `DisplayChoice::Auto`. Then `to_string()` returns "auto", not "AUTO". The test checks round-trip for lowercase. So "AUTO" doesn't round-trip. Is that a bug? It's expected behavior (normalization). Not a bug.

## Potential bug: `widget-runtime/src/surface.rs` `Surface::open` `device_key`

For display, key is `format!("display:{:?}:{:?}:{}", d.vendor, d.model, d.name)`. `{:?}` for Option<u32> includes "Some(1234)". So the key string includes things like `display:Some(3736):Some(60672):Monitor #60672`. That's fine, just deterministic.

## Potential bug: `app.rs` `reload` drops surface before creating new one

## Potential bug: `app.rs` `poll_layout` can reload while evaluation is in progress

If layout changes during evaluation, `reload` is called, which drops surface, clears shown, creates new surface, starts new evaluations. The old `Evaluated` events might still arrive via the event proxy and be handled by `on_runtime`. `on_runtime` will try to find the instance in the new layout. If the instance id is the same (because layout reloaded with same instance), it might apply defaults. But the widget def might be different. This is a race, but the app is designed to reload infrequently. Not a critical bug.

## Potential bug: `edge-store/src/install.rs` `install` uses `report.widget.take()`

After `validate_dir` returns, if no errors, `report.widget` should be Some. But what if validation had no errors but WidgetDef::load still failed? Actually, `validate_dir` sets `report.widget` only if `WidgetDef::load` succeeds. If it fails, `report.error(error_chain(&e))` is called, so `has_errors()` is true, and `install` returns Invalid before reaching `report.widget.take()`. So `report.widget.take()` returns None only if validation passed but somehow no widget was set, which shouldn't happen. Not a bug.

OK, let me also consider if there are any issues with the `ureq` version 3 API. The code uses:
- `ureq::Agent::config_builder()` - yes in ureq 3.
- `.tls_config(tls)` - yes.
- `.timeout_global(Some(TIMEOUT))` - yes.
- `.http_status_as_error(false)` - yes.
- `.user_agent(USER_AGENT)` - yes.
- `.build().new_agent()` - yes.
- `agent().get(&url).call()` - yes.
- `resp.status().as_u16()` - yes.
- `resp.headers().get(...)` - yes.
- `resp.body_mut().with_config().limit(MAX_BODY_BYTES).read_to_vec()` - yes.

So ureq API usage is correct.

Let me think about the `surface.rs` `serve` function more. The `serve` function is called from the async protocol closure. It uses `eprintln!` for errors. That's fine.

One thing: `serve` uses `read_asset(root, url_path)`. If `url_path` is empty (which shouldn't happen because the URL always has a path), `resolve` would push `index.html`. But `read_asset` is only called with non-empty paths from the protocol handler (because request URI always has at least `/`). OK.

Another thing: in `serve`, when building the response, it always sets `CACHE_CONTROL: no-store`. But for the common fallback from a real file, it still sets no-store. OK.

## Potential bug: `surface.rs` `build_view` `with_url` creates URL with `id.simple()` as host. But the custom protocol is scheme-based, not host-based. wry's async protocol for a scheme handles all hosts with that scheme. So `icuew://anything/` goes to the same protocol handler. But the closure uses `instance.id` for IPC and root. The host part doesn't matter for routing, but it makes each widget's origin different. Good.

## Potential bug: `surface.rs` `serve` doesn't check that URL host matches instance

It doesn't need to; the origin isolation is handled by WebKit based on the URL. But if a widget somehow requests `icuew://other-instance/secret.js`, the protocol handler would serve from the current instance's root. This is actually a security issue! Because the protocol handler is registered per webview, but it only knows the current instance's root. If a widget makes a cross-origin request to `icuew://other-instance/...`, the same handler would serve it from the current widget's files. But WebKit's same-origin policy would prevent a widget from reading `icuew://other-instance/` because it's a different origin. However, the protocol handler itself doesn't validate the host. If there's a way to bypass SOP, this could leak files.

But within the same webview, you can't request a different origin. And each widget has its own webview with its own protocol handler closure. The closure for widget A serves A's root. A request from widget A to `icuew://B/` would be handled by A's closure, serving A's files. But B's webview has a different closure. So the origin `icuew://B` in widget A's webview doesn't actually reach B's handler; it reaches A's handler. WebKit would treat `icuew://A` and `icuew://B` as different origins, so the request would be blocked by SOP. Unless... the widget uses `fetch` with `no-cors` mode? Then the response is opaque. So no leak.

Still, it's a bit fragile. A more robust design would validate the host in `serve` and only serve if it matches the instance. But this isn't a current bug per se.

Let me fix the uninstall ordering. The current code:
```rust
fn uninstall(store: &Store, id: &WidgetId, version: Option<&str>, force: bool) -> Result<()> {
    let mut layout = store.load_layout()?;
    let users: Vec<_> = layout
        .instances()
        .filter(|(_, i)| &i.widget == id && version.is_none_or(|v| v == i.version))
        .map(|(_, i)| i.id)
        .collect();
    if !users.is_empty() && !force {
        bail!(
            "{} layout instance(s) use {id}; remove them first or pass --force",
            users.len()
        );
    }
    for instance in users {
        layout.remove(instance)?;
    }
    store.save_layout(&layout)?;
    for v in store.uninstall(id, version)? {
        println!("removed {id} {v}");
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

Better:
```rust
fn uninstall(store: &Store, id: &WidgetId, version: Option<&str>, force: bool) -> Result<()> {
    let mut layout = store.load_layout()?;
    let users: Vec<_> = layout
        .instances()
        .filter(|(_, i)| &i.widget == id && version.is_none_or(|v| v == i.version))
        .map(|(_, i)| i.id)
        .collect();
    if !users.is_empty() && !force {
        bail!(
            "{} layout instance(s) use {id}; remove them first or pass --force",
            users.len()
        );
    }
    // Remove from disk first; only update the layout if that succeeds.
    let removed = store.uninstall(id, version)?;
    for instance in users {
        layout.remove(instance)?;
    }
    store.save_layout(&layout)?;
    for v in removed {
        println!("removed {id} {v}");
    }
    Ok(())
}
```

Wait, but if `store.uninstall` succeeds but `layout.remove` or `store.save_layout` fails, then the widget is removed from disk but the layout still references it. That's also inconsistent. The current code saves layout first, then uninstalls. My proposed change reverses the risk.

Actually, the cleanest approach is to do all mutations in a transaction-like way. But there's no transaction. The question is: which failure mode is worse?

Current: layout saved without references, but widget still on disk. Next reload will show empty slot. User can retry uninstall.

Proposed: widget removed from disk, but layout still references it. Next reload will try to load missing widget and skip with error.

I think the current behavior is actually safer: if uninstall fails, at least the layout no longer references the widget. But the layout is saved even if uninstall fails, which means references are gone but files remain.

Hmm, but the user gets an error and might retry. If they retry, the layout already has no references, so `users` will be empty, and `store.uninstall` will run. It will succeed (or fail again). If it succeeds this time, files are removed. If it fails, files remain. So eventually it works.

If we change to uninstall first then save layout, and uninstall fails, layout still references widget. User retries, same check, same failure. No progress.

If uninstall succeeds but save_layout fails (rare), files are gone but layout references remain. User reload sees missing widget. They can manually remove reference.

So current behavior: references removed first. This is arguably better because the common failure is uninstall failing due to permissions, and removing references allows retry.

Actually, I think the current ordering is intentional and reasonable. So it's not a bug. I'll leave it.

1: // Stock Portfolio widget (iCUE Widget API 1.x).
2: //
3: // Quotes come from Yahoo Finance's chart API. Under EdgeHost, fetch() to the domain declared in
4: // manifest.json is performed by the host, so the API's missing CORS headers do not matter.
5: //
6: // State (holdings edited on the widget, selected period) is kept in localStorage under uniqueId.
7: // The "holdings" and "period" settings seed it; changing those settings re-seeds it.
8: 
9: icueEvents = {
10: 	onICUEInitialized: init,
11: 	onDataUpdated: onSettingsChanged,
12: };
13: 
14: const PERIODS = [
15: 	["1d", "1D", "5m"],
16: 	["5d", "5D", "30m"],
17: 	["1mo", "1M", "1d"],
18: 	["6mo", "6M", "1d"],
19: 	["ytd", "YTD", "1d"],
20: 	["1y", "1Y", "1d"],
21: ];
22: const INTERVAL = Object.fromEntries(PERIODS.map(([key, , interval]) => [key, interval]));
23: const TICKER = /^[A-Z][A-Z0-9.\-]{0,9}$/;
24: const MAX_HOLDINGS = 40;
25: 
26: const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);
27: const usd = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { style: "currency", currency: "USD" });
28: const sharesFmt = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { maximumFractionDigits: 4 });
29: const signedUsd = (v) => `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${usd.format(Math.abs(v))}`;
30: const signedPct = (v) => (Number.isFinite(v) ? `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${Math.abs(v * 100).toFixed(2)}%` : "");
31: const trendClass = (v) => (v > 0 ? "up" : v < 0 ? "down" : "flat");
32: const setting = (name, fallback) => (typeof window[name] === "undefined" || window[name] === null ? fallback : window[name]);

34: let state = null;
35: let quotes = {};
36: let timer = null;
37: let requestSeq = 0;

39: // ---------- state ----------

41: function parseHoldings(text) {
42: 	const out = [];
43: 	for (const entry of String(text || "").split(/[,;\n]+/)) {
44: 		const [sym, qty] = entry.trim().split(/[\s:=]+/);
45: 		const symbol = (sym || "").toUpperCase();
46: 		const shares = Number(qty);
47: 		if (TICKER.test(symbol) && Number.isFinite(shares) && shares > 0) out.push({ symbol, shares });
48: 	}
49: 	return out.slice(0, MAX_HOLDINGS);
50: }
51: 
52: function loadStored() {
53: 	try {
54: 		return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(uniqueId) || "null");
55: 	} catch (_) {
56: 		return null;
57: 	}
58: }
59: 
60: function save() {
61: 	try {
62: 		localStorage.setItem(uniqueId, JSON.stringify(state));
63: 	} catch (e) {
64: 		console.warn("could not save portfolio", e);
65: 	}
66: }
67: 
68: function seededState() {
69: 	const seed = String(setting("holdings", ""));
70: 	const seedPeriod = INTERVAL[setting("period", "1d")] ? setting("period", "1d") : "1d";
71: 	const stored = loadStored();
72: 	const fresh = { seed, seedPeriod, holdings: parseHoldings(seed), period: seedPeriod };
73: 	if (!stored || !Array.isArray(stored.holdings)) return fresh;
74: 	// Settings changed since the widget last saw them: they win.
75: 	if (stored.seed !== seed) return { ...fresh, period: stored.period };
76: 	if (stored.seedPeriod !== seedPeriod) return { ...stored, seedPeriod, period: seedPeriod };
77: 	return stored;
78: }

83: 	const url =
84: 		`https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/${encodeURIComponent(symbol)}` +
85: 		`?range=${range}&interval=${INTERVAL[range]}&includePrePost=false`;
86: 	const response = await fetch(url);
87: 	let body = null;

96: 		throw new Error((err && err.description) || `HTTP ${response.status}`);
97: 	}
98: 	const meta = result.meta || {};
99: 	const quote = (result.indicators && result.indicators.quote && result.indicators.quote[0]) || {};
100: 	const closes = quote.close || [];
101: 	const points = (result.timestamp || []).map((t, i) => [t, closes[i]]).filter((p) => Number.isFinite(p[1]));
102: 	const last = points.length ? points[points.length - 1][1] : NaN;
103: 	const price = Number.isFinite(meta.regularMarketPrice) ? meta.regularMarketPrice : last;

106: 	if (!Number.isFinite(base)) base = points.length ? points[0][1] : price;
107: 	if (!Number.isFinite(price)) throw new Error("no price");
108: 	return { symbol, price, base, time: meta.regularMarketTime, points, currency: meta.currency || "USD" };

112: 	if (!state || state.holdings.length === 0) {
113: 		quotes = {};
114: 		render();
115: 		return;
116: 	}
117: 	const seq = ++requestSeq;
118: 	setStatus("Updating…");
119: 	const range = state.period;
120: 	const results = await Promise.allSettled(state.holdings.map((h) => fetchSeries(h.symbol, range)));
121: 	if (seq !== requestSeq) return; // a newer refresh (e.g. period change) superseded this one
122: 	quotes = {};
123: 	results.forEach((r, i) => {
124: 		const symbol = state.holdings[i].symbol;
125: 		quotes[symbol] = r.status === "fulfilled" ? r.value : { symbol, error: String((r.reason && r.reason.message) || r.reason) };
126: 	});
127: 	const failed = Object.values(quotes).filter((q) => q.error);
128: 	console.log(`portfolio ${range}: ${state.holdings.length - failed.length}/${state.holdings.length} quotes` + (failed.length ? `; failed: ${failed.map((q) => `${q.symbol} (${q.error})`).join(", ")}` : ""));
129: 	render();
130: }
131: 
132: function rowsWithQuotes() {
133: 	return state.holdings
134: 		.map((h) => {
135: 			const q = quotes[h.symbol];
136: 			if (!q || q.error) return { ...h, q, value: NaN, change: NaN };
137: 			const value = h.shares * q.price;
138: 			const change = h.shares * (q.price - q.base);
139: 			return { ...h, q, value, change, pct: q.base ? q.price / q.base - 1 : NaN };
140: 		})
141: 		.sort((a, b) => (Number.isFinite(b.value) ? b.value : -1) - (Number.isFinite(a.value) ? a.value : -1));
142: }
143: 
144: // Portfolio value over the period: at each timestamp, shares × last known close (base before the
145: // first bar), starting from the period's base value and ending at the current value.
146: function portfolioSeries(rows) {
147: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => r.q && !r.q.error);
148: 	if (ok.length === 0) return [];
149: 	const times = [...new Set(ok.flatMap((r) => r.q.points.map((p) => p[0])))].sort((a, b) => a - b);
150: 	const cursor = ok.map(() => 0);
151: 	const last = ok.map((r) => r.q.base);
152: 	const series = [ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.shares * r.q.base, 0)];
153: 	for (const t of times) {
154: 		let total = 0;
155: 		ok.forEach((r, k) => {
156: 			const pts = r.q.points;
157: 			while (cursor[k] < pts.length && pts[cursor[k]][0] <= t) {
158: 				last[k] = pts[cursor[k]][1];
159: 				cursor[k] += 1;
160: 			}
161: 			total += r.shares * last[k];
162: 		});
163: 		series.push(total);
164: 	}
165: 	series.push(ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0));
166: 	return series;
167: }
168: 
169: // ---------- rendering ----------
170: 
171: function setStatus(text) {
172: 	$("status").textContent = text;
173: }
174: 
175: function applyTheme() {
176: 	const s = document.documentElement.style;
177: 	s.setProperty("--fg", setting("textColor", "#F2F4F8"));
178: 	s.setProperty("--accent", setting("accentColor", "#FFD200"));
179: 	s.setProperty("--bg", setting("backgroundColor", "#12151C"));
180: 	s.setProperty("--up", setting("upColor", "#2ECC71"));
181: 	s.setProperty("--down", setting("downColor", "#FF5C5C"));
182: }
183: 
184: function renderPeriods() {
185: 	const nav = $("periods");
186: 	nav.replaceChildren(
187: 		...PERIODS.map(([key, label]) => {
188: 			const b = document.createElement("button");
189: 			b.type = "button";
190: 			b.textContent = label;
191: 			b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", String(key === state.period));
192: 			b.addEventListener("click", () => {
193: 				if (state.period === key) return;
194: 				state.period = key;
195: 				save();
196: 				renderPeriods();
197: 				refresh();
198: 			});
199: 			return b;
200: 		}),
201: 	);
202: }
203: 
204: function renderChart(series, trend) {
205: 	const line = $("line");
206: 	const area = $("area");
207: 	const baseline = $("baseline");
208: 	document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--trend", trend < 0 ? "var(--down)" : "var(--up)");
209: 	if (series.length < 2) {
210: 		line.setAttribute("points", "");
211: 		area.setAttribute("d", "");
212: 		return;
213: 	}
214: 	const min = Math.min(...series);
215: 	const max = Math.max(...series);
216: 	const span = max - min || 1;
217: 	const x = (i) => (i / (series.length - 1)) * 1000;
218: 	const y = (v) => 290 - ((v - min) / span) * 280;
219: 	const pts = series.map((v, i) => `${x(i).toFixed(1)},${y(v).toFixed(1)}`);
220: 	line.setAttribute("points", pts.join(" "));
221: 	area.setAttribute("d", `M0,300 L${pts.join(" L")} L1000,300 Z`);
222: 	const by = y(series[0]).toFixed(1);
223: 	baseline.setAttribute("y1", by);
224: 	baseline.setAttribute("y2", by);
225: }
226: 
227: function cell(text, className) {
228: 	const td = document.createElement("td");
229: 	td.textContent = text;
230: 	if (className) td.className = className;
231: 	return td;
232: }
233: 
234: function render() {
235: 	const rows = rowsWithQuotes();
236: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => Number.isFinite(r.value));
237: 	const total = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0);
238: 	const change = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.change, 0);
239: 	const start = total - change;
240: 	const pct = start ? change / start : NaN;
241: 	const periodLabel = (PERIODS.find(([k]) => k === state.period) || [, ""])[1];
242: 
243: 	$("total").textContent = ok.length ? usd.format(total) : "—";
244: 	const ch = $("change");
245: 	ch.textContent = ok.length ? `${signedUsd(change)} (${signedPct(pct)}) · ${periodLabel}` : state.holdings.length ? "No quotes" : "Tap Edit to add holdings";
246: 	ch.className = ok.length ? trendClass(change) : "flat";
247: 
248: 	$("rows").replaceChildren(
249: 		...rows.map((r) => {
250: 			const tr = document.createElement("tr");
251: 			tr.append(cell(r.symbol, "sym"), cell(sharesFmt.format(r.shares), "num"));
252: 			if (r.q && r.q.error) {
253: 				const err = cell(r.q.error, "err");
254: 				err.colSpan = 3;
255: 				tr.append(err);
256: 			} else if (!r.q) {
257: 				tr.append(cell("…", "num"), cell("", "num"), cell("", "num"));
258: 			} else {
259: 				tr.append(
260: 					cell(usd.format(r.q.price), "num"),
261: 					cell(usd.format(r.value), "num"),
262: 					cell(`${signedUsd(r.change)}  ${signedPct(r.pct)}`, `num ${trendClass(r.change)}`),
263: 				);
264: 			}
265: 			return tr;
266: 		}),
267: 	);
268: 
269: 	renderChart(portfolioSeries(rows), change);
270: 
271: 	const latest = Math.max(0, ...ok.map((r) => r.q.time || 0));
272: 	const failed = rows.length - ok.length;
273: 	const asOf = latest
274: 		? new Date(latest * 1000).toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York", month: "short", day: "numeric", hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit" }) + " ET"
275: 		: "";
276: 	setStatus([asOf && `As of ${asOf}`, failed ? `${failed} ticker(s) failed` : "", "Yahoo Finance"].filter(Boolean).join(" · "));
277: }
278: 
279: // ---------- editor ----------
280: 
281: function editorRow(symbol = "", shares = "") {
282: 	const row = document.createElement("div");
283: 	row.className = "editor-row";
284: 	const t = document.createElement("input");
285: 	t.className = "ticker";
286: 	t.placeholder = "Ticker";
287: 	t.value = symbol;
288: 	t.autocapitalize = "characters";
289: 	t.spellcheck = false;
290: 	const s = document.createElement("input");
291: 	s.className = "shares";
292: 	s.placeholder = "Shares";
293: 	s.inputMode = "decimal";
294: 	s.value = shares === "" ? "" : String(shares);
295: 	const del = document.createElement("button");
296: 	del.type = "button";
297: 	del.textContent = "✕";
298: 	del.title = "Remove";
299: 	del.addEventListener("click", () => row.remove());
300: 	row.append(t, s, del);
301: 	return row;
302: }
303: 
304: function openEditor(holdings) {
305: 	$("editor-error").textContent = "";
306: 	$("editor-rows").replaceChildren(...holdings.map((h) => editorRow(h.symbol, h.shares)), editorRow());
307: 	$("editor").hidden = false;
308: }
309: 
310: function readEditor() {
311: 	const holdings = [];
312: 	const problems = [];
313: 	const seen = new Set();
314: 	for (const row of $("editor-rows").querySelectorAll(".editor-row")) {
315: 		const symbol = row.querySelector(".ticker").value.trim().toUpperCase();
316: 		const sharesText = row.querySelector(".shares").value.trim().replace(/,/g, "");
317: 		if (!symbol && !sharesText) continue;
318: 		const shares = Number(sharesText);
319: 		if (!TICKER.test(symbol)) problems.push(`“${symbol || "?"}” is not a ticker`);
320: 		else if (!Number.isFinite(shares) || shares <= 0) problems.push(`${symbol}: shares must be a positive number`);
321: 		else if (seen.has(symbol)) problems.push(`${symbol} is listed twice`);
322: 		else {
323: 			seen.add(symbol);
324: 			holdings.push({ symbol, shares });
325: 		}
326: 	}
327: 	if (holdings.length > MAX_HOLDINGS) problems.push(`at most ${MAX_HOLDINGS} holdings`);
328: 	return { holdings, problems };
329: }
330: 
331: function wireEditor() {
332: 	$("edit").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(state.holdings));
333: 	$("add-row").addEventListener("click", () => {
334: 		const row = editorRow();
335: 		$("editor-rows").append(row);
336: 		row.querySelector("input").focus();
337: 	});
338: 	$("reset").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(parseHoldings(setting("holdings", ""))));
339: 	$("cancel").addEventListener("click", () => ($("editor").hidden = true));
340: 	$("save").addEventListener("click", () => {
341: 		const { holdings, problems } = readEditor();
342: 		if (problems.length) {
343: 			$("editor-error").textContent = problems.join(" · ");
344: 			return;
345: 		}
346: 		state.holdings = holdings;
347: 		save();
348: 		$("editor").hidden = true;
349: 		quotes = {};
350: 		render();
351: 		refresh();
352: 	});
353: }
354: 
355: // ---------- lifecycle ----------
356: 
357: function restartTimer() {
358: 	clearInterval(timer);
359: 	const seconds = Math.max(15, Number(setting("refreshSeconds", 60)) || 60);
360: 	timer = setInterval(refresh, seconds * 1000);
361: }
362: 
363: let initialized = false;
364: 
365: function init() {
366: 	if (initialized) return;
367: 	initialized = true;
368: 	applyTheme();
369: 	state = seededState();
370: 	save();
371: 	wireEditor();
372: 	renderPeriods();
373: 	render();
374: 	refresh();
375: 	restartTimer();
376: }
377: 
378: function onSettingsChanged() {
379: 	if (!initialized) return;
380: 	applyTheme();
381: 	const next = seededState();
382: 	const changed = next.seed !== state.seed || next.period !== state.period;
383: 	state = next;
384: 	save();
385: 	renderPeriods();
386: 	restartTimer();
387: 	if (changed) refresh();
388: 	else render();
389: }
390: 
391: if (typeof iCUE_initialized !== "undefined" && iCUE_initialized) {
392: 	init();
393: }

2: //
3: // Quotes come from Yahoo Finance's chart API. Under EdgeHost, fetch() to the domain declared in
4: // manifest.json is performed by the host, so the API's missing CORS headers do not matter.
5: //
6: // State (holdings edited on the widget, selected period) is kept in localStorage under uniqueId.
7: // The "holdings" and "period" settings seed it; changing those settings re-seeds it.
8: 
9: icueEvents = {
10: 	onICUEInitialized: init,
11: 	onDataUpdated: onSettingsChanged,
12: };
13: 
14: const PERIODS = [
15: 	["1d", "1D", "5m"],
16: 	["5d", "5D", "30m"],
17: 	["1mo", "1M", "1d"],
18: 	["6mo", "6M", "1d"],
19: 	["ytd", "YTD", "1d"],
20: 	["1y", "1Y", "1d"],
21: ];
22: const INTERVAL = Object.fromEntries(PERIODS.map(([key, , interval]) => [key, interval]));
23: const TICKER = /^[A-Z][A-Z0-9.\-]{0,9}$/;
24: const MAX_HOLDINGS = 40;
25: 
26: const $ = (id) => document.getElementById(id);
27: const usd = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { style: "currency", currency: "USD" });
28: const sharesFmt = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { maximumFractionDigits: 4 });
29: const signedUsd = (v) => `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${usd.format(Math.abs(v))}`;
30: const signedPct = (v) => (Number.isFinite(v) ? `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${Math.abs(v * 100).toFixed(2)}%` : "");
31: const trendClass = (v) => (v > 0 ? "up" : v < 0 ? "down" : "flat");
32: const setting = (name, fallback) => (typeof window[name] === "undefined" || window[name] === null ? fallback : window[name]);
33: 
34: let state = null;
35: let quotes = {};
36: let timer = null;
37: let requestSeq = 0;
38: 
39: // ---------- state ----------
40: 
41: function parseHoldings(text) {
42: 	const out = [];
43: 	for (const entry of String(text || "").split(/[,;\n]+/)) {
44: 		const [sym, qty] = entry.trim().split(/[\s:=]+/);
45: 		const symbol = (sym || "").toUpperCase();
46: 		const shares = Number(qty);
47: 		if (TICKER.test(symbol) && Number.isFinite(shares) && shares > 0) out.push({ symbol, shares });
48: 	}
49: 	return out.slice(0, MAX_HOLDINGS);
50: }
51: 
52: function loadStored() {
53: 	try {
54: 		return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(uniqueId) || "null");
55: 	} catch (_) {
56: 		return null;
57: 	}
58: }
59: 
60: function save() {
61: 	try {
62: 		localStorage.setItem(uniqueId, JSON.stringify(state));
63: 	} catch (e) {
64: 		console.warn("could not save portfolio", e);
65: 	}
66: }
67: 
68: function seededState() {
69: 	const seed = String(setting("holdings", ""));
70: 	const seedPeriod = INTERVAL[setting("period", "1d")] ? setting("period", "1d") : "1d";
71: 	const stored = loadStored();
72: 	const fresh = { seed, seedPeriod, holdings: parseHoldings(seed), period: seedPeriod };
73: 	if (!stored || !Array.isArray(stored.holdings)) return fresh;
74: 	// Settings changed since the widget last saw them: they win.
75: 	if (stored.seed !== seed) return { ...fresh, period: stored.period };
76: 	if (stored.seedPeriod !== seedPeriod) return { ...stored, seedPeriod, period: seedPeriod };
77: 	return stored;
78: }
79: 
80: // ---------- data ----------
81: 
82: async function fetchSeries(symbol, range) {
83: 	const url =
84: 		`https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/${encodeURIComponent(symbol)}` +
85: 		`?range=${range}&interval=${INTERVAL[range]}&includePrePost=false`;
86: 	const response = await fetch(url);
87: 	let body = null;
88: 	try {
89: 		body = await response.json();
90: 	} catch (_) {
91: 		throw new Error(`HTTP ${response.status}`);
92: 	}
93: 	const result = body && body.chart && body.chart.result && body.chart.result[0];
94: 	if (!result) {
95: 		const err = body && body.chart && body.chart.error;
96: 		throw new Error((err && err.description) || `HTTP ${response.status}`);
97: 	}
98: 	const meta = result.meta || {};
99: 	const quote = (result.indicators && result.indicators.quote && result.indicators.quote[0]) || {};
100: 	const closes = quote.close || [];
101: 	const points = (result.timestamp || []).map((t, i) => [t, closes[i]]).filter((p) => Number.isFinite(p[1]));
102: 	const last = points.length ? points[points.length - 1][1] : NaN;
103: 	const price = Number.isFinite(meta.regularMarketPrice) ? meta.regularMarketPrice : last;
104: 	let base = meta.chartPreviousClose;
105: 	if (range === "1d" && Number.isFinite(meta.previousClose)) base = meta.previousClose;
106: 	if (!Number.isFinite(base)) base = points.length ? points[0][1] : price;
107: 	if (!Number.isFinite(price)) throw new Error("no price");
108: 	return { symbol, price, base, time: meta.regularMarketTime, points, currency: meta.currency || "USD" };
109: }
110: 
111: async function refresh() {
112: 	if (!state || state.holdings.length === 0) {
113: 		quotes = {};
114: 		render();
115: 		return;
116: 	}
117: 	const seq = ++requestSeq;
118: 	setStatus("Updating…");
119: 	const range = state.period;
120: 	const results = await Promise.allSettled(state.holdings.map((h) => fetchSeries(h.symbol, range)));
121: 	if (seq !== requestSeq) return; // a newer refresh (e.g. period change) superseded this one
122: 	quotes = {};
123: 	results.forEach((r, i) => {
124: 		const symbol = state.holdings[i].symbol;
125: 		quotes[symbol] = r.status === "fulfilled" ? r.value : { symbol, error: String((r.reason && r.reason.message) || r.reason) };
126: 	});
127: 	const failed = Object.values(quotes).filter((q) => q.error);
128: 	console.log(`portfolio ${range}: ${state.holdings.length - failed.length}/${state.holdings.length} quotes` + (failed.length ? `; failed: ${failed.map((q) => `${q.symbol} (${q.error})`).join(", ")}` : ""));
129: 	render();
130: }
131: 
132: function rowsWithQuotes() {
133: 	return state.holdings
134: 		.map((h) => {
135: 			const q = quotes[h.symbol];
136: 			if (!q || q.error) return { ...h, q, value: NaN, change: NaN };
137: 			const value = h.shares * q.price;
138: 			const change = h.shares * (q.price - q.base);
139: 			return { ...h, q, value, change, pct: q.base ? q.price / q.base - 1 : NaN };
140: 		})
141: 		.sort((a, b) => (Number.isFinite(b.value) ? b.value : -1) - (Number.isFinite(a.value) ? a.value : -1));
142: }
143: 
144: // Portfolio value over the period: at each timestamp, shares × last known close (base before the
145: // first bar), starting from the period's base value and ending at the current value.
146: function portfolioSeries(rows) {
147: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => r.q && !r.q.error);
148: 	if (ok.length === 0) return [];
149: 	const times = [...new Set(ok.flatMap((r) => r.q.points.map((p) => p[0])))].sort((a, b) => a - b);
150: 	const cursor = ok.map(() => 0);
151: 	const last = ok.map((r) => r.q.base);
152: 	const series = [ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.shares * r.q.base, 0)];
153: 	for (const t of times) {
154: 		let total = 0;
155: 		ok.forEach((r, k) => {
156: 			const pts = r.q.points;
157: 			while (cursor[k] < pts.length && pts[cursor[k]][0] <= t) {
158: 				last[k] = pts[cursor[k]][1];
159: 				cursor[k] += 1;
160: 			}
161: 			total += r.shares * last[k];
162: 		});
163: 		series.push(total);
164: 	}
165: 	series.push(ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0));
166: 	return series;
167: }
168: 
169: // ---------- rendering ----------
170: 
171: function setStatus(text) {
172: 	$("status").textContent = text;
173: }
174: 
175: function applyTheme() {
176: 	const s = document.documentElement.style;
177: 	s.setProperty("--fg", setting("textColor", "#F2F4F8"));
178: 	s.setProperty("--accent", setting("accentColor", "#FFD200"));
179: 	s.setProperty("--bg", setting("backgroundColor", "#12151C"));
180: 	s.setProperty("--up", setting("upColor", "#2ECC71"));
181: 	s.setProperty("--down", setting("downColor", "#FF5C5C"));
182: }
183: 
184: function renderPeriods() {
185: 	const nav = $("periods");
186: 	nav.replaceChildren(
187: 		...PERIODS.map(([key, label]) => {
188: 			const b = document.createElement("button");
189: 			b.type = "button";
190: 			b.textContent = label;
191: 			b.setAttribute("aria-pressed", String(key === state.period));
192: 			b.addEventListener("click", () => {
193: 				if (state.period === key) return;
194: 				state.period = key;
195: 				save();
196: 				renderPeriods();
197: 				refresh();
198: 			});
199: 			return b;
200: 		}),
201: 	);
202: }
203: 
204: function renderChart(series, trend) {
205: 	const line = $("line");
206: 	const area = $("area");
207: 	const baseline = $("baseline");
208: 	document.documentElement.style.setProperty("--trend", trend < 0 ? "var(--down)" : "var(--up)");
209: 	if (series.length < 2) {
210: 		line.setAttribute("points", "");
211: 		area.setAttribute("d", "");
212: 		return;
213: 	}
214: 	const min = Math.min(...series);
215: 	const max = Math.max(...series);
216: 	const span = max - min || 1;
217: 	const x = (i) => (i / (series.length - 1)) * 1000;
218: 	const y = (v) => 290 - ((v - min) / span) * 280;
219: 	const pts = series.map((v, i) => `${x(i).toFixed(1)},${y(v).toFixed(1)}`);
220: 	line.setAttribute("points", pts.join(" "));
221: 	area.setAttribute("d", `M0,300 L${pts.join(" L")} L1000,300 Z`);
222: 	const by = y(series[0]).toFixed(1);
223: 	baseline.setAttribute("y1", by);
224: 	baseline.setAttribute("y2", by);
225: }
226: 
227: function cell(text, className) {
228: 	const td = document.createElement("td");
229: 	td.textContent = text;
230: 	if (className) td.className = className;
231: 	return td;
232: }
233: 
234: function render() {
235: 	const rows = rowsWithQuotes();
236: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => Number.isFinite(r.value));
237: 	const total = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0);
238: 	const change = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.change, 0);
239: 	const start = total - change;
240: 	const pct = start ? change / start : NaN;
241: 	const periodLabel = (PERIODS.find(([k]) => k === state.period) || [, ""])[1];
242: 
243: 	$("total").textContent = ok.length ? usd.format(total) : "—";
244: 	const ch = $("change");
245: 	ch.textContent = ok.length ? `${signedUsd(change)} (${signedPct(pct)}) · ${periodLabel}` : state.holdings.length ? "No quotes" : "Tap Edit to add holdings";
246: 	ch.className = ok.length ? trendClass(change) : "flat";
247: 
248: 	$("rows").replaceChildren(
249: 		...rows.map((r) => {
250: 			const tr = document.createElement("tr");
251: 			tr.append(cell(r.symbol, "sym"), cell(sharesFmt.format(r.shares), "num"));
252: 			if (r.q && r.q.error) {
253: 				const err = cell(r.q.error, "err");
254: 				err.colSpan = 3;
255: 				tr.append(err);
256: 			} else if (!r.q) {
257: 				tr.append(cell("…", "num"), cell("", "num"), cell("", "num"));
258: 			} else {
259: 				tr.append(
260: 					cell(usd.format(r.q.price), "num"),
261: 					cell(usd.format(r.value), "num"),
262: 					cell(`${signedUsd(r.change)}  ${signedPct(r.pct)}`, `num ${trendClass(r.change)}`),
263: 				);
264: 			}
265: 			return tr;
266: 		}),
267: 	);
268: 
269: 	renderChart(portfolioSeries(rows), change);
270: 
271: 	const latest = Math.max(0, ...ok.map((r) => r.q.time || 0));
272: 	const failed = rows.length - ok.length;
273: 	const asOf = latest
274: 		? new Date(latest * 1000).toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York", month: "short", day: "numeric", hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit" }) + " ET"
275: 		: "";
276: 	setStatus([asOf && `As of ${asOf}`, failed ? `${failed} ticker(s) failed` : "", "Yahoo Finance"].filter(Boolean).join(" · "));
277: }

279: // ---------- editor ----------
280: 
281: function editorRow(symbol = "", shares = "") {
282: 	const row = document.createElement("div");
283: 	row.className = "editor-row";
284: 	const t = document.createElement("input");
285: 	t.className = "ticker";
286: 	t.placeholder = "Ticker";
287: 	t.value = symbol;
288: 	t.autocapitalize = "characters";
289: 	t.spellcheck = false;
290: 	const s = document.createElement("input");
291: 	s.className = "shares";
292: 	s.placeholder = "Shares";
293: 	s.inputMode = "decimal";
294: 	s.value = shares === "" ? "" : String(shares);
295: 	const del = document.createElement("button");
296: 	del.type = "button";
297: 	del.textContent = "✕";
298: 	del.title = "Remove";
299: 	del.addEventListener("click", () => row.remove());
300: 	row.append(t, s, del);
301: 	return row;
302: }
303: 
304: function openEditor(holdings) {
305: 	$("editor-error").textContent = "";
306: 	$("editor-rows").replaceChildren(...holdings.map((h) => editorRow(h.symbol, h.shares)), editorRow());
307: 	$("editor").hidden = false;
308: }
309: 
310: function readEditor() {
311: 	const holdings = [];
312: 	const problems = [];
313: 	const seen = new Set();
314: 	for (const row of $("editor-rows").querySelectorAll(".editor-row")) {
315: 		const symbol = row.querySelector(".ticker").value.trim().toUpperCase();
316: 		const sharesText = row.querySelector(".shares").value.trim().replace(/,/g, "");
317: 		if (!symbol && !sharesText) continue;
318: 		const shares = Number(sharesText);
319: 		if (!TICKER.test(symbol)) problems.push(`“${symbol || "?"}” is not a ticker`);
320: 		else if (!Number.isFinite(shares) || shares <= 0) problems.push(`${symbol}: shares must be a positive number`);
321: 		else if (seen.has(symbol)) problems.push(`${symbol} is listed twice`);
322: 		else {
323: 			seen.add(symbol);
324: 			holdings.push({ symbol, shares });
325: 		}
326: 	}
327: 	if (holdings.length > MAX_HOLDINGS) problems.push(`at most ${MAX_HOLDINGS} holdings`);
328: 	return { holdings, problems };
329: }
330: 
331: function wireEditor() {
332: 	$("edit").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(state.holdings));
333: 	$("add-row").addEventListener("click", () => {
334: 		const row = editorRow();
335: 		$("editor-rows").append(row);
336: 		row.querySelector("input").focus();
337: 	});
338: 	$("reset").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(parseHoldings(setting("holdings", ""))));
339: 	$("cancel").addEventListener("click", () => ($("editor").hidden = true));
340: 	$("save").addEventListener("click", () => {
341: 		const { holdings, problems } = readEditor();
342: 		if (problems.length) {
343: 			$("editor-error").textContent = problems.join(" · ");
344: 			return;
345: 		}
346: 		state.holdings = holdings;
347: 		save();
348: 		$("editor").hidden = true;
349: 		quotes = {};
350: 		render();
351: 		refresh();
352: 	});
353: }
354: 
355: // ---------- lifecycle ----------
356: 
357: function restartTimer() {
358: 	clearInterval(timer);
359: 	const seconds = Math.max(15, Number(setting("refreshSeconds", 60)) || 60);
360: 	timer = setInterval(refresh, seconds * 1000);
361: }
362: 
363: let initialized = false;
364: 
365: function init() {
366: 	if (initialized) return;
367: 	initialized = true;
368: 	applyTheme();
369: 	state = seededState();
370: 	save();
371: 	wireEditor();
372: 	renderPeriods();
373: 	render();
374: 	refresh();
375: 	restartTimer();
376: }
377: 
378: function onSettingsChanged() {
379: 	if (!initialized) return;
380: 	applyTheme();
381: 	const next = seededState();
382: 	const changed = next.seed !== state.seed || next.period !== state.period;
383: 	state = next;
384: 	save();
385: 	renderPeriods();
386: 	restartTimer();
387: 	if (changed) refresh();
388: 	else render();
389: }
390: 
391: if (typeof iCUE_initialized !== "undefined" && iCUE_initialized) {
392: 	init();
393: }

Look at `seededState()`:
```js
function seededState() {
	const seed = String(setting("holdings", ""));
	const seedPeriod = INTERVAL[setting("period", "1d")] ? setting("period", "1d") : "1d";
	const stored = loadStored();
	const fresh = { seed, seedPeriod, holdings: parseHoldings(seed), period: seedPeriod };
	if (!stored || !Array.isArray(stored.holdings)) return fresh;
	// Settings changed since the widget last saw them: they win.
	if (stored.seed !== seed) return { ...fresh, period: stored.period };
	if (stored.seedPeriod !== seedPeriod) return { ...stored, seedPeriod, period: seedPeriod };
	return stored;
}
```

In `onSettingsChanged()`:
```js
function onSettingsChanged() {
	if (!initialized) return;
	applyTheme();
	const next = seededState();
	const changed = next.seed !== state.seed || next.period !== state.period;
	state = next;
	save();
	renderPeriods();
	restartTimer();
	if (changed) refresh();
	else render();
}
```

119: 	const trTable = P.tr;
120: 	const trSync = (key) => (Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(trTable, key) ? trTable[key] : String(key));

122: 	window.iCUE = {
123: 		iCUELanguage: P.lang,

125: 		isPreview: P.preview,
126: 		widgetId: P.widgetId,

130: 	window.uniqueId = P.instanceId;
131: 	window.tr = (key) => Promise.resolve(trSync(key));
132: 	window.plugins = window.plugins || {};
133: 	window.iCUE_initialized = false;
134: 	for (const facade of P.plugins) {
135: 		window[`plugin${facade}_initialized`] = false;
136: 		console.warn(`plugin ${facade} is not available yet in EdgeHost`);
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	// Attribute expressions see a synchronous tr(), as iCUE resolves them to plain strings.
140: 	const evaluate = (expr) => new Function("tr", `"use strict"; return (${expr});`)(trSync);
141: 	const own = (object, key) => object != null && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(object, key);
142: 
143: 	// Runtime pages receive concrete values; anything missing is undefined, as in iCUE.
144: 	for (const p of P.props) {
145: 		window[p.name] = own(P.values, p.name) ? P.values[p.name] : undefined;
146: 	}
147: 
148: 	const IDENT = /^[A-Za-z_$][\w$]*$/;
149: 	// Widgets may declare their hooks with `var`, bare assignment, or `let`/`const`; the last
150: 	// creates a global lexical binding that is not a window property, so fall back to an
151: 	// indirect (global-scope) eval of the bare name.
152: 	const globalValue = (name) => {
153: 		if (name in window) return window[name];
154: 		try {
155: 			return (0, eval)(`typeof ${name} === "undefined" ? undefined : ${name}`);
156: 		} catch (_) {
157: 			return undefined;
158: 		}
159: 	};
160: 	const callHook = (objectName, hookName) => {
161: 		const target = globalValue(objectName);
162: 		const hook = target && target[hookName];
163: 		if (typeof hook !== "function") return;
164: 		try {
165: 			hook();
166: 		} catch (e) {
167: 			console.error(`${objectName}.${hookName} threw:`, e);
168: 		}
169: 	};
170: 
171: 	const importModules = async () => {
172: 		for (const m of P.modules) {
173: 			try {
174: 				const ns = await import(m.url);
175: 				if (IDENT.test(m.global)) window[m.global] = ns;
176: 			} catch (e) {
177: 				console.error(`module ${m.url} failed to load:`, e);
178: 			}
179: 		}
180: 	};
181: 
182: 	const EVALUATE_TIMEOUT_MS = 10000;
183: 	const withTimeout = (promise, ms) =>
184: 		Promise.race([
185: 			promise,
186: 			new Promise((_, reject) => setTimeout(() => reject(new Error(`timed out after ${ms} ms`)), ms)),
187: 		]);
188: 
189: 	// Evaluate mode (hidden page): compute defaults after modules (and, from M3, provider
190: 	// snapshots) are available, then report them. Values that JSON cannot carry are dropped.
191: 	const evaluateDefaults = async () => {
192: 		await importModules();
193: 		const values = {};
194: 		for (const p of P.props) {
195: 			if (p.defaultExpr == null) continue;
196: 			try {
197: 				let value = evaluate(p.defaultExpr);
198: 				// search-combobox defaults are provider functions that may return a promise of
199: 				// [key, displayText]; iCUE calls them and stores the key.
200: 				if (typeof value === "function") value = value();
201: 				if (value && typeof value.then === "function") value = await withTimeout(value, EVALUATE_TIMEOUT_MS);
202: 				if (p.kind === "search-combobox" && Array.isArray(value)) value = value[0];
203: 				if (value !== undefined) {
204: 					values[p.name] = value;
205: 					window[p.name] = value;
206: 				}
207: 			} catch (e) {
208: 				console.warn(`default for property ${p.name} could not be evaluated: ${fmt(e)}`);
209: 			}
210: 		}
211: 		let plain = {};
212: 		try {
213: 			plain = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(values));
214: 		} catch (e) {
215: 			console.error(`defaults are not serialisable: ${fmt(e)}`);
216: 		}
217: 		post({ k: "evaluated", values: plain });
218: 	};
219: 
220: 	let booted = false;
221: 	const boot = async () => {
222: 		if (booted) return;
223: 		booted = true;
224: 		if (P.mode === "evaluate") {
225: 			await evaluateDefaults();
226: 			return;
227: 		}
228: 		await importModules();
229: 		window.iCUE_initialized = true;
230: 		callHook("icueEvents", "onICUEInitialized");
231: 		callHook("icueEvents", "onDataUpdated");
232: 		post({ k: "booted" });
233: 	};
234: 
235: 	if (document.readyState === "loading") {
236: 		document.addEventListener("DOMContentLoaded", () => void boot());
237: 	} else {
238: 		void boot();
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	window.__edgehost = { boot };
242: })(__PAYLOAD__);

63:     /// Re-read `layout.json`, reopen the surface and show the current page.
64:     fn reload(&mut self, target: &EventLoopWindowTarget<AppEvent>) -> Result<()> {
65:         self.layout_mtime = self.layout_file_mtime();

109:     /// Mount the current page, evaluating defaults first where needed.
110:     fn show_page(&mut self) {
111:         let Some(surface) = self.surface.as_mut() else {
112:             return;
113:         };
114:         surface.unmount_all();
115:         self.shown.clear();
116:         if let Some(menus) = &self.menus {

118:         }

120:             eprintln!("the layout is empty: add widgets with `edgectl layout add <widget-id>`");
121:             return;
122:         };
123:         for inst in &page.instances {
124:             let root = self.store.widget_dir(&inst.widget, &inst.version);
125:             let def = match WidgetDef::load(&root) {
126:                 Ok(d) => d,
127:                 Err(e) => {

129:                     continue;
130:                 }
131:             };
132:             let widget = WidgetInstance {

134:                 root,
135:                 def,
136:                 slot: inst.slot,
137:                 origin: inst.origin,
138:             };
139:             let result = if inst.needs_evaluation() {
140:                 surface.evaluate(&widget)
141:             } else {
142:                 surface.mount(&widget, &inst.values)
143:             };
144:             match result {
145:                 Ok(()) => self.shown.push(widget),
146:                 Err(e) => eprintln!("skipping {} ({}): {e:#}", inst.widget, inst.id.short()),
147:             }
148:         }
149:     }

151:     /// Handle a report from a widget view.
152:     pub fn on_runtime(&mut self, event: RuntimeEvent) {
153:         let (instance, values) = match event {
154:             RuntimeEvent::Evaluated { instance, values } => (instance, values),
155:             RuntimeEvent::WantsKeyboard => {
156:                 if let Some(surface) = &self.surface {
157:                     surface.take_keyboard();
158:                 }
159:                 return;
160:             }
161:             RuntimeEvent::Close => return,
162:         };
163:         let id = InstanceId(instance);
164:         // Persist even if the page changed meanwhile; the defaults are still valid.
165:         if let Err(e) = self.layout.apply_evaluated(id, values) {
166:             eprintln!("evaluated defaults for an unknown instance: {e}");
167:             return;
168:         }
169:         if let Err(e) = self.store.save_layout(&self.layout) {
170:             eprintln!("saving layout.json: {e:#}");
171:         }
172:         self.layout_mtime = self.layout_file_mtime();
173:         let Some(surface) = self.surface.as_mut() else {
174:             return;
175:         };
176:         surface.end_evaluation(instance);
177:         let values = self
178:             .layout
179:             .instances()
180:             .find(|(_, i)| i.id == id)
181:             .map(|(_, i)| i.values.clone());
182:         if let (Some(widget), Some(values)) = (self.shown.iter().find(|w| w.id == instance), values)
183:         {
184:             if let Err(e) = surface.mount(widget, &values) {
185:                 eprintln!("showing {}: {e:#}", widget.def.manifest.id);
186:             }
187:         }
188:     }

190:     /// Handle a menu choice. Returns `true` to quit.
191:     pub fn on_menu(&mut self, id: &str, target: &EventLoopWindowTarget<AppEvent>) -> bool {
192:         let pages = self.layout.pages.len();
193:         match id {
194:             ids::QUIT => return true,
195:             ids::NEXT if pages > 0 => {
196:                 self.page = (self.page + 1) % pages;
197:                 self.show_page();
198:             }
199:             ids::PREVIOUS if pages > 0 => {
200:                 self.page = (self.page + pages - 1) % pages;
201:                 self.show_page();
202:             }
203:             ids::RELOAD => self.reload_or_report(target),
204:             ids::OPEN_DATA => {
205:                 if let Err(e) = std::process::Command::new("open")
206:                     .arg(self.store.root())
207:                     .spawn()
208:                 {
209:                     eprintln!("opening {}: {e}", self.store.root().display());
210:                 }
211:             }
212:             _ => {}
213:         }
214:         false
215:     }

218:     pub fn poll_layout(&mut self, target: &EventLoopWindowTarget<AppEvent>) {

220:             eprintln!("layout.json changed; reloading");
221:             self.reload_or_report(target);

228:         }
229:     }

233:             .and_then(|m| m.modified())
234:             .ok()
235:     }
