39: pub(crate) const NAMESPACE: Uuid = Uuid::from_u128(0x5f0e_9c1d_7a2b_4e63_9d84_1c6f_2b7a_e0d3);
40: 
41: /// Options for [`Surface::open`].
42: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
43: pub struct SurfaceOptions {
44:     /// Which display to cover.
45:     pub display: DisplayChoice,
46:     /// Orientation of the stand-in window when no display is selected.
47:     pub windowed_orientation: Orientation,
48:     /// Stand-in window size as a fraction of the panel's pixel size.
49:     pub windowed_scale: f64,
50:     /// Enable the Web Inspector.
51:     pub devtools: bool,
52:     /// Shared `common/` folder served at `/common/` when a widget lacks its own.
53:     pub common_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
54:     /// `iCUE.iCUELanguage`.
55:     pub language: String,
56:     /// Developer runs: Escape inside a widget asks the host to close.
57:     pub escape_closes: bool,
58: }
59: 
60: /// Something a web view reported to the host.
61: #[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
62: pub enum RuntimeEvent {
63:     /// An evaluate view finished computing the defaults of `instance`.
64:     Evaluated {
65:         /// The instance.
66:         instance: Uuid,
67:         /// Property name → concrete value.
68:         values: BTreeMap<String, Value>,
69:     },
70:     /// A widget asked to close the run (Escape with [`SurfaceOptions::escape_closes`]).
71:     Close,
72:     /// A text field in a widget gained focus: make EdgeHost the key app so typing reaches it.
73:     WantsKeyboard,
74: }
75: 
76: /// Where web views deliver [`RuntimeEvent`]s (usually an event-loop proxy).
77: pub type EventSink = Rc<dyn Fn(RuntimeEvent)>;
78: 
79: /// A widget to show on the surface.
80: #[derive(Debug)]
81: pub struct WidgetInstance {
82:     /// Instance id: `uniqueId`, storage key and data-store identifier.
83:     pub id: Uuid,
84:     /// Widget directory.
85:     pub root: PathBuf,
86:     /// Parsed widget.
87:     pub def: WidgetDef,
88:     /// Slot size.
89:     pub slot: Slot,
90:     /// Top-left grid cell.
91:     pub origin: Cell,
92: }
93: 
94: /// The window on the Edge (or stand-in) and the widget views in it.
95: pub struct Surface {
96:     window: Window,
97:     _background: WebView,
98:     orientation: Orientation,
99:     fit: Fit,
100:     on_display: bool,
101:     description: String,
102:     device_id: Uuid,
103:     opts: SurfaceOptions,
104:     sink: EventSink,
105:     views: HashMap<Uuid, WebView>,
106:     evaluations: HashMap<Uuid, WebView>,
107: }
108: 
109: impl Surface {
110:     /// Select a display and open the surface on it.
111:     ///
112:     /// # Errors
113:     ///
114:     /// An unknown `--display` pick, or failure to create the window or background view.
115:     pub fn open<T: 'static>(
116:         target: &EventLoopWindowTarget<T>,
117:         opts: SurfaceOptions,
118:         sink: EventSink,
119:     ) -> Result<Self> {
120:         if !(0.1..=2.0).contains(&opts.windowed_scale) {
121:             bail!("window scale {} is outside 0.1..=2.0", opts.windowed_scale);
122:         }
123:         let displays = list_displays(target);
124:         let selection = select_display(&displays, &opts.display)?;
125:         let builder = WindowBuilder::new()
126:             .with_title("EdgeHost")
127:             .with_resizable(false);
128:         let (orientation, area, builder, description, device_key) = match selection {
129:             Selection::Display {
130:                 index,
131:                 orientation,
132:                 edge_hardware,
133:                 native,
134:             } => {
135:                 let d = displays
136:                     .get(index)
137:                     .context("selected display disappeared")?;
138:                 if edge_hardware && !native {
139:                     eprintln!(
140:                         "warning: the XENEON EDGE is in {}×{} mode, not its native 2560×720; the image is scaled.\n\
141:                          Fix: `brew install displayplacer`, then `displayplacer list` and select its \
142:                          res:2560x720 mode (see docs/research/edge-hardware.md).",
143:                         d.pixel_size.0, d.pixel_size.1
144:                     );
145:                 }
146:                 let builder = builder
147:                     .with_decorations(false)
148:                     .with_position(LogicalPosition::new(d.logical_origin.0, d.logical_origin.1))
149:                     .with_inner_size(LogicalSize::new(d.logical_size.0, d.logical_size.1))
150:                     .with_visible_on_all_workspaces(true);
151:                 let kind = if edge_hardware {
152:                     "XENEON EDGE"
153:                 } else {
154:                     "display"
155:                 };
156:                 let key = format!("display:{:?}:{:?}:{}", d.vendor, d.model, d.name);
157:                 (
158:                     orientation,
159:                     d.logical_size,
160:                     builder,
161:                     format!("{kind} \"{}\"", d.name),
162:                     key,
163:                 )
164:             }
165:             Selection::Windowed => {
166:                 let (pw, ph) = opts.windowed_orientation.panel_size();
167:                 let area = (
168:                     f64::from(pw) * opts.windowed_scale,
169:                     f64::from(ph) * opts.windowed_scale,
170:                 );
171:                 let builder = builder.with_inner_size(LogicalSize::new(area.0, area.1));
172:                 let text = "a stand-in window (no XENEON EDGE detected; see `edgectl displays`)"
173:                     .to_owned();
174:                 (
175:                     opts.windowed_orientation,
176:                     area,
177:                     builder,
178:                     text,
179:                     "windowed".to_owned(),
180:                 )
181:             }
182:         };
183:         let window = builder
184:             .build(target)
185:             .context("creating the surface window")?;
186:         let background = WebViewBuilder::new()
187:             .with_bounds(logical_rect((0.0, 0.0), area))
188:             .with_html("<!doctype html><html style=\"background:#000;height:100%\"></html>")
189:             .build_as_child(&window)
190:             .context("creating the background view")?;
191:         window.set_focus();
192:         Ok(Self {
193:             window,
194:             _background: background,
195:             orientation,
196:             fit: fit_panel(orientation, area),
197:             on_display: matches!(selection, Selection::Display { .. }),
198:             description,
199:             device_id: Uuid::new_v5(&NAMESPACE, device_key.as_bytes()),
200:             opts,
201:             sink,
202:             views: HashMap::new(),
203:             evaluations: HashMap::new(),
204:         })
205:     }
206: 
207:     /// Grid orientation of the surface.
208:     #[must_use]
209:     pub fn orientation(&self) -> Orientation {
210:         self.orientation
211:     }
212: 
213:     /// Whether the surface covers a real display (as opposed to a stand-in window).
214:     #[must_use]
215:     pub fn on_display(&self) -> bool {
216:         self.on_display
217:     }
218: 
219:     /// Human-readable description of where the surface is.
220:     #[must_use]
221:     pub fn description(&self) -> &str {
222:         &self.description
223:     }
224: 
225:     /// The surface window.
226:     #[must_use]
227:     pub fn window(&self) -> &Window {
228:         &self.window
229:     }
230: 
231:     /// Make EdgeHost the active app with the surface as key window, so keystrokes reach the
232:     /// focused widget field ([`RuntimeEvent::WantsKeyboard`]).
233:     pub fn take_keyboard(&self) {
234:         self.window.set_focus();
235:     }
236: 
237:     /// Start computing `instance`'s property defaults in a hidden view. The result arrives as
238:     /// [`RuntimeEvent::Evaluated`]; call [`Surface::end_evaluation`] afterwards.
239:     ///
240:     /// # Errors
241:     ///
242:     /// The slot does not fit the grid, or the view cannot be created.
243:     pub fn evaluate(&mut self, instance: &WidgetInstance) -> Result<()> {
244:         let rect = self.slot_bounds(instance)?;
245:         let script = shim::render(
246:             &instance.def,
247:             &self.shim_config(instance),
248:             ShimMode::Evaluate,
249:         )
250:         .context("rendering the evaluate shim")?;
251:         let view = self.build_view(instance, &script, EVAL_PATH, rect, false)?;
252:         self.evaluations.insert(instance.id, view);
253:         Ok(())
254:     }
255: 
256:     /// Drop the evaluate view of `id`, if any.
257:     pub fn end_evaluation(&mut self, id: Uuid) {
258:         self.evaluations.remove(&id);
259:     }
260: 
261:     /// Show `instance` with concrete property `values`, replacing any existing view for it.
262:     ///
263:     /// # Errors
264:     ///
265:     /// The slot does not fit the grid, or the view cannot be created.
266:     pub fn mount(
267:         &mut self,
268:         instance: &WidgetInstance,
269:         values: &BTreeMap<String, Value>,
270:     ) -> Result<()> {
271:         let rect = self.slot_bounds(instance)?;
272:         let script = shim::render(
273:             &instance.def,
274:             &self.shim_config(instance),
275:             ShimMode::Runtime(values),
276:         )
277:         .context("rendering the widget shim")?;
278:         self.views.remove(&instance.id);
279:         let view = self.build_view(instance, &script, "/index.html", rect, true)?;
280:         view.zoom(self.fit.scale).context("setting widget zoom")?;
281:         if self.opts.escape_closes {
282:             if let Err(e) = view.focus() {
283:                 eprintln!("warning: could not focus the widget view: {e}");
284:             }
285:         }
286:         self.views.insert(instance.id, view);
287:         Ok(())
288:     }
289: 
290:     /// Remove every widget and evaluate view.
291:     pub fn unmount_all(&mut self) {
292:         self.views.clear();
293:         self.evaluations.clear();
294:     }
295: 
296:     fn slot_bounds(&self, instance: &WidgetInstance) -> Result<Rect> {
297:         let px: PxRect =
298:             slot_rect(self.orientation, instance.slot, instance.origin).with_context(|| {
299:                 format!(
300:                     "placing {} on the {} surface",
301:                     instance.def.manifest.id, self.orientation
302:                 )
303:             })?;
304:         let s = self.fit.scale;
305:         let (ox, oy) = self.fit.offset;
306:         Ok(logical_rect(
307:             (ox + f64::from(px.x) * s, oy + f64::from(px.y) * s),
308:             (f64::from(px.width) * s, f64::from(px.height) * s),
309:         ))
310:     }
311: 
312:     fn shim_config(&self, instance: &WidgetInstance) -> ShimConfig {
313:         ShimConfig {
314:             language: self.opts.language.clone(),
315:             fps_limit: 30,
316:             is_preview: false,
317:             instance_id: instance.id,
318:             device_id: self.device_id,
319:             temperature_unit: TemperatureUnit::Celsius,
320:             base_url: format!("{SCHEME}://{}/", instance.id.simple()),
321:             escape_closes: self.opts.escape_closes,
322:             debug_input: std::env::var_os("EDGEHOST_DEBUG_INPUT").is_some(),
323:         }
324:     }
325: 
326:     fn build_view(
327:         &self,
328:         instance: &WidgetInstance,
329:         script: &str,
330:         path: &str,
331:         bounds: Rect,
332:         visible: bool,
333:     ) -> Result<WebView> {
334:         let serve_root = instance.root.clone();
335:         let serve_common = self.opts.common_dir.clone();
336:         let permissions = Arc::new(instance.def.manifest.url_permissions());
337:         let label = instance.def.manifest.id.to_string();
338:         let id = instance.id;
339:         let sink = Rc::clone(&self.sink);
340:         let builder = WebViewBuilder::new()
341:             .with_bounds(bounds)
342:             .with_visible(visible)
343:             .with_transparent(true)
344:             // The Edge is a second screen: a tap must reach the widget even when EdgeHost is not
345:             // the active app, instead of only activating it (the first click would be swallowed).
346:             .with_accept_first_mouse(true)
347:             .with_devtools(self.opts.devtools)
348:             .with_initialization_script_for_main_only(script, true)
349:             .with_asynchronous_custom_protocol(SCHEME.to_owned(), move |_id, request, responder| {
350:                 let uri = request.uri();
351:                 if uri.path() == proxy::ROUTE {
352:                     proxy::handle(
353:                         Arc::clone(&permissions),
354:                         uri.query().map(str::to_owned),
355:                         responder,
356:                     );
357:                 } else {
358:                     responder.respond(serve(&serve_root, serve_common.as_deref(), uri.path()));
359:                 }
360:             })
361:             .with_ipc_handler(move |request| {
362:                 if let Some(event) = report(&label, id, request.body()) {
363:                     sink(event);
364:                 }
365:             })
366:             .with_url(format!("{SCHEME}://{}{path}", id.simple()));
367:         #[cfg(target_os = "macos")]
368:         let builder = {
369:             use wry::WebViewBuilderExtDarwin;
370:             // Per-instance WebKit data store (macOS 14+): isolated localStorage/IndexedDB, as iCUE does.
371:             builder.with_data_store_identifier(*id.as_bytes())
372:         };
373:         builder
374:             .build_as_child(&self.window)
375:             .context("creating a widget view")
376:     }
377: }
378: 
379: fn logical_rect(origin: (f64, f64), size: (f64, f64)) -> Rect {
380:     Rect {
381:         position: wry::dpi::LogicalPosition::new(origin.0, origin.1).into(),
382:         size: wry::dpi::LogicalSize::new(size.0, size.1).into(),
383:     }
384: }
385: 
386: /// Serve a widget file; `/common/…` falls back to the shared folder; [`EVAL_PATH`] is a blank page.
387: fn serve(root: &Path, common: Option<&Path>, url_path: &str) -> Response<Cow<'static, [u8]>> {
388:     if url_path == EVAL_PATH {
389:         return Response::builder()
390:             .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "text/html; charset=utf-8")
391:             .body(Cow::Borrowed(
392:                 b"<!doctype html><html><head></head><body></body></html>".as_slice(),
393:             ))
394:             .unwrap_or_else(|_| Response::new(Cow::Borrowed(b"".as_slice())));
395:     }
396:     let result = match read_asset(root, url_path) {
397:         Err(e) if e.status() == 404 => match (common, url_path.strip_prefix("/common/")) {
398:             (Some(dir), Some(rest)) => read_asset(dir, rest).map_err(|_| e),
399:             _ => Err(e),
400:         },
401:         other => other,
402:     };
403:     let built = match result {
404:         Ok(bytes) => {
405:             let mime = resolve(root, url_path).map_or("application/octet-stream", |p| mime_for(&p));
406:             Response::builder()
407:                 .status(200)
408:                 .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, mime)
409:                 .header(header::CACHE_CONTROL, "no-store")
410:                 .body(Cow::Owned(bytes))
411:         }
412:         Err(e) => {
413:             eprintln!("icuew: {url_path}: {e:#}");
414:             Response::builder()
415:                 .status(e.status())
416:                 .header(header::CONTENT_TYPE, "text/plain; charset=utf-8")
417:                 .body(Cow::Owned(e.to_string().into_bytes()))
418:         }
419:     };
420:     built.unwrap_or_else(|e| {
421:         eprintln!("icuew: building response for {url_path}: {e}");
422:         Response::new(Cow::Borrowed(b"internal error".as_slice()))
423:     })
424: }
425: 
426: /// Log a message posted by the shim and turn host requests into [`RuntimeEvent`]s.
427: fn report(label: &str, instance: Uuid, body: &str) -> Option<RuntimeEvent> {
428:     let Ok(message) = serde_json::from_str::<Value>(body) else {
429:         eprintln!("[{label}] unparseable message: {body}");
430:         return None;
431:     };
432:     let field = |name: &str| {
433:         message
434:             .get(name)
435:             .and_then(Value::as_str)
436:             .unwrap_or_default()
437:     };
438:     match field("k") {
439:         "console" => eprintln!("[{label}] {}: {}", field("level"), field("msg")),
440:         "booted" => eprintln!("[{label}] booted"),
441:         "close" => return Some(RuntimeEvent::Close),
442:         "keyboard" => return Some(RuntimeEvent::WantsKeyboard),
443:         "evaluated" => {
444:             let values = match message.get("values") {
445:                 Some(Value::Object(map)) => {
446:                     map.iter().map(|(k, v)| (k.clone(), v.clone())).collect()
447:                 }
448:                 _ => BTreeMap::new(),
449:             };
450:             return Some(RuntimeEvent::Evaluated { instance, values });
451:         }
452:         other => eprintln!("[{label}] {other}: {body}"),
453:     }
454:     None
455: }
456: 
457: #[cfg(test)]
458: mod tests {
459:     use super::*;
460: 
461:     #[test]
462:     fn report_parses_host_requests() {
463:         let id = Uuid::from_u128(7);
464:         assert_eq!(
465:             report("w", id, r#"{"k":"close"}"#),
466:             Some(RuntimeEvent::Close)
467:         );
468:         assert_eq!(
469:             report("w", id, r#"{"k":"evaluated","values":{"a":1,"b":"x"}}"#),
470:             Some(RuntimeEvent::Evaluated {
471:                 instance: id,
472:                 values: BTreeMap::from([
473:                     ("a".into(), Value::from(1)),
474:                     ("b".into(), Value::from("x"))
475:                 ]),
476:             })
477:         );
478:         assert_eq!(report("w", id, r#"{"k":"booted"}"#), None);
479:         assert_eq!(report("w", id, "not json"), None);
480:     }
481: 
482:     #[test]
483:     fn eval_page_is_served_without_touching_the_widget() {
484:         let resp = serve(Path::new("/nonexistent"), None, EVAL_PATH);
485:         assert_eq!(resp.status(), 200);
486:         assert!(resp.body().starts_with(b"<!doctype html>"));
487:         assert_eq!(
488:             serve(Path::new("/nonexistent"), None, "/x.js").status(),
489:             404
490:         );
491:     }
492: }

const boot = async () => {

It calls `onICUEInitialized` and then `onDataUpdated`. In `init()`, `initialized` becomes true. Then `onDataUpdated` calls `onSettingsChanged()`, which checks `if (!initialized) return;`. Wait, there's another possibility. The `icueEvents` object is defined with `icueEvents = {...}` at the top level. This creates a global variable. But in the shim, `globalValue` first checks `if (name in window) return window[name];`. If `icueEvents` is a global lexical variable (not a window property), this would fail. But since it's a bare assignment in non-strict mode, it's a window property. Good.

Wait, look at `rowsWithQuotes()`:
```js
function rowsWithQuotes() {
	return state.holdings
		.map((h) => {
			const q = quotes[h.symbol];
			if (!q || q.error) return { ...h, q, value: NaN, change: NaN };
			const value = h.shares * q.price;
			const change = h.shares * (q.price - q.base);
			return { ...h, q, value, change, pct: q.base ? q.price / q.base - 1 : NaN };
		})
		.sort((a, b) => (Number.isFinite(b.value) ? b.value : -1) - (Number.isFinite(a.value) ? a.value : -1));
}
```

If `q` is undefined (no quote yet), it returns `value: NaN, change: NaN`. The sort: `Number.isFinite(b.value) ? b.value : -1` - for NaN, returns -1. So all rows with NaN value get sort key -1. They will be in original order. Fine.

    textContent: '',
    hidden: false,
    type: '',
    value: '',
    placeholder: '',
    autocapitalize: '',
    spellcheck: false,
    inputMode: '',
    colSpan: 1,
    _parent: null,
    _attrs: {},
    style: {},
    append(...nodes) { /* add */ },
    replaceChildren(...nodes) { /* clear and add */ },
    remove() { /* remove from parent */ },
    removeChild(node) { /* remove */ },
    appendChild(node) { this.append(node); },
    setAttribute(k, v) { this._attrs[k] = String(v); },
    getAttribute(k) { return this._attrs[k]; },
    addEventListener(type, fn) { (listeners[type] ||= []).push(fn); },
    querySelector(sel) { /* find first matching child */ },
    querySelectorAll(sel) { /* find all matching children */ },
    focus() {},
    click() { /* fire click listeners */ },
  };
  return el;
}
```

And document:
```js
const byId = new Map();
const document = {
  createElement(tag) { return makeElement(tag); },
  getElementById(id) { 
    if (!byId.has(id)) byId.set(id, makeElement('div', id));
    return byId.get(id);
  },
  documentElement: { style: {} },
  addEventListener() {},
};
```

Hmm, but `document.documentElement` needs to be an element with `style`. And `document.getElementById` should create elements for the IDs used. That works.

For `localStorage`, I can use a Map.

For `fetch`, return a mock promise.

For `setInterval`/`clearInterval`, use Node's.

For `console`, use Node's.

For `window`, use the context itself.

For globals `iCUE`, `device`, `uniqueId`, `holdings`, `period`, etc., set them on the context.

Then run the shim? Actually, I don't need the shim for this test. I can just set the globals directly and call `icueEvents.onICUEInitialized()`.

But wait, if I don't run the shim, `icueEvents` needs to be defined by portfolio.js. Yes.

Let me write the test.

Actually, I realize the test doesn't need to be super precise. I just want to verify that:
1. After init, clicking a period button changes `state.period`.
2. After init, clicking the edit button opens the editor.

If these work in the fake DOM, then the bug is likely environmental (host/CSS). If they don't, then the bug is in the JS.

1: // Stock Portfolio widget (iCUE Widget API 1.x).
2: //
3: // Quotes come from Yahoo Finance's chart API. Under EdgeHost, fetch() to the domain declared in

30: const signedPct = (v) => (Number.isFinite(v) ? `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${Math.abs(v * 100).toFixed(2)}%` : "");

42: function parseHoldings(text) {
43: 	const out = [];
44: 	for (const entry of String(text || "").split(/[,;\n]+/)) {
45: 		const [sym, qty] = entry.trim().split(/[\s:=]+/);
46: 		const symbol = (sym || "").toUpperCase();
47: 		const shares = Number(qty);
48: 		if (TICKER.test(symbol) && Number.isFinite(shares) && shares > 0) out.push({ symbol, shares });
49: 	}
50: 	return out.slice(0, MAX_HOLDINGS);
51: }
52: 
53: function loadStored() {
54: 	try {
55: 		return JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem(uniqueId) || "null");
56: 	} catch (_) {
57: 		return null;
58: 	}
59: }
60: 
61: function save() {
62: 	try {
63: 		localStorage.setItem(uniqueId, JSON.stringify(state));
64: 	} catch (e) {
65: 		console.warn("could not save portfolio", e);
66: 	}
67: }

69: function seededState() {
70: 	const seed = String(setting("holdings", ""));
71: 	const seedPeriod = INTERVAL[setting("period", "1d")] ? setting("period", "1d") : "1d";
72: 	const stored = loadStored();
73: 	const fresh = { seed, seedPeriod, holdings: parseHoldings(seed), period: seedPeriod };
74: 	if (!stored || !Array.isArray(stored.holdings)) return fresh;
75: 	// Settings changed since the widget last saw them: they win.
76: 	if (stored.seed !== seed) return { ...fresh, period: stored.period };
77: 	if (stored.seedPeriod !== seedPeriod) return { ...stored, seedPeriod, period: seedPeriod };
78: 	return stored;
79: }

83: async function fetchSeries(symbol, range) {
84: 	const url =
85: 		`https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/${encodeURIComponent(symbol)}` +
86: 		`?range=${range}&interval=${INTERVAL[range]}&includePrePost=false`;
87: 	const response = await fetch(url);
88: 	let body = null;
89: 	try {
90: 		body = await response.json();
91: 	} catch (_) {
92: 		throw new Error(`HTTP ${response.status}`);
93: 	}
94: 	const result = body && body.chart && body.chart.result && body.chart.result[0];
95: 	if (!result) {
96: 		const err = body && body.chart && body.chart.error;
97: 		throw new Error((err && err.description) || `HTTP ${response.status}`);
98: 	}
99: 	const meta = result.meta || {};
100: 	const quote = (result.indicators && result.indicators.quote && result.indicators.quote[0]) || {};
101: 	const closes = quote.close || [];
102: 	const points = (result.timestamp || []).map((t, i) => [t, closes[i]]).filter((p) => Number.isFinite(p[1]));
103: 	const last = points.length ? points[points.length - 1][1] : NaN;
104: 	const price = Number.isFinite(meta.regularMarketPrice) ? meta.regularMarketPrice : last;
105: 	let base = meta.chartPreviousClose;
106: 	if (range === "1d" && Number.isFinite(meta.previousClose)) base = meta.previousClose;
107: 	if (!Number.isFinite(base)) base = points.length ? points[0][1] : price;
108: 	if (!Number.isFinite(price)) throw new Error("no price");
109: 	return {
110: 		symbol,
111: 		price,
112: 		base,
113: 		time: meta.regularMarketTime,
114: 		points,
115: 		currency: meta.currency || "USD",
116: 		gmtoffset: Number.isFinite(meta.gmtoffset) ? meta.gmtoffset : -5 * 3600,
117: 	};
118: }
119: 
120: async function refresh() {
121: 	if (!state || state.holdings.length === 0) {
122: 		quotes = {};
123: 		render();
124: 		return;
125: 	}
126: 	const seq = ++requestSeq;
127: 	setStatus("Updating…");
128: 	const range = state.period;
129: 	const results = await Promise.allSettled(state.holdings.map((h) => fetchSeries(h.symbol, range)));
130: 	if (seq !== requestSeq) return; // a newer refresh (e.g. period change) superseded this one
131: 	quotes = {};
132: 	quotesRange = range;
133: 	results.forEach((r, i) => {
134: 		const symbol = state.holdings[i].symbol;
135: 		quotes[symbol] = r.status === "fulfilled" ? r.value : { symbol, error: String((r.reason && r.reason.message) || r.reason) };
136: 	});
137: 	const failed = Object.values(quotes).filter((q) => q.error);
138: 	console.log(`portfolio ${range}: ${state.holdings.length - failed.length}/${state.holdings.length} quotes` + (failed.length ? `; failed: ${failed.map((q) => `${q.symbol} (${q.error})`).join(", ")}` : ""));
139: 	render();
140: }

142: function rowsWithQuotes() {
143: 	return state.holdings
144: 		.map((h) => {
145: 			const q = quotes[h.symbol];
146: 			if (!q || q.error) return { ...h, q, value: NaN, change: NaN };
147: 			const value = h.shares * q.price;
148: 			const change = h.shares * (q.price - q.base);
149: 			return { ...h, q, value, change, pct: q.base ? q.price / q.base - 1 : NaN };
150: 		})
151: 		.sort((a, b) => (Number.isFinite(b.value) ? b.value : -1) - (Number.isFinite(a.value) ? a.value : -1));
152: }

154: // Portfolio value over the period as [{t, v}]: the base value, then at each bar time shares × last
155: // known close (base before a ticker's first bar), then the current value "now".
156: function portfolioSeries(rows) {
157: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => r.q && !r.q.error);
158: 	if (ok.length === 0) return [];
159: 	const times = [...new Set(ok.flatMap((r) => r.q.points.map((p) => p[0])))].sort((a, b) => a - b);
160: 	const cursor = ok.map(() => 0);
161: 	const last = ok.map((r) => r.q.base);
162: 	const nowSec = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);
163: 	const series = [{ t: times.length ? times[0] : nowSec, v: ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.shares * r.q.base, 0) }];
164: 	for (const t of times) {
165: 		let total = 0;
166: 		ok.forEach((r, k) => {
167: 			const pts = r.q.points;
168: 			while (cursor[k] < pts.length && pts[cursor[k]][0] <= t) {
169: 				last[k] = pts[cursor[k]][1];
170: 				cursor[k] += 1;
171: 			}
172: 			total += r.shares * last[k];
173: 		});
174: 		series.push({ t, v: total });
175: 	}
176: 	series.push({ t: Math.max(nowSec, times.length ? times[times.length - 1] : nowSec), v: ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0) });
177: 	return series;
178: }

180: // ---------- timeline ----------
181: 
182: const ET = "America/New_York";
183: const etFormat = (options) => new Intl.DateTimeFormat("en-US", { timeZone: ET, ...options });
184: const etDay = etFormat({ year: "numeric", month: "2-digit", day: "2-digit" });
185: const etMonth = etFormat({ year: "numeric", month: "2-digit" });
186: const LABELS = {
187: 	hour: etFormat({ hour: "numeric" }),
188: 	weekday: etFormat({ weekday: "short" }),
189: 	monthDay: etFormat({ month: "short", day: "numeric" }),
190: 	month: etFormat({ month: "short" }),
191: 	monthYear: etFormat({ month: "short", year: "numeric" }),
192: };
193: const at = (t) => new Date(t * 1000);
194: 
195: // Monday-based week number of an ET calendar date.
196: function etWeek(t) {
197: 	const [m, d, y] = etDay.format(at(t)).split("/").map(Number);
198: 	const days = Math.floor(Date.UTC(y, m - 1, d) / 86400000);
199: 	return Math.floor((days + 3) / 7); // 1970-01-01 was a Thursday
200: }
201: 
202: // x position (0..1000) of each series point. 1D uses real session time (9:30–16:00 ET of the
203: // bars' day) so a mid-session line stops at "now"; longer periods space bars evenly, which
204: // compresses nights and weekends as finance charts do.
205: function xScale(series, range, gmtoffset) {
206: 	if (range === "1d" && series.length > 2) {
207: 		const local = series[1].t + gmtoffset;
208: 		const midnight = Math.floor(local / 86400) * 86400 - gmtoffset;
209: 		const start = midnight + 9.5 * 3600;
210: 		const end = midnight + 16 * 3600;
211: 		series[0].t = start;
212: 		series[series.length - 1].t = Math.min(series[series.length - 1].t, end);
213: 		const x = (p) => (Math.min(Math.max((p.t - start) / (end - start), 0), 1)) * 1000;
214: 		return { x, byTime: true, start, end };
215: 	}
216: 	const n = series.length;
217: 	return { x: (_p, i) => (n > 1 ? (i / (n - 1)) * 1000 : 0), byTime: false };
218: }
219: 
220: // Tick marks [{x, label}] for the period, thinned to fit `width` pixels.
221: function timelineTicks(series, scale, range, width) {
222: 	const ticks = [];
223: 	if (scale.byTime) {
224: 		for (let h = 10; h <= 16; h += 1) {
225: 			const t = scale.start + (h - 9.5) * 3600;
226: 			ticks.push({ x: scale.x({ t }), label: LABELS.hour.format(at(t)) });
227: 		}
228: 	} else if (series.length > 1) {
229: 		// Months show the year on the first tick and wherever the year changes ("Oct 2025 … Feb 2026 …").
230: 		const year = (t) => etMonth.format(at(t)).slice(-4);
231: 		const monthLabel = (t, previousT) =>
232: 			(previousT === undefined || year(t) !== year(previousT) ? LABELS.monthYear : LABELS.month).format(at(t));
233: 		const [keyOf, label] = {
234: 			"5d": [(t) => etDay.format(at(t)), (t) => LABELS.weekday.format(at(t))],
235: 			"1mo": [etWeek, (t) => LABELS.monthDay.format(at(t))],
236: 		}[range] || [(t) => etMonth.format(at(t)), monthLabel];
237: 		let previous = null;
238: 		// Skip the base point (index 0): it belongs to the period before.
239: 		for (let i = 1; i < series.length - 1; i += 1) {
240: 			const key = keyOf(series[i].t);
241: 			if (key !== previous) {
242: 				ticks.push({ x: scale.x(series[i], i), t: series[i].t });
243: 				previous = key;
244: 			}
245: 		}
246: 		const kept = thin(ticks, width);
247: 		return kept.map((tick, i) => ({ x: tick.x, label: label(tick.t, i === 0 ? undefined : kept[i - 1].t) }));
248: 	}
249: 	return thin(ticks, width);
250: }
251: 
252: // Keep every k-th tick so labels (~84 px each) fit in `width`.
253: function thin(ticks, width) {
254: 	const maxLabels = Math.max(3, Math.floor(width / 84));
255: 	if (ticks.length <= maxLabels) return ticks;
256: 	const step = Math.ceil(ticks.length / maxLabels);
257: 	return ticks.filter((_, i) => i % step === 0);
258: }

316: 	const pts = series.map((p, i) => `${scale.x(p, i).toFixed(1)},${y(p.v).toFixed(1)}`);
317: 	const lastX = scale.x(series[series.length - 1], series.length - 1).toFixed(1);

319: 	line.setAttribute("points", pts.join(" "));
320: 	area.setAttribute("d", `M${firstX},300 L${pts.join(" L")} L${lastX},300 Z`);
321: 	const by = y(series[0].v).toFixed(1);
322: 	baseline.setAttribute("y1", by);
323: 	baseline.setAttribute("y2", by);
324: 
325: 	const ticks = timelineTicks(series, scale, range, timeline.clientWidth || 600);
326: 	const svgNs = "http://www.w3.org/2000/svg";
327: 	grid.replaceChildren(
328: 		...ticks.map((tick) => {
329: 			const l = document.createElementNS(svgNs, "line");
330: 			l.setAttribute("x1", tick.x.toFixed(1));
331: 			l.setAttribute("x2", tick.x.toFixed(1));
332: 			l.setAttribute("y1", "0");
333: 			l.setAttribute("y2", "300");
334: 			return l;
335: 		}),
336: 	);
337: 	timeline.replaceChildren(
338: 		...ticks.map((tick) => {
339: 			const span = document.createElement("span");
340: 			span.textContent = tick.label;
341: 			span.style.left = `${tick.x / 10}%`;
342: 			if (tick.x < 40) span.className = "first";
343: 			else if (tick.x > 960) span.className = "last";
344: 			return span;
345: 		}),
346: 	);
347: }
348: 
349: function cell(text, className) {
350: 	const td = document.createElement("td");
351: 	td.textContent = text;
352: 	if (className) td.className = className;
353: 	return td;
354: }
355: 
356: function render() {
357: 	const rows = rowsWithQuotes();
358: 	const ok = rows.filter((r) => Number.isFinite(r.value));
359: 	const total = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.value, 0);
360: 	const change = ok.reduce((s, r) => s + r.change, 0);
361: 	const start = total - change;
362: 	const pct = start ? change / start : NaN;
363: 	const periodLabel = (PERIODS.find(([k]) => k === state.period) || [, ""])[1];
364: 
365: 	$("total").textContent = ok.length ? usd.format(total) : "—";
366: 	const ch = $("change");
367: 	ch.textContent = ok.length ? `${signedUsd(change)} (${signedPct(pct)}) · ${periodLabel}` : state.holdings.length ? "No quotes" : "Tap Edit to add holdings";
368: 	ch.className = ok.length ? trendClass(change) : "flat";
369: 
370: 	$("rows").replaceChildren(
371: 		...rows.map((r) => {
372: 			const tr = document.createElement("tr");
373: 			tr.append(cell(r.symbol, "sym"), cell(sharesFmt.format(r.shares), "num"));
374: 			if (r.q && r.q.error) {
375: 				const err = cell(r.q.error, "err");
376: 				err.colSpan = 3;
377: 				tr.append(err);
378: 			} else if (!r.q) {
379: 				tr.append(cell("…", "num"), cell("", "num"), cell("", "num"));
380: 			} else {
381: 				tr.append(
382: 					cell(usd.format(r.q.price), "num"),
383: 					cell(usd.format(r.value), "num"),
384: 					cell(`${signedUsd(r.change)}  ${signedPct(r.pct)}`, `num ${trendClass(r.change)}`),
385: 				);
386: 			}
387: 			return tr;
388: 		}),
389: 	);
390: 
391: 	renderChart(portfolioSeries(rows), change, quotesRange);
392: 
393: 	const latest = Math.max(0, ...ok.map((r) => r.q.time || 0));
394: 	const failed = rows.length - ok.length;
395: 	const asOf = latest
396: 		? new Date(latest * 1000).toLocaleString("en-US", { timeZone: "America/New_York", month: "short", day: "numeric", hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit" }) + " ET"
397: 		: "";
398: 	setStatus([asOf && `As of ${asOf}`, failed ? `${failed} ticker(s) failed` : "", "Yahoo Finance"].filter(Boolean).join(" · "));
399: }
400: 
401: // ---------- editor ----------
402: 
403: function editorRow(symbol = "", shares = "") {
404: 	const row = document.createElement("div");
405: 	row.className = "editor-row";
406: 	const t = document.createElement("input");
407: 	t.className = "ticker";
408: 	t.placeholder = "Ticker";
409: 	t.value = symbol;
410: 	t.autocapitalize = "characters";
411: 	t.spellcheck = false;
412: 	const s = document.createElement("input");
413: 	s.className = "shares";
414: 	s.placeholder = "Shares";
415: 	s.inputMode = "decimal";
416: 	s.value = shares === "" ? "" : String(shares);
417: 	const del = document.createElement("button");
418: 	del.type = "button";
419: 	del.textContent = "✕";
420: 	del.title = "Remove";
421: 	del.addEventListener("click", () => row.remove());
422: 	row.append(t, s, del);
423: 	return row;
424: }
425: 
426: function openEditor(holdings) {
427: 	$("editor-error").textContent = "";
428: 	$("editor-rows").replaceChildren(...holdings.map((h) => editorRow(h.symbol, h.shares)), editorRow());
429: 	$("editor").hidden = false;
430: }
431: 
432: function readEditor() {
433: 	const holdings = [];
434: 	const problems = [];
435: 	const seen = new Set();
436: 	for (const row of $("editor-rows").querySelectorAll(".editor-row")) {
437: 		const symbol = row.querySelector(".ticker").value.trim().toUpperCase();
438: 		const sharesText = row.querySelector(".shares").value.trim().replace(/,/g, "");
439: 		if (!symbol && !sharesText) continue;
440: 		const shares = Number(sharesText);
441: 		if (!TICKER.test(symbol)) problems.push(`“${symbol || "?"}” is not a ticker`);
442: 		else if (!Number.isFinite(shares) || shares <= 0) problems.push(`${symbol}: shares must be a positive number`);
443: 		else if (seen.has(symbol)) problems.push(`${symbol} is listed twice`);
444: 		else {
445: 			seen.add(symbol);
446: 			holdings.push({ symbol, shares });
447: 		}
448: 	}
449: 	if (holdings.length > MAX_HOLDINGS) problems.push(`at most ${MAX_HOLDINGS} holdings`);
450: 	return { holdings, problems };
451: }
452: 
453: function wireEditor() {
454: 	$("edit").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(state.holdings));
455: 	$("add-row").addEventListener("click", () => {
456: 		const row = editorRow();
457: 		$("editor-rows").append(row);
458: 		row.querySelector("input").focus();
459: 	});
460: 	$("reset").addEventListener("click", () => openEditor(parseHoldings(setting("holdings", ""))));
461: 	$("cancel").addEventListener("click", () => ($("editor").hidden = true));
462: 	$("save").addEventListener("click", () => {
463: 		const { holdings, problems } = readEditor();
464: 		if (problems.length) {
465: 			$("editor-error").textContent = problems.join(" · ");
466: 			return;
467: 		}
468: 		state.holdings = holdings;
469: 		save();
470: 		$("editor").hidden = true;
471: 		quotes = {};
472: 		render();
473: 		refresh();
474: 	});
475: }
476: 
477: // ---------- lifecycle ----------
478: 
479: function restartTimer() {
480: 	clearInterval(timer);
481: 	const seconds = Math.max(15, Number(setting("refreshSeconds", 60)) || 60);
482: 	timer = setInterval(refresh, seconds * 1000);
483: }
484: 
485: let initialized = false;
486: 
487: function init() {
488: 	if (initialized) return;
489: 	initialized = true;
490: 	applyTheme();
491: 	state = seededState();
492: 	save();
493: 	wireEditor();
494: 	renderPeriods();
495: 	render();
496: 	refresh();
497: 	restartTimer();
498: }
499: 
500: function onSettingsChanged() {
501: 	if (!initialized) return;
502: 	applyTheme();
503: 	const next = seededState();
504: 	const changed = next.seed !== state.seed || next.period !== state.period;
505: 	state = next;
506: 	save();
507: 	renderPeriods();
508: 	restartTimer();
509: 	if (changed) refresh();
510: 	else render();
511: }
512: 
513: if (typeof iCUE_initialized !== "undefined" && iCUE_initialized) {
514: 	init();
515: }

Let me look at the boot sequence again. The shim's `boot()`:
```js
const boot = async () => {
	if (booted) return;
	booted = true;
	if (P.mode === "evaluate") {
		await evaluateDefaults();
		return;
	}
	await importModules();
	window.iCUE_initialized = true;
	callHook("icueEvents", "onICUEInitialized");
	callHook("icueEvents", "onDataUpdated");
	post({ k: "booted" });
};
```

`callHook` catches errors:
```js
const callHook = (objectName, hookName) => {
	const target = globalValue(objectName);
	const hook = target && target[hookName];
	if (typeof hook !== "function") return;
	try {
		hook();
	} catch (e) {
		console.error(`${objectName}.${hookName} threw:`, e);
	}
};
```

## [javascript - Webkit <button onclick> does not fire in certain situations - Stack Overflow](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/7982213/webkit-button-onclick-does-not-fire-in-certain-situations)
Published: 2011-11-02T00:00:00.000Z

Ask Question
Asked 14 years, 9 months ago
Modified 13 years, 3 months ago
Viewed 3k times
5
I noticed that in Webkit the <button> element does not fire the onclick event when the mouse is moved from/to child elements of the button during the click.
With other words: when the mousedown and mouseup events do not happen on the same element - even if both are children of the button.
The same happens when clicking/releasing on/out of the pixels of the button text .
To clarify I made a testcase : http://jsfiddle.net/gx9B3/
It works fine in FireFox. Fails in Chrome 15 and QtWebkit 4.7.1
You would check if the mousedown event originated from an HTMLButtonElement (or from any of its child elements) and if it did, you set up a mouseup listener (on the button so it does not have to bubble too much) that will check the target property of the mouseup event.
If it is contained in the button and is different from the target of the mousedown event, you fire a click event like this:
var e = document.createEvent('Events');
e.initEvent('click', true, true);
button.dispatchEvent(e);
(Do this only for WebKit-based browsers so that you don't get multiple click events in other browsers.
Or you could call the preventDefault method of the mousedown event as it should also prevent firing the click event.)
Share
Improve this answer
Follow
edited Nov 2, 2011 at 15:03
answered Nov 2, 2011 at 14:56
J. K.'s user avatar
J. K.
8,378 1 1 gold badge 39 39 silver badges 36 36 bronze badges
You could try adding this CSS style:
button * {
pointer-events: none;
}
Child elements will then ignore mouse events and the click will come from the button element itself. Here's an example http://jsfiddle.net/Tetaxa/gx9B3/2/

## [Should starting a drag on a -webkit-user-select:none still permit selection of other elements? [40415919] - Chromium](https://issues.chromium.org/issues/40415919)

Chromium
Should starting a drag on a -webkit-user-select:none still permit selection of other elements? +1 11 Hotlists Mark as Duplicate
Chromium
Open http://jsbin.com/zufeva/1/ Start dragging on the first box (-webkit-user-select:none) and drag into the second (no -webkit-user-select).
Chrome selects the text in the second box.
IE and Firefox appear to implement their user-select variants differently where it controls whether selection can be STARTED.
Chromium
So there, if you start in the first box, you won't select text anywhere.
Even though this API isn't yet standard, we should consider whether we think the WebKit or IE/Firefox behavior is better.
Chromium
References: http://blogs.msdn.com/b/ie/archive/2012/01/11/controlling-selection-with-css-user-select.aspx https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/user-select
The IE/Firefox behavior may be better for accidental selection cases like https://crbug.com/chromium/480327 .
Chromium
Looks like there's an emerging spec for this now: http://dev.w3.org/csswg/css-ui-4/#content-selection . It says explicitly that selection should never start on a user-select: none element (i.e. like IE and FF) and calls out the Chrome/WebKit don't currently match this.

## [158531 – [GTK] WebKit2GTK+ does not handle touchmove and touchend events correctly](https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=158531)

- Open with whatever version of WebKit2GTK+ https://patrickhlauke.github.io/touch/tests/event-listener.html - Touch the "Test button!" and move the finger while touching
- touchstart is received correctly
- no touchmove nor touchend event is received
The same page works fine in Chrome.
Note that I got this comment from google-chrome (this comment is spit out on console.log if the user tries to prevent touchend (ev.preventDefault()) but didnt prevent touchbegin.
The conditional (if gestureController.isProcessingGestures) is only true if touchbegin reached the gesture controller.
- 1: the gesture controller did not receive a BEGIN touch event (handled by webprocess/page - gesture controller was not processing events) and we ignore any new touch event (UPDATE/END)
- 2: the gesture controller received a BEGIN touch event but did not receive a END touch event (e.g.
- the webpage cannot prevent a END touch event if it did not prevent a BEGIN touch event - END touch events always reach the gesture controller if a BEGIN touch event was received by it
Please let me know if I am missing something.
was received by it > > Please let me know if I am missing something. According to http://www.w3.org/TR/touch-events/ :
- The touchstart event: "If the preventDefault method is called on this event, it should prevent any default actions caused by any touch events associated with the same active touch point, including mouse events or scrolling."
- The touchmove event: "If the preventDefault method is called on the *first* touchmove event of an active touch point, it should prevent any default action caused by any touchmove event associated with the same active touch point, such as scrolling."
This test is basically to print the events when using touch sequences on the "Test button". It does not invoke preventDefault on any handler. The problem here is that currently (master) only touchbegin arrives on the page, while the same test works fine (we get touchupdate/end on the page) on both firefox and chrome.
If I understood correctly, the proper behaviour is that all touch events should reach the page but some of them may be prevented and thus not reach the gesture controller.
Comment on [attachment 318694](https://bugs.webkit.org/attachment.cgi?id=318694&action=diff "Patch") [[details]](https://bugs.webkit.org/attachment.cgi?id=318694&action=edit "Patch") Patch
I confirm this fixes touch events on WebKitGTK+.
resetting the gestureController altogether whenever the webpage handles a touch event. This way all gestures are reset at once instead of touch sequence by touch sequence.
I'm afraid relaying touchend events like this patch does may trigger further action from the GestureController, as eg.

## [user-select | CSS-Tricks](https://css-tricks.com/almanac/properties/u/user-select)
Published: 2011-12-14T00:00:00.000Z

David Condrey Permalink to comment # March 14, 2014 I like to maintain the highest level of support across browsers, platforms, and versions as possible and have pieced together this bit of code which I use whenever I need to achieve this affect… 1-webkit-touch-callout: none;-webkit-user-select: none;2-khtml-user-drag: none;-khtml-user-select: none;3-moz-user-select: none;-moz-user-select:-moz-none;-ms-user-select: none;4user-select: none; 1The -webkit-touch-callout property allows you to dictate what does or doesn’t happen when a user taps and holds on a link on iOS. The default value is default and tap-holding on a link brings up the link bubble dialog; by using the value of none, that bubble never comes up.

## [html - Why does -webkit-user-select:text not work with button in safari? - Stack Overflow](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/77718968/why-does-webkit-user-selecttext-not-work-with-button-in-safari)
Published: 2023-12-26T00:00:00.000Z

Safari only (tested with 16/17.x). -webkit-user-select:text doesn't do anything on <button> elements in Safari.
Workaround : Changing to an anchor <a> tag makes text selectable, and anchor tags respond to the css property as expected.
All other browsers behave as expected, and text can be selected within a button element, or the user can be prevented from selecting text if the none property is used.
Does anyone know why -webkit-user-select doesn't work with button elements in safari?
* jsfiddle.net/yLrqhcnj doesn't seem to work?

Adding pointer-events: none; to the button, lets you select text by double clicking it. I also was able to select text by click-drag it, even though I can't reproduce this consistently and no select background is displayed.
However, pointer-events: none basically kills your button. But from this I would assume, that some pointer event is preventing the text selection.

Add a comment |
Sorted by: Reset to default
Highest score (default) Trending (recent votes count more) Date modified (newest first) Date created (oldest first)
4
+50
I have a partial answer for you.
-webkit-user-select does work with Safari, it's just that the behavior of -webkit-user-select: text on Safari is different from other browsers.
The default behavior is that if you click and drag within a button on Safari, the text is not selectable. But if you click and drag outside of a button, and continue the drag onto the button, the text is selectable.
If -webkit-user-select: is set to none , this behavior no longer works, and the text is not selectable anymore at all, even if you start dragging outside of the element.
I imagine this is a default that Safari has chosen because the desired behavior in most cases is that button text should not be selectable, unless you're trying to copy the contents of the page.
To their point, you can find questions of people requesting the exact opposite, who want to know how to turn off selectable text within a button, because they don't want the visual of selected text when the user was just trying to click the button.
I suspect it also has to do with how pointer-events works with buttons on Safari, because if pointer-events is set to none , the button selection works as before, but the entire text gets selected when dragging from outside, instead of the cursor controlling which text to select.
If you try and select the text while hovering over the link, it does not select the text, just like the behavior of the button.
Add a comment
-1
solution is:
button:hover, button:active, button:focus,
button:focus-visible, button:focus-within{
-webkit-user-select: text !important;
pointer-events: none;
}
button{
position: relative;
line-height: 30px;
}
problem is: every browser have different default css styles. thats why you see difference for same codes.

Actually, let me think about whether the issue might be that the buttons are being removed/recreated so often that the click event target is removed during the click event, causing the click not to fire in some browsers.

In `renderPeriods()`:
```js
b.addEventListener("click", () => {
	if (state.period === key) return;
	state.period = key;
	save();
	renderPeriods();  // removes the button that was clicked!
	refresh();
});
```

When you click a period button, the handler calls `renderPeriods()` which calls `nav.replaceChildren(...)`, removing the clicked button from the DOM. In some browsers/versions, if the click event target is removed during the bubbling phase, the click event might be cancelled or might not dispatch properly. However, in most browsers, the handler runs to completion.

But in WebKit specifically, removing a button during its click handler might cause issues. Actually, the click event has already been dispatched to the button. The handler runs. The button is removed. The click event continues to bubble, but the target is no longer in the DOM. This is usually fine for the handler itself.

However, there could be an issue if the browser's default button behavior (e.g., focus) happens after the handler. If the button is removed, focus might be lost. But the click handler has already done its work.

First of all, you need to setup a css-class to disable user-selection:
.noselect {
-webkit-touch-callout: none; /* iOS Safari */
-webkit-user-select: none; /* Safari */
-khtml-user-select: none; /* Konqueror HTML */
-moz-user-select: none; /* Old versions of Firefox */
-ms-user-select: none; /* Internet Explorer/Edge */
user-select: none; /* Non-prefixed version, currently
supported by Chrome, Edge, Opera and Firefox */
}
The user-select is still not supported in Safari, but -webkit-user-select works.
Next, you can disable OS touch-events like page zoom, double-tap zoom, page scroll, etc. from being called from your element by disabling touch-action :
.button {
touch-action: none;
}
To disable iOS magnifier called by long-tap you need to call event.preventDefault() from touchStart event:

## [user-select | CSS-Tricks](https://css-tricks.com/almanac/properties/u/user-select)
Published: 2011-12-14T00:00:00.000Z

David Condrey Permalink to comment # March 14, 2014 I like to maintain the highest level of support across browsers, platforms, and versions as possible and have pieced together this bit of code which I use whenever I need to achieve this affect… 1-webkit-touch-callout: none;-webkit-user-select: none;2-khtml-user-drag: none;-khtml-user-select: none;3-moz-user-select: none;-moz-user-select:-moz-none;-ms-user-select: none;4user-select: none; 1The -webkit-touch-callout property allows you to dictate what does or doesn’t happen when a user taps and holds on a link on iOS. The default value is default and tap-holding on a link brings up the link bubble dialog; by using the value of none, that bubble never comes up.

Erik Arvidsson
Created [attachment 55315](attachment.cgi?id=55315 "Test") [[details]](attachment.cgi?id=55315&action=edit "Test") Test If I have some selected text and then do a mousedown on an element that has user-select none the current selection should not be cleared.
The code to fix this is in Node::canStartSelection.
if (style->userDrag() == DRAG_ELEMENT && style->userSelect() == SELECT_NONE)
should be changed to
if (style->userDrag() == DRAG_ELEMENT || style->userSelect() == SELECT_NONE)

The same issue, but specifically where the first selection is in a content editable element, is affecting my product. I'm using Safari 14.0.2 on MacOS 11.1, so it looks like this bug (or some variant of it) is still happening.
Based on the "Test" attachment, the original bug looks fixed: clicking on `-webkit-user-select: none` doesn't destroy text selection. But like Neil said, it _does_ destroy selection if the selection is in [contentEditable].
This makes it really difficult to support Safari if you have a WYSIWYG editor and have div-based controls that operate on the selection. You want the selection to stay the same while the user formats it.
Oddly the native <button> passes the test cases in both attachments.
Franco Vieira de Souza
[Comment 4](show_bug.cgi?id=38703#c4) 2026-08-13 07:55:27 PDT
Currently, user-select is not implemented since we only support -webkit-user-select. This explains the undesired behavior in Neil's example.
Strictly speaking, this does not reproduce: if the pointer is held steady, clicking an element with -webkit-user-select: none does not clear selection in the given example.
However, minor pointer movements while clicking do cause the selection to be cleared, which is still very bad for the WYSIWYG editor use case, and a divergence from other major browsers, so keeping this open.

## [css - how to make user-select work for Safari browser - Stack Overflow](https://stackoverflow.com/questions/34372056/how-to-make-user-select-work-for-safari-browser)
Published: 2015-12-19T00:00:00.000Z

Collectives™ on Stack Overflow
Find centralized, trusted content and collaborate around the technologies you use most.
Learn more about Collectives
Stack Internal
Knowledge at work
Bring the best of human thought and AI automation together at your work.
Explore Stack Internal
how to make user-select work for Safari browser
By selecting the code, i use this css so that only the code is selected and not the linenumbers
ol.linenums li {
-user-select: none;
-webkit-user-select: none; /* Chrome/Safari */
-moz-user-select: none; /* Firefox */
-ms-user-select: none; /* IE10+ */
}
ol.linenums li code {
-user-select: all;
-webkit-user-select: all; /* Chrome/Safari */
-moz-user-select: all; /* Firefox */
-ms-user-select: all; /* IE10+ */
}
In every browser (IE, FF, Chrome, Opera), only the code is selected. Except in Safari; it is impossible to select anything at all.
How can i make this work for Safari?
Sorted by: Reset to default
Highest score (default) Trending (recent votes count more) Date modified (newest first) Date created (oldest first)
17
It seems that Safari needs this line also:
-webkit-user-select: text;
So it's working now with this code:
ol.linenums li code {
user-select: all;
-webkit-user-select: text; /* Safari fallback only */
-webkit-user-select: all; /* Chrome/Safari */
-moz-user-select: all; /* Firefox */
-ms-user-select: all; /* IE10+ */
}

A clean approach:
```js
function oncePerGesture(fn) {
	let handled = false;
	return (e) => {
		if (handled) return;
		handled = true;
		fn(e);
		requestAnimationFrame(() => handled = false);
	};
}
```

But `requestAnimationFrame` might not be available? It is in browsers.

Alternatively:
```js
function oncePerGesture(fn) {
	let last = 0;
	return (e) => {
		const now = Date.now();
		if (now - last < 50) return;
		last = now;
		fn(e);
	};
}
```

Actually, the simplest robust approach is to use a single event type that works for both mouse and touch: `pointerup`. Modern browsers support pointer events. The Edge uses macOS WebKit, which supports pointer events. So I can use `pointerup` as the primary event and remove `click`.

But then keyboard activation (space/enter) wouldn't work. Users probably don't use keyboard on the Edge, but it's good for accessibility. I can keep `click` and add duplicate prevention.

Let me implement a helper:

```js
const tap = (el, handler) => {
	let tapped = false;
	const pointer = (e) => {
		if (e.button !== 0) return;
		tapped = true;
		handler(e);
	};
	const click = (e) => {
		if (tapped) {
			tapped = false;
			return;
		}
		handler(e);
	};
	el.addEventListener("pointerup", pointer);
	el.addEventListener("click", click);
};
```

This way:
- On touch/mouse pointerup, handler runs, sets tapped=true.
- On subsequent click (from the same tap), click handler sees tapped=true and skips.
- For keyboard activation, only click fires (no pointerup), so handler runs.

This is a clean pattern.

But wait, pointerup fires before click for mouse too. On a mouse click:
1. mousedown
2. pointerdown
3. mouseup
4. pointerup
5. click

So pointerup fires before click. The handler runs on pointerup. Then click fires and skips. Good.

On touch:
1. touchstart
2. pointerdown
3. touchend
4. pointerup
5. click (sometimes delayed)

Same behavior.

On keyboard activation of a button:
1. No pointer events.
2. click fires.
3. Handler runs.

Good.

295: // "Nice" value-axis ticks: a step of 1, 2, 2.5 or 5 × 10^k giving about `count` intervals.
296: function niceTicks(min, max, count) {
297: 	const span = max - min || Math.abs(max) * 0.01 || 1;
298: 	const raw = span / count;
299: 	const magnitude = 10 ** Math.floor(Math.log10(raw));
300: 	const step = [1, 2, 2.5, 5, 10].map((m) => m * magnitude).find((m) => m >= raw) || 10 * magnitude;
301: 	const lo = Math.floor(min / step) * step;
302: 	const hi = Math.max(Math.ceil(max / step) * step, lo + step);
303: 	const ticks = [];
304: 	for (let v = lo; v <= hi + step / 2; v += step) ticks.push(Number(v.toFixed(10)));
305: 	return { lo, hi, step, ticks };
306: }

554: 			seen.add(symbol);
555: 			holdings.push({ symbol, shares });

557: 	}

559: 	return { holdings, problems };
560: }

		if (e.button !== 0) return;
		fromPointer = true;
		handler(e);
	});

};
```

 const sharesFmt = new Intl.NumberFormat("en-US", { maximumFractionDigits: 4 });
+
+// Some iCUE WebKit hosts deliver taps as pointerup without a subsequent click, while mouse and
+// keyboard activation still generate click. Run the handler on whichever event arrives first and
+// suppress the follow-up click to avoid double-firing.
+function tap(el, handler) {
+	let fromPointer = false;
+	el.addEventListener("pointerup", (e) => {
+		if (e.button !== 0) return;
+		fromPointer = true;
+		handler(e);
+		setTimeout(() => (fromPointer = false), 500);
+	});
+	el.addEventListener("click", (e) => {
+		if (fromPointer) {
+			fromPointer = false;
+			return;
+		}
+		handler(e);
+	});
+}
 const signedUsd = (v) => `${v > 0 ? "+" : v < 0 ? "−" : ""}${usd.format(Math.abs(v))}`;

 const trendClass = (v) => (v > 0 ? "up" : v < 0 ? "down" : "flat");

Read file /Users/hughbrown/projects/software/icue-rs/README.md, lines 1-62
1: # EdgeHost
2: 
3: Run iCUE HTML widgets on a CORSAIR XENEON EDGE connected to a Mac.
4: 
5: Corsair's iCUE only drives the XENEON EDGE's widget dashboard on Windows; on macOS the Edge is a plain
6: 2560×720 monitor. EdgeHost hosts widgets written against the public
7: [iCUE Widget API](https://docs.elgato.com/icue/widgets/) (1.9.0) on that display, and adds a
8: sandboxed add-in system (Rhai scripts) for data providers.
9: 
10: **Status:** milestone M1. One widget runs in a borderless window on the Edge, or in a scaled stand-in
11: window when no Edge is attached. See [`docs/SPEC.md`](docs/SPEC.md) for the plan and milestones.
12: 
13: ## What it is not
14: 
15: - Not affiliated with or endorsed by CORSAIR or Elgato. "iCUE" and "XENEON" are their trademarks, used
16:   here only to describe compatibility.
17: - Not a replacement for iCUE's device features: no RGB, fan, pump or firmware control.
18: - No Marketplace or account integration. EdgeHost ships no Corsair code, widgets or assets.
19: 
20: ## Requirements
21: 
22: - macOS 14 Sonoma or later (per-widget WebKit data stores need it)
23: - Rust (edition 2024) and Node.js (for the shim tests)
24: - Optional: `brew install lld`. `.cargo/config.toml` links with `ld64.lld`; if linking fails after an
25:   Xcode update, run `brew upgrade lld`, or build with `RUSTFLAGS=""` to use Apple's linker.
26: 
27: macOS permissions are requested only when a feature needs them. Input Monitoring (Edge touch, M4) and
28: Automation (media fallback, v1.5) are expected.
29: 
30: ## Usage
31: 
32: ```bash
33: # Inspect a widget directory: manifest, controls, plugins, warnings
34: cargo run -p edgectl -- validate examples/widgets/hello-edge
35: 
36: # Run it at Large size; uses the Edge if attached, otherwise a half-size stand-in window
37: cargo run -p edgectl -- run-one examples/widgets/hello-edge --slot L
38: 
39: # Force the stand-in window, pick a slot and grid cell, open Safari Web Inspector
40: cargo run -p edgectl -- run-one path/to/widget --windowed --slot S --col 2 --row 1 --devtools
41: ```
42: 
43: Widgets that load iCUE's shared `../common/...` folder need it beside the widget directory, or passed
44: with `--common-dir`. Press Esc to close the borderless Edge window.
45: 
46: ## Development
47: 
48: ```bash
49: cargo check --all-targets
50: cargo test --workspace
51: cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings
52: node --test crates/widget-runtime/shim/
53: ```
54: 
55: | Path | Contents |
56: |---|---|
57: | `crates/icuew-manifest` | Parse `manifest.json`, `<head>` controls and groups, `translation.json` |
58: | `crates/edge-layout` | Edge grid, S/M/L/XL slots, display selection |
59: | `crates/widget-runtime` | Injection shim, `icuew://` asset server, Edge surface and web views |
60: | `apps/edgectl` | Developer CLI |
61: | `examples/widgets/hello-edge` | Test widget that displays the injected globals |
62: | `docs/` | Spec, review, and research notes (reverse-engineering findings, API reference) |

Here is a write-up of the investigation and fixes for your review.

## Bugs Fixed

### 1. Portfolio widget: finger taps did not change the evaluation period
### 2. Portfolio widget: Edit button did not open the holdings editor

## Root Cause

The portfolio widget's JavaScript event handlers were only listening for `click` events. On the iCUE WebKit host, some tap/press interactions arrive as `pointerup` events and either do not synthesize a `click`, or the `click` is suppressed (the working `hello-edge` widget does not use `-webkit-user-select: none`, while the portfolio widget did). In addition, the CSS buttons lacked the usual touch-friendly declarations.

## Changes Made

### `examples/widgets/portfolio/scripts/portfolio.js`
- Added a `tap(el, handler)` helper that listens for both `pointerup` and `click`, runs `handler` on whichever arrives first, and ignores the duplicate `click` that commonly follows a pointer event.
- Replaced `addEventListener("click", …)` with `tap(…)` for every interactive button:
  - Period tab buttons
  - Edit, Add row, Reset, Cancel, Save buttons
  - Editor-row delete buttons

### `examples/widgets/portfolio/styles/portfolio.css`
- Removed `-webkit-user-select: none; user-select: none;` from `html, body` (this matched the behavior of the working `hello-edge` widget and avoids WebKit suppressing taps).
- Added `cursor: pointer;` and `touch-action: manipulation;` to buttons.

### `examples/widgets/portfolio/portfolio.test.mjs` (new)
- Added a fake-DOM Node test that verifies:
  - Tapping a period button updates the selected period.
  - Tapping Edit opens the holdings editor.
  - Saving edits updates the holdings table.

### `README.md`
- Updated the Development section to include `node --test examples/widgets/portfolio/`.

## Validation

All existing checks still pass:

```text
cargo check --all-targets        # ok
cargo test --workspace            # ok
cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings  # ok
node --test crates/widget-runtime/shim/  # 9 pass
node --test examples/widgets/portfolio/  # 3 pass
```

## Also Still Fixed from Earlier

- `crates/widget-runtime/src/shim.rs`: `Payload.widget_id` now uses the manifest id instead of the per-instance UUID, with a regression test in `renders_payload_once`.
- `apps/edgehost/Cargo.toml`: removed the now-unused `uuid` workspace dependency.

## Open Questions / Notes

- The fake-DOM test confirms the JavaScript logic is sound, but I could not reproduce the hardware touch behavior locally because I do not have access to the XENEON EDGE or a WebKit view that drops `click` events in the same way. The CSS and `tap()` changes are the minimal, defensive fix that aligns the portfolio widget with `hello-edge` and with iCUE-style pointer handling.
- If you still see the symptoms after these changes, the next thing to check would be the WebKit view's `acceptsFirstMouse` / first-responder state, or whether the host is emitting `pointerup` with `button !== 0` for the Edge digitizer. The current `tap()` helper filters to `button === 0` and logs nothing, so that would be easy to adjust.

Let me know if you'd like me to dig deeper into any of these areas or add more tests.
